<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001076 -->
# A genuinely non-core binary staircase on the arithmetic-rank body

Project: gd-exact-profile-20260926. Worker: max. Allocated round: 64.
Originating guidance: 465affb3ea6cfb83. Frozen plan: b4edb4877ec644b4.

**Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED mathematical draft. No fact_id.**
The assignment forbids a formal gate. All new geometry, trajectory,
inequalities and limits below belong to this one joint proof attempt.
None is being represented as an accepted predecessor. The original
all-word, all-horizon exact minimax Goal remains OPEN.

The rank body is unchanged:

    F_d(A)=|A|(d-|A|)/d,
    K_d={g in R^d: sum_i g_i=0,
                  sum_(i in A) g_i<=F_d(A) for every A subset[d]}.

We freeze one initial profile in dimension d=2^m. Its actual trajectory
never enters K_d, including at the first update and at the terminal point.
It has m-1 distinct ordered-block transitions, and its gradients span an
m-dimensional space. Each transition is proved at its original physical
bridge. The terminal value nevertheless loses to the same-word scalar
Huber witness by a nonvanishing true-radius factor.

The exact limit of its normalized payoff V_(m,tau) is

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1) V_(m,tau)
      = Z_1 exp(-tau) / [2(1+eta)],
    eta = 1/[2(4lambda^2-1)],  lambda=1+sqrt(2).       (A)

Convergence is uniform for tau in [1/5,1/4]. In that whole interval the
limit is strictly below 2976/2989<249/250, and eventually the actual
finite payoff satisfies B_(2^m-1)V_(m,tau)<249/250 uniformly in tau.
At tau=1/4 the limit is below 47616/50225<19/20, and the finite payoff
is eventually below 19/20. These are upper bounds on this constructed
family's payoff, not on the unrestricted worst-case functional W_n.

## 1. Accepted inputs, retrieval, and exact scope

Accepted fact **1252b8823bb5a290** supplies the original normalization

    W_n(h)=sup 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2),
    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)grad f(x_t),

over the complete finite-dimensional convex differentiable L-smooth
class with an attained minimizer. We retain every physical step and
every ordered interpolation row. In our constructed instance the
denominator is the squared distance to the ENTIRE minimum set.

Accepted fact **04219554e41d9750** supplies the original benchmark
recurrences and the positive finite ray limit:

    Omega(a,b)=(a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    G(a,b)=2a+b/2+sqrt(b^2+8ab)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,
    B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1 in (0,infinity).         (1.1)

The maxima include ALL integer splits. No seed hypothesis from that
fact is assumed. For the numerical constant in the deficit we prove a
new elementary scalar bound B_n<=(62/25)(n+1)^rho in Section 9,
where rho=log_2(lambda). It avoids using an unverified transcription
of saved interval endpoints and avoids evaluating the recurrence.

The complete earlier records d54d97705d464425, 63d6dc5d659fd7a4 and
a488f62fb6c05ea8 were read. They are awareness only. Sections 2--4
below rederive the body, smooth envelope and exact sorting law; the
earlier unverified projection law is not an accepted premise. The old
paired start and all first-update-core starts are excluded from the
present construction. We do not retune that paired family.

Primary orientation was Francis Bach, *Learning with Submodular
Functions: A Convex Optimization Perspective*, arXiv:1111.6453v2
(2013), https://arxiv.org/html/1111.6453v2 . The exact downloaded local
text at workers/max/downloads/bach_1111.6453v2.txt was available;
Proposition 3.2 and its greedy-support proof, and Appendix A.3's
ordered quadratic projection proof, were read. The relevant concepts
are the base polyhedron of a normalized submodular function and
decreasing Euclidean isotonic regression. Our rank function is NOT
nondecreasing, so positive-polymatroid assertions are not substituted.
All mathematical ingredients actually used are proved below.

Matlas returned 2106.13683 Proposition 4.1 as related orientation on
the proximal map of pairwise absolute differences. Attempts to open
its arXiv HTML v2 and v1 returned "Cache miss". No statement from that
paper is used. A separate Matlas query for the scalar power envelope
returned unrelated covariance/polynomial lemmas; none is imported.
This is a continuation of the same rank-body program, with the exact
local primary source read, not a new literature branch rejected
without examination.

## 2. The unchanged Euclidean body and its support

Let e be the all-ones vector, E_d={x:<e,x>=0}, and

    a_i=(d+1-2i)/d,             1<=i<=d.

If the two disjoint differences of sets A and B have sizes p and q,
expanding the cardinality quadratic gives

    F_d(A)+F_d(B)-F_d(A union B)-F_d(A intersection B)=2pq/d>=0.

Also F_d(empty)=F_d([d])=0 and

    sum_(i=1)^k a_i=k(d-k)/d.                         (2.1)

Every permutation of a is in K_d because its largest k-coordinate
sum is (2.1). Singleton and complementary-singleton constraints give
|g_i|<=(d-1)/d for g in K_d. Thus K_d is nonempty and compact.

If w_(pi(1))>=...>=w_(pi(d)), summation by parts gives

    <w,g>
      =sum_(k<d)(w_(pi(k))-w_(pi(k+1)))
                           sum_(i<=k)g_(pi(i))
      <=sum_(k<d)(w_(pi(k))-w_(pi(k+1))) k(d-k)/d
      =sum_i a_i w_(pi(i)).                          (2.2)

The permutation g_(pi(i))=a_i attains this bound. It follows that

    K_d=conv{permutations of a},
    sigma_d(w):=max_(g in K_d)<w,g>
      =sum_i a_i w_(pi(i))=(1/d)sum_(i<j)|w_i-w_j|.   (2.3)

For the convex-hull equality, if a point g of K_d were outside the
hull, let v be its closest hull point. Differentiating squared
distance along segments gives <g-v,t-v><=0 for all hull points t.
The direction g-v would then strictly separate g from that hull,
contradicting (2.2) and its attainment. The last equality in (2.3)
counts the d+1-2i occurrences of the i-th sorted coordinate.

The body is centrally symmetric since -a is a permutation of a.
It contains a relative neighborhood of zero in E_d: for centered g
and a set of size k,

    |sum_(i in A)g_i|<=||g|| sqrt(k(d-k)/d).

Since k(d-k)>=d-1 for 1<=k<d, the ball in E_d of radius
sqrt((d-1)/d) satisfies every constraint. A strictly smaller radius
satisfies every proper constraint strictly. All norms here and below
are the original coordinate Euclidean norms.

## 3. One static smooth function and its whole minimum set

Define the single static function

    Phi_d(x)=max_(g in K_d){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.          (3.1)

Let P_d(x) be the unique closest point of x in K_d. Completing the
square identifies the maximizer in (3.1) as P_d(x). The closest-point
condition, proved by expanding squared distances along segments, is

    <x-P_d(x),g-P_d(x)><=0  for every g in K_d.        (3.2)

Adding this condition at x and x' gives

    <x-x',P_d(x)-P_d(x')>>=||P_d(x)-P_d(x')||^2.

Consequently P_d is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating each maximum in (3.1)
at the other maximizer shows

    0<=Phi_d(x')-Phi_d(x)-<P_d(x),x'-x>
       <=<P_d(x')-P_d(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

This proves differentiability and

    grad Phi_d(x)=P_d(x),
    Phi_d(x)=(||x||^2-||x-P_d(x)||^2)/2.             (3.3)

Convexity follows from the maximum representation. There is no
dimension factor in smoothness: on a neighborhood of zero in E_d,
P_d(x)=x, so its optimal global Lipschitz constant is exactly 1.

Writing bar(x)=d^(-1)sum_i x_i, the full minimum set is

    argmin Phi_d=R e,  min Phi_d=0,
    dist(x,argmin Phi_d)^2=||x-bar(x)e||^2.            (3.4)

Indeed Phi_d>=0 by g=0 and Phi_d(te)=0 because K_d is centered.
For nonzero z=x-bar(x)e, a sufficiently small epsilon in (0,2)
has epsilon z in K_d. Its value in (3.1) is
(epsilon-epsilon^2/2)||z||^2>0, excluding all other minimizers.
The distance assertion is orthogonal projection onto the line.

For any advertised L>0 use f=L Phi_d. Its actual GD with steps h_t/L
is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t P_d(x_t). The body is not rescaled and h_t is
not changed. Initial normalization below divides by the exact radius
in (3.4); no fictitious unit-distance start is imposed.

## 4. Exact interval-average law, including sorting and all subset rows

For sorted coordinates y_1>=...>=y_d put z_i=y_i-a_i. For a contiguous
interval [p,q], let av_(p,q)(z) be its arithmetic mean. The exact
residual and projection in these coordinates are

    u_i=min_(1<=p<=i) max_(i<=q<=d) av_(p,q)(z),
    g_i=y_i-u_i.                                    (4.1)

Here is a full proof of the needed ordered projection assertion.
Minimize ||u-z||^2/2 on the decreasing cone u_1>=...>=u_d. Existence
and uniqueness follow from coercivity and strict convexity. Define
R_k=sum_(i<=k)(u_i-z_i), R_0=0. Perturbation by a constant vector,
increase of any prefix, and small decrease at a strict jump give

    R_d=0,  R_k>=0,  R_k(u_k-u_(k+1))=0.             (4.2)

Conversely, these conditions imply optimality: for any decreasing v,

    <u-z,v-u>
      =sum_(k<d) R_k[(v_k-v_(k+1))-(u_k-u_(k+1))]>=0.

Expansion of the squared objective now proves the converse.
On a maximal constant block [b,c] of u with level t, (4.2) implies
R_(b-1)=R_c=0, t=av_(b,c)(z), every prefix of that block has average
at most t, and every suffix has average at least t. For i in the
block, choosing p=b in (4.1) makes all its q-averages at most t,
with equality at q=c; later blocks have no larger levels. Conversely
for every p<=i, choosing q=c gives an average at least t, using the
suffix of the first encountered block and any intervening blocks.
This proves the min-max formula (4.1).

The vector g=y-u is centered and obeys

    sum_(i<=k)g_i=k(d-k)/d-R_k<=k(d-k)/d.             (4.3)

It is decreasing. Inside a constant block its differences are those
of y. At a block end c the suffix/prefix inequalities give
z_c>=u_c and z_(c+1)<=u_(c+1), whence
g_c>=a_c>a_(c+1)>=g_(c+1). Thus (4.3) implies every subset bound,
not just prefix bounds. Finally, for v in K_d,

    <u,v-g>=sum_(k<d)(u_k-u_(k+1))sum_(i<=k)(v_i-g_i)<=0:

every strict jump is at a saturated prefix of g by (4.2), and v
obeys the same body's upper bound. This is (3.2), proving that g
is the true Euclidean projection. Undoing the sorting permutation
proves the assertion for unsorted x. Equal coordinates cause no
ambiguity, since uniqueness fixes their equal projected coordinates.

For an arbitrary physical step, if pi_t maps sorted ranks to original
coordinate labels, the actual update is

    v_(t,i)=(1-h_t)y_(t,i)+h_t u_(t,i),
    y_(t+1,k)=v_(t,sigma_t(k)),
    pi_(t+1)(k)=pi_t(sigma_t(k)),                    (4.4)

where sigma_t sorts v_t, breaking ties by original labels. This is
only a state permutation; no physical step is reordered. The frozen
profile below admits a proof that pi_t stays the original lexicographic
order, with successively larger tie blocks. We establish that claim
before using it, rather than assuming that symmetry preserves ranks.

## 5. The one frozen profile and the unchanged physical word

For the principal result fix m>=3 and tau in [1/5,1/4]. Put

    d=2^m, n=2^m-1,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), alpha=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j),
    b_j=1+alpha c^j, S_j=sum H_j,  0<=j<m.           (5.1)

Every child uses these SAME enclosing (m,tau) parameters. The actual
word is H_m=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)). Define its actual prefix masses

    L_t=sum_(i<t)h_i,  0<=t<=n,  M=2S_m+1.           (5.2)

The symbol L_t is a prefix mass; L without subscript remains the
advertised smoothness. Concatenation proves that the first occurrence
of level j is physical step index 2^j-1, the preceding word is H_j,
and therefore

    L_(2^j)=S_j+b_j=:P_j,      0<=j<m.              (5.3)

For example this is the UPDATE COUNT 2^j, not the query just before
the bridge. Subsequent copies of any level retain every one of their
steps. The all-index formula is h_t=1+alpha c^(nu_2(t+1)), where
nu_2 counts factors of 2. This follows because adding 2^j to a
positive integer smaller than 2^j preserves its smaller valuation
in the right child. Every h_t is strictly greater than 1.

Label the d physical coordinates by epsilon=(epsilon_1,...,epsilon_m)
in {+1,-1}^m, with +1 preceding -1 in lexicographic order. This label
has rank

    i(epsilon)=1+sum_(k=1)^m ((1-epsilon_k)/2)2^(m-k).

The arithmetic rank vector in this fixed physical labeling is

    a(epsilon)=sum_(k=1)^m 2^(-k)epsilon_k.           (5.4)

The vectors epsilon_k, viewed as d-coordinate columns, are centered
and orthogonal: <epsilon_k,epsilon_l>=d if k=l and zero otherwise.
This follows by pairing labels that differ in one sign. We do not
replace the physical Euclidean metric by a metric on m coefficients.

For 2<=k<=m set

    T_k=2^(m-k+1),
    q_1(0)=M, q_k(0)=L_(T_k)=P_(m-k+1).

The ONE initial profile frozen in plan b4edb4877ec644b4 is

    x_0(epsilon)=sum_(k=1)^m 2^(-k)q_k(0)epsilon_k
       =(M/2)epsilon_1
         +sum_(k=2)^m 2^(-k)P_(m-k+1)epsilon_k.      (5.5)

There is no adjustable amplitude or numerical scan in (5.5). In
particular this is not the previous paired-rank profile. The extinction
times are T_m=2,T_(m-1)=4,...,T_2=2^(m-1), all specified directly
from the original physical hierarchy. The first mode survives the
whole word; the other modes have successively different lifetimes.

The exact trajectory statements in Sections 6--7 in fact hold for
all m>=2 and 0<tau<m. The interval in the principal result is needed
only for the uniform limiting comparison, not to assert non-core entry.

## 6. Actual ordered blocks and every nonlinear transition

For every query 0<=t<=n define

    q_1(t)=M-L_t,
    q_k(t)=L_(T_k)-L_t  if t<T_k,
    q_k(t)=0           if t>=T_k,           2<=k<=m.  (6.1)

Let r(t) be the number of positive q_k(t). Its formula is

    r(0)=m,
    r(t)=m-floor(log_2 t),                 1<=t<=n.  (6.2)

We claim that the actual iterate, gradient and residual are

    x_t(epsilon)=sum_(k<=r(t))2^(-k)q_k(t)epsilon_k,
    g_t(epsilon)=sum_(k<=r(t))2^(-k)epsilon_k,
    u_t(epsilon)=sum_(k<=r(t))2^(-k)(q_k(t)-1)epsilon_k.
                                                               (6.3)

We first prove the projection assertion for these displayed states,
then verify the physical recurrence. For an active k>=2, the prefix
mass still to be taken includes the last step at its fixed lifetime:

    q_k(t)>=h_(T_k-1)=b_(m-k+1)>1   whenever t<T_k.   (6.4)

Also q_1(t)>=S_m+1>1. All active coefficients are strictly ordered

    q_1(t)>q_2(t)>...>q_(r(t))(t)>1.                 (6.5)

For k>=2 their successive differences are positive differences of
the fixed prefix masses L_(T_k). The first difference is positive
because M>L_(T_2). Hence, for any active k,

    2^(-k)(q_k-1)>sum_(l=k+1)^r 2^(-l)(q_l-1),
    2^(-k)q_k    >sum_(l=k+1)^r 2^(-l)q_l.           (6.6)

For r=k the right side is zero. Otherwise use q_l<q_k and the
strict finite geometric bound sum_(l>k)^r 2^(-l)<2^(-k).

At the first differing active sign, (6.6) proves that the coordinate
with +1 is larger. Thus BOTH x_t and u_t are strictly lexicographic
in the first r signs, and constant on each block of labels with those
signs fixed. There are exactly 2^r consecutive maximal residual
blocks, each of size ell=2^(m-r). Within each block, g_t in (6.3)
is precisely the average of a: the last m-r signs in (5.4) average
to zero. The same is true of the average relation

    u_t|_[b,c]=av_(b,c)(x_t-a).

For a prefix of length s inside such a block, whose size is ell,
the residual R in (4.2) has the within-block value

    sum_(i=b)^(b+s-1)(a_i-av_(b,c)(a))=s(ell-s)/d.   (6.7)

This follows by summing the arithmetic sequence with gap 2/d.
It is nonnegative and returns to zero at s=ell. At every boundary
u_t jumps strictly by (6.6). Consequently (4.2) holds on the whole
ordered coordinate list. Section 4 now proves that the displayed
g_t is exactly P_d(x_t), satisfying every original subset constraint
and the full normal-cone condition. This is also an explicit solution
of every min-max in (4.1) at these queries.

The starting state is (5.5). Suppose (6.3) is the actual state at t<n.
The original step x_t-h_t g_t subtracts h_t from every active q_k.
For k>=2 still active after the step this yields
L_(T_k)-L_(t+1), exactly (6.1). If t=T_k-1, it yields
h_(T_k-1)-h_t=0 exactly. Already inactive modes stay zero. The first
mode becomes M-L_(t+1). These are precisely the next coefficients
in (6.1), whose true projection has just been proved. Induction proves
(6.3) for EVERY physical query and every physical update.

In particular, at the first occurrence of every level 1<=j<m,

    t=2^j-1: k=m-j+1 is active and q_k(t)=b_j,
    step h_t=b_j: q_k(t+1)=0,
    t+1=2^j: r drops from m-j+1 to m-j.               (6.8)

Each pair of adjacent old residual blocks differing in this last
active sign becomes one larger block. Its rank-gradient component
2^(-k)epsilon_k disappears. The other modes remain positive and
correctly ordered. There are m-1 such events at the original physical
update counts 2,4,...,2^(m-1), INCLUDING the largest root bridge.
After that bridge, the entire final H_(m-1) is retained and is described
by (6.1) with r=1; its constant remaining gradient is integrated over
every step, without replacing the earlier nonlinear history by a mass.

The original lexicographic permutation is valid throughout. New ties
are broken in that same label order; the proof (6.6), not symmetry
alone, excludes any unreported crossing or reversal. Residual block
counts are d,d/2,...,2 at the corresponding transition states. They
never merge to the one-block quadratic core. The gradients at these
states span the whole m-dimensional span of epsilon_1,...,epsilon_m,
since consecutive gradients differ by the nonzero orthogonal vector
2^(-k)epsilon_k. Thus this is a growing nonlinear trajectory, not one
constant-gradient exterior ray with a relabeled scalar amplitude.

Most directly, every query is genuinely outside K_d. For the top
half A={epsilon:epsilon_1=+1}, (6.3) gives

    sum_(epsilon in A)x_t(epsilon)=d q_1(t)/4>d/4=F_d(A).
                                                               (6.9)

The lower signs cancel on this set. This proves non-core status at
t=1 and throughout the final tail, including t=n, without a no-core
hypothesis. Although the final tail is one exterior mode, there is no
quadratic-core entry anywhere in the constructed trajectory.

## 7. Original metric, all ordered interpolation rows, and terminal loss

All x_t are centered. Thus zero is their closest minimizer on the
entire line (3.4), and the true initial squared radius is

    R_m^2=d [M^2/4+sum_(k=2)^m 4^(-k)P_(m-k+1)^2]
         =(d/4)(M^2+D_m),
    D_m=sum_(j=1)^(m-1)4^(j-m)(S_j+b_j)^2>0.         (7.1)

Every factor d comes from the actual Euclidean orthogonality in
Section 5. There is no uncharged dimension or coefficient-space
normalization. At any query the value is exactly

    Phi_d(x_t)=d sum_(k<=r(t))4^(-k)(q_k(t)-1/2).     (7.2)

This follows by substituting the true gradient in (3.1). At the
terminal point r(n)=1, q_1(n)=S_m+1, so

    x_n=(S_m+1)epsilon_1/2,
    g_n=epsilon_1/2,
    Phi_d(x_n)=d(2S_m+1)/8=d M/8.                   (7.3)

It follows that the actual normalized payoff of f=L Phi_d is

    V_(m,tau)=2f(x_n)/(L dist(x_0,argmin f)^2)
             =M/(M^2+D_m)
             =1/[M(1+E_m)],  E_m=D_m/M^2>0.         (7.4)

This also gives W_n(H_m)>=V_(m,tau) as a legal single-function lower
witness. The upper bounds on V below cannot be reversed into upper
bounds on W_n.

For clarity all original interpolation rows are paid directly. Put
F_i=Phi_d(x_i), G_i=g_i, Y_i=x_i-g_i and add the closest minimizer
sample x_star=G_star=F_star=0. For EVERY ordered pair of samples,

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<G_i,x_j-x_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2
        =<G_i-G_j,Y_j><=0.                          (7.5)

The identity follows by substitution of F_i=<x_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.
The inequality is (3.2) at j, since G_i belongs to K_d. It includes
all distant, incoming-terminal, outgoing-terminal and optimum pairs.
Their Gram matrices are PSD because these are actual vectors in R^d.
No pair or vector coordinate has been deleted.

The scalar unit-threshold Huber function H(u), equal to u^2/2 for
|u|<=1 and |u|-1/2 for |u|>=1, is a legal 1-smooth comparison on the
SAME word. Started at M=2S_m+1, it stays above 1 with state M-L_t.
Its exact true-distance terminal payoff is 1/M. Therefore

    V_(m,tau)/(1/M)=1/(1+E_m)<1                      (7.6)

for every m>=2 and 0<tau<m, not merely in the asymptotic diagnostic
interval. The disappearing modes contribute their complete original
squared radii to D_m and contribute zero terminal value. Orthogonality
prevents their initial costs from cancelling. This is the specific
loss mechanism of the frozen profile.

The same finite algebra explains the whole scope of this failure
mechanism, without selecting a second start: take m>=2, d=2^m, any
length-n word with all steps>1, and integer lifetimes
n>=T_2>...>T_m>=1. Write L_t for its true prefix masses and S=L_n.
For the profile Q epsilon_1/2+sum_(k=2)^m 2^(-k)L_(T_k)epsilon_k,
where Q>S+1, the proof of (6.3) applies with these prefix masses.
Writing D=sum_(k=2)^m 4^(1-k)L_(T_k)^2, its exact payoff is

    [2Q-(2S+1)]/[Q^2+D],

with D>0 the paid lower-mode radius contribution. It is strictly less
than 1/(2S+1), because the difference after positive cross multiplication
is (Q-(2S+1))^2+D>0. This scoped observation excludes rescuing this
extinction mechanism merely by increasing its surviving top amplitude.
It does not cover starts with surviving lower modes, overshoots, new
rank permutations, or general non-core points of the same body. The
selected profile remains exactly (5.5), with no repair or retuning.

## 8. Closed mass formulas and a quantitative growing-dimension limit

Put

    zeta=alpha/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)],  u=1-zeta.

For x=tau/m in (0,1),

    c(c-2)=1+(4+2sqrt(2))x+lambda^2 x^2>1>beta,

so 0<zeta<1 and u>0. The defining concatenation has
S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+alpha c^j. Direct substitution at j=0 and in this
identity proves, symbolically for every j,

    S_j=zeta c^j+u 2^j-1,
    P_j=S_j+b_j=zeta(c-1)c^j+u 2^j,
    M=2zeta c^m+2u 2^m-1.                          (8.1)

No recurrence has been executed. Let A=zeta(c-1). Summing the three
geometric series in the square of P_j gives the finite closed formula

    D_m=4^(-m){
         A^2 [(4c^2)^m-4c^2]/(4c^2-1)
       + 2Au [(8c)^m-8c]/(8c-1)
       + u^2 [16^m-16]/15 }.                        (8.2)

All j from 1 through m-1, including the root transition, appear in
this identity. It keeps every radius contribution; none has been
discarded as a small-coordinate or short-child error.

Here is explicit uniform control of its growing terms. On
tau in [1/5,1/4] and m>=3, c lies in the compact interval
[lambda,13lambda/12], zeta is bounded away from zero, and

    c=lambda+O(1/m), zeta=1+O(1/m), u=O(1/m),        (8.3)

with constants independent of tau. The displayed polynomial for
c(c-2) proves all of these estimates, including the positive lower
bound for zeta. Define

    delta_m=(u/zeta)(2/c)^m,
    epsilon_m=1/(2zeta c^m).

Then M=2zeta c^m(1+delta_m-epsilon_m). Dividing (8.2) by
4zeta^2 c^(2m), its three terms become respectively

    (c-1)^2/[4(4c^2-1)] * [1-(4c^2)^(1-m)],
    (c-1)u/[2zeta(8c-1)] * (2/c)^m * [1-(8c)^(1-m)],
    u^2/[60zeta^2] * (2/c)^(2m) * [1-16^(1-m)].      (8.4)

To obtain E_m, divide their sum by
(1+delta_m-epsilon_m)^2. This denominator tends uniformly to 1
and is bounded away from zero for all sufficiently large m. Every
coefficient in (8.4) is uniformly bounded. The second and third terms
have the indicated exponentially decaying factors; the omitted piece
of the first is also exponentially small. Thus

    E_m=eta+O(1/m),
    eta=(lambda-1)^2/[4(4lambda^2-1)]
       =1/[2(4lambda^2-1)]>0,                       (8.5)

uniformly on the tau interval. This proves in particular that the
paid radius of the nonlinear modes is a nonzero limiting fraction,
not an infinitesimal decoration of a scalar ray.

For completeness, log(1+x) lies between x-x^2/2 and x for x>=0;
this follows by integrating 1/(1+x) between 1-x and 1. Hence

    exp(tau-tau^2/(2m)) <= c^m/lambda^m <= exp(tau).

Together with (8.1)--(8.3), this gives, uniformly in tau,

    M/lambda^m=2exp(tau)+O(1/m),
    lambda^m V_(m,tau)
       =exp(-tau)/[2(1+eta)]+O(1/m).                (8.6)

There is one constant K, independent of tau and m, bounding the
absolute error in the second line by K/m for sufficiently large m.
The geometric errors above are bounded by a constant times 1/m;
this follows, for any fixed 0<r<1, from convergence of sum m r^m.
Thus this is a quantitative bound on all accumulated growing-family
terms, not a formal interchange of a growing sum and a limit.

Multiplying by the accepted convergent scalar ray in (1.1) proves
(A), uniformly on [1/5,1/4]. More explicitly its uniform error is
bounded by

    (62/25)K/m + K' |B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m-Z_1|,        (8.7)

for a fixed K', using the fully proved scalar envelope in the next
section. No quantitative rate for the accepted ray is assumed.

## 9. An elementary all-split scalar envelope and absolute deficit

This section is part of the same JOINT UNVERIFIED argument. Its
scalar bound is proved independently of saved numerical endpoints.
Set rho=log_2(lambda), p=rho/2 and kappa=62/25. One has p>5/8,
because lambda^4=17+12sqrt(2)>32 (use sqrt(2)>5/4).

First the following elementary power inequality holds:

    (25/31)x^(2p)+(1-x)^p<=1,
                0<=x<=1, p>=5/8.                   (9.1)

Increasing p can only decrease the left side, so consider

    psi(x)=(25/31)x^(5/4)+(1-x)^(5/8).

Its endpoint values are 1 and 25/31. An interior critical point obeys

    x^(1/4)(1-x)^(3/8)=31/50,
    x^2(1-x)^3=(31/50)^8.                           (9.2)

The function x^2(1-x)^3 decreases for x>=2/5. For x>=5/8,

    x^2(1-x)^3<=675/32768<21/1000
        <(147/1000)^2<(31/50)^8.                    (9.3)

The fixed rational comparisons use 675000<688128,
21000<21609, and
(31/50)^4=923521/6250000>147/1000 since 923521>918750.
Thus every interior critical point lies below 5/8.

At a critical point, the derivative equation makes

    psi(x)=(1-x/2)/(1-x)^(3/8)=:g(x).

The logarithmic derivative of g has the sign of 5x-2:

    g'(x)/g(x)=(5x-2)/[8(1-x)(2-x)].

Consequently its maximum on [0,5/8] is at an endpoint. Now g(0)=1
and g(5/8)<1, the latter being equivalent to

    11^8=214358881<226492416=27*2^23.

The continuous function psi attains a maximum either at an endpoint
or at such an interior critical point. This proves (9.1).
All these are displayed symbolic rational inequalities, not results
of an executed calculation or a numerical evaluation.

We also give the primitive power inequality rather than assume a
new recurrence fact. For positive a,b let A=a^(1/rho), D=b^(1/rho),
T=(A+D)^rho. Put E(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. The function
z -> log E(exp z) is convex: its second derivative is

    rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
       *[(1+t)^rho-1-rho*t]/E(t)^2 >=0,  t=exp z,

because the bracket vanishes at zero and has positive derivative
for t>0. Convexity at opposite arguments gives

    E(t)E(1/t)>=E(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2.

It follows that (T-a)(T-b)>=2ab, or
T^2-(a+b)T-ab>=0. Since T is positive, it lies above the positive
root Omega(a,b). Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction over
ALL original primitive splits therefore proves

    C_n<=(n+1)^rho.                                 (9.4)

We claim the corresponding dominant bound

    B_n<=kappa(n+1)^rho,              n>=0.          (9.5)

The case n=0 is 1<=kappa. In an arbitrary split at n>=1 put
A=k+1, D=n-k and N=A+D=n+1. By induction and (9.4), the two
arguments of G are at most A^rho and kappa D^rho. The function G
is increasing in both nonnegative arguments, directly from its formula.
Its value G(a,b) is the square of the positive root v of
v^2-sqrt(b)v-2a=0. Therefore it is at most T=kappa N^rho if

    2A^rho+kappa D^(rho/2)N^(rho/2)<=kappa N^rho.

On dividing by kappa N^rho, this is exactly (9.1) with x=A/N
and p=rho/2, since 2/kappa=25/31. It holds for EVERY split, so
taking their original maximum proves (9.5), without balancing or
deleting any split. In particular, (1.1) gives

    Z_1<=62/25.                                    (9.6)

It also gives an explicit finite all-depth upper law for the chosen
actual instance:

    B_(2^m-1)V_(m,tau)
       <=(62/25)lambda^m M/(M^2+D_m),               (9.7)

where M,D_m are the closed expressions (8.1)--(8.2). This is not
an upper bound for unrestricted W_n(H_m).

To obtain an absolute benchmark deficit, lambda<5/2 implies
eta>1/48. For every tau in [1/5,1/4], positivity of the exponential
series gives exp(tau)>=exp(1/5)>1+1/5+1/50=61/50. Thus

    Z_1 exp(-tau)/[2(1+eta)]
       <(31/25)(50/61)(48/49)
       =2976/2989<249/250.                          (9.8)

The last comparison is 744000<744261 after multiplication by
the positive denominators. Uniform convergence in Section 8 now
supplies a single finite integer M_0>=3 such that

    B_(2^m-1)V_(m,tau)<249/250
       for EVERY m>=M_0 and EVERY tau in [1/5,1/4].  (9.9)

No numerical value of M_0 is asserted. At tau=1/4, use
exp(1/4)>1+1/4+1/32=41/32 instead. The limit then satisfies

    Z_1 exp(-1/4)/[2(1+eta)]
       <(31/25)(32/41)(48/49)
       =47616/50225<19/20.                          (9.10)

The final comparison is 952320<954275. Hence at tau=1/4 the
actual finite scaled payoff is below 19/20 at every sufficiently
large depth. This sharper single-tau statement retains the SAME
frozen profile, body and physical word convention.

## 10. Exact failure scope and what remains open

The selected profile does meet the non-core geometry obligation:
first update outside K_d, every later query outside K_d, m-1
genuine block mergers, gradient span m, actual root-bridge transition,
and full original tail. No step permutation, coordinate deletion,
smoothness discount, or designated-minimizer radius is used.

It fails as a route to matching the benchmark on the stated diagnostic
interval. Its exact finite payoff is (7.4), its full limiting law is
(A), and its uniform strict benchmark deficit is (9.9). The failure
is not first-update core entry, nor eventual quadratic multiplication:
the lower modes die exactly at their physical bridges while their
initial Euclidean cost remains. Their limiting radius surcharge is
eta>0. The finite same-word Huber deficit holds on the larger domain
m>=2, 0<tau<m, as does the exact transition law.

The argument does NOT exclude other non-core starts of this SAME
arithmetic-rank body. In particular, starts with genuine rank crossings,
re-emerging modes, or several surviving terminal modes are not covered.
It does not optimize the all-word interval-average law, does not give
an upper bound for W_n, and does not settle the original minimax Goal.
The additional scalar envelope (9.5) is a benchmark bound, not a
certificate on arbitrary functions or schedules.

There was ONE selected profile and ZERO mathematical repairs. No
second profile or continuation allocation is inferred from these open
directions. No formal submission is licensed by this complete draft.

## Notation index

F_d: frozen cardinality rank function. K_d: its unchanged centered base
body. Phi_d: static smooth envelope. P_d: Euclidean projection. E_d:
centered coordinate hyperplane. e: all-ones vector. bar(x): coordinate
mean. a_i: arithmetic rank increment. sigma_d: body's support function.

projection, and isotonic prefix residual in Section 4. pi_t,sigma_t:
rank-to-label and next-sort permutations; distinct from the support
function sigma_d. L: advertised smoothness; L_t: original prefix mass.

(5.1), always with the same enclosing (m,tau). rho=log_2(lambda).
nu_2: binary valuation. epsilon_k: k-th binary-sign coordinate vector.
i(epsilon): its original lexicographic rank. T_k: physical update
count where mode k>=2 disappears. q_k(t): actual binary coefficient.
r(t): number of active modes. ell: size of a current rank block.

M=2S_m+1: top initial coefficient. P_j=S_j+b_j: actual prefix mass
through the first level-j bridge; distinct from projection P_d.
R_m: true initial distance to the minimum line. D_m: paid lower-mode
radius surcharge after the common factor d/4. E_m=D_m/M^2. V_(m,tau):
actual normalized payoff of the frozen instance. eta: its positive
limiting relative radius surcharge. zeta,u,A in Section 8: scalar
mass coefficients; u there is not the residual vector in Section 4.
delta_m,epsilon_m: exact relative mass corrections. K,K': fixed error
constants. kappa=62/25 and p=rho/2: scalar-envelope constants. psi,g
in Section 9: one-variable functions, distinct from projection vectors.
M_0: an eventual uniform depth, whose numerical value is not asserted.

# Paid short-leaf pairs and uniform dyadic fans on the original space

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, worker max79. The original target is OPEN.
This is one bounded invocation under TASK.md and guidance29dd9d75f3f7d055:
two explicit coefficient laws, zero mathematical programs, numerical replay,
formal submissions or subagents. No statement below is a newly accepted fact.
All new identities and failure arguments are derived together, not used as
independently accepted intermediate results.

The two proposed additions genuinely change the Huber contact derivative.
Their entire initial/common Schur charge is displayed, with one global
objective allowance. Nevertheless an ORIGINAL H4 block direction annihilates
every adjacent short-leaf pair and gives a nonpositive sum at each uniform
dyadic fan. Its inherited base action is strictly negative. Thus neither law
is PSD at the requested level, or at any other objective level. The same
calculation covers arbitrary nonnegative weights in the specified additive
support classes. It does not cover a changed base, signed reallocations,
nonuniform within-cell fan weights, or the unrestricted full dual.

## 1. Original target and exact accepted scopes

For every integer n>=0 and word h in [0,infinity)^n, W_n(h) is the supremum
of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional real
Euclidean spaces, convex differentiable L-smooth functions with an attained
minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star. The original physical updates are
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i), where L>0. Thus W=2R.

The ORIGINAL benchmark is C_0=B_0=1 and

    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

All splits are integers. The target asks whether
lim_n B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n)W_n(h)=1; an affirmative lower statement
requires, for every eta>0, a word-independent N_eta such that every actual
n>=N_eta and every such word has B_n W_n(h)>=1-eta.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation convention,
the full independent-vector certificate, and exact terminal completion.
Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 supplies its stated primitive/objective
composition identities and benchmark, not an unconditional primitive seed.
Only the benchmark conclusion of fact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10, is used:

    B_(2^m-1)/D_m<237/250 for every m>=1536, tau=1/4.       (1)

Here D_m and the SAME-enclosing-m word are defined in Section2. A full-class
bound W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=21/(20D_m) would therefore imply
B_n W_n(H_m)<4977/5000 on unbounded ACTUAL horizons. This draft does not
obtain that bound. A failed certificate supplies no upper or lower bound on W.

## 2. Fixed physical hierarchy

Fix m>=1536 throughout and set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, tstar=1/lambda,
    Hword_0=(), Hword_(j+1)=(Hword_j,1+d_j,Hword_j), d_j=a*c^j,
    n_j=2^j-1, A_j=1+sum Hword_j, S_j=A_j-1,
    D_j=2A_j-1, t_j=d_j/A_j, h=H_m=Hword_m, n=n_m, D=D_m.

Every child retains this enclosing epsilon. In original indices,
h_i=1+a*c^level(i), where level(i) is the largest integer l with
2^l dividing i+1, for 0<=i<n. In particular every even query has
physical step b=1+a. The index n is the unqueried terminal; set h_n=0
only as notation in matrix formulas. No terminal step is executed.

The exact scalar identities, which are not executed recurrences, are

    A_j=v_h*c^j+(1-v_h)*2^j, v_h=a/(c-2),
    A_(j+1)=(2+t_j)A_j, t_(j+1)=c*t_j/(2+t_j).

They follow by summing the physical concatenation and solving its geometric
sum. Since c(c-2)>1>beta, one has 0<v_h<1. Consequently t_j increases
strictly and 0<t_j<c-2<1/2. For the final inequality,
lambda<17/7 and epsilon<=1/6144 imply c<5/2.
Direct substitution gives

    b=sqrt(2)-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)],
    A_1=lambda-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)], d_1=1-epsilon,
    A_2=lambda^2-epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))],
    d_2=lambda(1-epsilon^2),
    lambda*d_2-A_2
      =epsilon[1+4/(lambda(1+epsilon))-lambda^2 epsilon]>0. (2)

The bracket is positive since lambda^2<6 and epsilon<=1/6144.
Also lambda*d_1-A_1=epsilon[-lambda+2/(lambda(1+epsilon))]<0.
Thus t_0,t_1<tstar<t_2<=t_j for j>=2. In particular b>1,d_2>0,
and t_3>tstar. These strict signs are all that the negative test needs.

## 3. Every inherited primitive and objective coefficient

The inherited array is rederived here. Its prior unverified reports are
awareness, not premises. A primitive depth-j array Lambda^j has indices
0,...,n_j. Start with Lambda^0=0 and zero diagonal. At a depth-(j+1)
node, put p=n_j, z=n_(j+1). Embed the left Lambda^j with factor1 and
the right Lambda^j with factor k_j. For each nonterminal right query i add

    Lambda_(i,p) += u_j h_i, Lambda_(i,z) += v_j h_i,
    Lambda_(z,p) += w_j,    Lambda_(p,z) += z_j,
    u_j=2lambda,
    v_j=[2lambda*d_j+2(A_j+d_j)]/A_j,
    k_j=2+lambda+(lambda+1)t_j,
    w_j=4A_j+2d_j-2lambda(A_j-1), z_j=2A_j.           (3)

The last two additions occur once per node, not once per right query.
All unspecified contributions are zero and overlapping ones are summed.
Equivalently, for every dyadic node of depth l+1 and offset o=k*2^(l+1),
write its indicated four local original-index row contributions from (3).
Multiply each by the product of k_r over strict ancestors whose right child
contains that node, and sum over nodes. This is a finite sum/product formula
for EVERY Lambda^j entry, without an optimizer or a computed recurrence.

All signs are positive: w_j=2lambda+2(d_j-A_j/lambda)>2lambda for j>=2;
w_0=4+2a>0; w_1>2lambda-2 because A_1<lambda and d_1>0.
The other factors in (3) are positive. The identities

    u_j+v_j=2(k_j-1),
    u_j(A_j-1)+w_j-z_j=2(A_j+d_j)

show that divergence is -2h_i at every primitive nonterminal and 2S_j
at its terminal. For example a right nonterminal has divergence
-2k_j h_i+(u_j+v_j)h_i=-2h_i. At p the divergence is
2S_j+z_j-u_j S_j-w_j=-2(1+d_j). Total divergence is zero,
so the terminal has the asserted remaining divergence.

For the objective array start Kbase^0=0, mu^0_0=2. At a join put

    s_j=(S_j+1+d_j)/(3S_j+2(1+d_j)), theta_j=1-s_j,
    Dc=D_j, Dp=D_(j+1), p=n_j, R_i=2^j+i.

Here Kbase is the finite array and mu is the outgoing optimum profile.
Every coefficient of its next depth is

    Kbase_(l,v)=Lambda^j_lv/Dp                    (left indices),
    Kbase_(R_i,R_v)=Kbase^j_iv                   (right indices),
    Kbase_(R_i,p)=s_j mu^j_i                    (including i=n_j),
    mu_l=2h_l/Dp                               (0<=l<n_j),
    mu_p=2s_j-2S_j/Dp,
    mu_(R_i)=theta_j mu^j_i.                         (4)

Every incoming-optimum multiplier is zero in this CHOSEN base. This is not
a claimed normal form for general certificates. All unlisted finite entries
are zero. One can unroll (4) without any ambiguity: for an objective depth-r
word, its right-spine node of depth l has offset o_l=2^r-2^l. Insert its
left primitive array Lambda^(l-1)/D_l on o_l,...,o_l+n_(l-1); its bridge
is o_l+n_(l-1); every right query sends s_(l-1) times the right-child mu
to that bridge. A query's mu is its local 2h_i/D_l if a left nonterminal,
or 2s_(l-1)-2S_(l-1)/D_l if that bridge, times
product_(t=l)^(r-1)theta_t. The terminal has mu_n=2product_(t=0)^(r-1)theta_t.
The unique first such left-or-bridge category assigns each nonterminal.
Together with the finite node sum for Lambda, these specify every original
Kbase and mu coefficient by finitely many sums/products of physical scalars.

The bridge sign is positive: writing A=S_j,Bjoin=1+d_j, the numerator is
(A+Bjoin)Dp-A(3A+2Bjoin)=A^2+4ABjoin+2Bjoin^2+A+Bjoin>0.
Also 0<s_j,theta_j<1. Primitive flow pays the left mu; at p the left
terminal divergence plus mu_p equals 2s_j, canceled by its incoming
right mass 2s_j. At every right query the old mu is split into its new
theta_j mu and its new s_j mu edge. Hence original value cancellation is

    sum_v Kbase_iv-sum_v Kbase_vi+mu_i=2*1_(i=n),
    sum_i mu_i=2, mu_i>0.                             (5)

No row points into the ACTUAL terminal n in this base. Internal child
terminal indices are still present, including their incoming rows.

## 4. Full polynomial identities for the base

Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0, as in fact1252b8823bb5a290.
All original vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_n) remain independent. The column Ppos
has entries Ppos_i below. Define

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, y_i=x_i-g_i,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_j=2sum_(i<n_j)h_i(f_i-f_(n_j))
          +sum_(i<n_j)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
          -A_j(A_j-1)||g_(n_j)||^2,
    Psrc_j=V_j+sum_(i!=v)Lambda^j_iv Q_iv.

Flow cancels every value in Psrc. Let r_R=sum_(right nonterminal i)h_i g_i,
Y=r_R-(A_j-1)g_z, Z=g_p-g_z. Expansion of the four unweighted row sums
in (3), in their listed order, gives the gradient expressions

    d_j<r_R,g_p>+||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                                          +(A_j-1)||g_p||^2/2;
    -||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2/2
                        +(A_j-1)||g_z||^2/2-<r_R,g_z>;
    d_j<g_z,g_p>+<g_z,r_R>+(||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2)/2;
    -(d_j+1/2)||g_p||^2+||g_z||^2/2-<g_p,r_R>-<g_p,g_z>.

For example the sum of forward within-right displacement terms is
-||r_R||^2/2+sum h_i^2||g_i||^2/2; this fixes its diagonal correction.
Multiply the displays by u_j,v_j,w_j,z_j and add V and the child terms.
Using lambda^2=2lambda+1 gives the complete identity

    Psrc_(j+1)=Psrc_j^left+k_j Psrc_j^right
      +(tstar-t_j)[(lambda+1)||Y||^2-2lambda A_j<Y,Z>
                         +A_j^2(2+tstar+t_j)||Z||^2].    (6)

This is an identity on all original gradients. In particular it makes no
positive-epsilon primitive PSD assertion.

Let Cbase^j be the entire gradient matrix of the objective polynomial after
(5). Put T_R=sum_i mu^j_i g_(R_i), x_p=x_0-sum_(l<n_j)h_l g_l and

    Rchild=Cbase^j(g_R)-Dc||T_R||^2/4,
    kappa_j=Dc-Dp theta_j^2.

Expansion of all (4) rows gives

    Mbase_(j+1)(1/Dp)=Psrc_j(g_left)/Dp+Rchild
     +||x_p+(S_j-Dp s_j)g_p-(Dp theta_j/2)T_R||^2/Dp
     +(kappa_j/4)||T_R-2g_p||^2.                       (7)

For a coefficient check, after subtracting the first two terms the remaining
polynomial is exactly

    ||x_p||^2/Dp+2(S_j/Dp-s_j)<x_p,g_p>
    +(S_j^2/Dp+2s_j)||g_p||^2-theta_j<x_p,T_R>
    +(1+d_j-s_j)<g_p,T_R>+Dc||T_R||^2/4.

Expanding the two squares agrees because
s_j(3S_j+2(1+d_j))=S_j+1+d_j. Neither Rchild nor kappa_j is assumed
nonnegative for the deformed hierarchy.

At the full Huber contact tuple x_0=D_j, g_i=1, every square argument
and each Y,Z in (6) is zero. Induction from Mbase_0=||x_0-g_0||^2
also makes the derivative of Rchild zero at its common-gradient vector.
Thus, writing e=(1,...,1),

    Cbase e=D mu/2, e^T Cbase e=D.                     (8)

For the actual m>=1536 (indeed m>=2), one useful original entry is
Cbase_00=2/D, with mu_0=2b/D: only the
first primitive depth1 term (2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2 contributes to the first
gradient diagonal in the left source, and the x_p square contributes b^2/D.
These statements are jointly derived here, not imported from the last report.

## 5. The two explicit additions and every final sign

The physical non-leaf checkpoints are the odd indices p<n. If level(p)=l,
then 1<=l<=m-1 and p=o+2^l-1 for a unique cell offset
o=k*2^(l+1), 0<=k<2^(m-l-1). Define the complete cell of original
gradient indices as o,...,o+2^(l+1)-1, and its short-leaf set

    E_(l,o)={o,o+2,...,o+2^(l+1)-2}.

These are actual even queried indices; every one has h_i=b. No terminal
gradient is a member of a leaf set or checkpoint set.

LAW A, preceding leaf. For each non-leaf p of level l put

    omega_p=epsilon*A_l^2/D,
    J^A_(p-1,p)=J^A_(p,p-1)=omega_p.                  (9)

LAW B, whole-cell fan. For each (l,o) and p=o+2^l-1 put

    gamma_(l,o)=epsilon*A_l^2/(D*2^l),
    J^B_(i,p) += gamma_(l,o), J^B_(p,i) += gamma_(l,o)
                         for every i in E_(l,o).      (10)

All other entries of J are zero and all stated contributions are summed.
The two laws are different supports, not two fits of the old optimum-row
transport. The factor 2^l makes their total added mass identical at every
scale, allowing a direct comparison without silently increasing a budget.
For either law the FINAL original coefficients are

    K_ij=Kbase_ij+J_ij (i!=j), K_ii=0,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i, lambda_(star,i)=0,
    q=21/(20D).                                      (11)

All final multipliers are nonnegative. Symmetry J_ij=J_ji preserves every
value-flow equation (5). The optimum profile is fixed as a deliberate
restriction of these two laws; no claim is made that a general slack-spending
construction may be reduced to this restriction. No decrement is hidden.

Each law has the ONE entire-word multiplier-mass account

    T79=(epsilon/D)sum_(l=1)^(m-1)2^(m-l-1)A_l^2,
    sum_(i!=j)J_ij=2T79.                             (12)

This is not a sufficient PSD budget. The actual global objective/contact
budget, including all cross terms between cells, is given next.

## 6. Every full entry, the changed contact, and its ONE Schur budget

For ANY array of the form (11) define out_i=mu_i+sum_v K_iv. The entire
matrix C in

    M(q)=q||x_0||^2-sum_i mu_i<x_0,g_i>
                       +sum_(i,j)C_ij<g_i,g_j>

has entries

    C_ii=out_i-1_(i=n)-h_i sum_(v>i)K_iv,
    2C_ij=-(K_ij+K_ji)-h_j sum_(v>j)K_iv
                         +h_i[mu_j+sum_(v<=i)K_jv], i<j. (13)

The complete original matrix therefore has initial entry q and initial
mixed entries -mu_i/2. To derive (13), flow says half the incident row mass
equals out_i-1_(i=n). A forward row sourced at i contributes -h_i K_iv
to its diagonal. For i<j the difference squares give -(K_ij+K_ji),
rows out of i ending after j give the second term, and rows out of j ending
at/before i or at the optimum give the last term. This also accounts for
every physical intermediate gradient in a nonlocal pair.

For an unordered pair represented by u<v let L_uv(g)=sum_(t=u)^(v-1)h_t g_t
and ell_uv=Ppos_v-Ppos_u. The exact added polynomial per unit weight is

    Q_uv+Q_vu=||g_u-g_v||^2-<g_u-g_v,L_uv(g)>
             =<g_u-g_v,y_v-y_u>.                     (14)

For v=u+1 across b this is
(1-b)||g_u||^2+(b-2)<g_u,g_v>+||g_v||^2. Its determinant is -b^2/4,
so it is indefinite whenever b>0. It is not a free PSD correction.

At g=e the value of (14) is zero, but its gradient is
ell_uv(e_v-e_u). Consequently define the entire contact-defect column

    d79=(1/2)sum_(unordered {u,v},u<v)J_uv*ell_uv*(e_v-e_u).

Here e_i denotes the standard coordinate column, distinct from e. Then

    Ce=D mu/2+d79, e^T d79=0, e^T C e=D.              (15)

For A the formula is d79=(b/2)sum_p omega_p(e_p-e_(p-1)). For B it is
(1/2)sum_(l,o)gamma_(l,o)sum_(i in E_(l,o))(Ppos_p-Ppos_i)(e_p-e_i),
with signed Ppos_p-Ppos_i valid on both sides of the checkpoint. Each is one
finite original-index vector, not separate contact credits. Both are nonzero:

    Ppos^T d79=(1/2)sum_(u<v)J_uv(Ppos_v-Ppos_u)^2>0.           (16)

All physical steps are positive and each law has a positive added edge.
Thus Ce=D mu/2 is NOT assumed; it is false for both candidates.

At x_0=D,g=e the complete value is (qD-1)D=D/20. Its initial/common
two-by-two block is

    Bcommon=[[q,-1],[-1,D]], det Bcommon=qD-1=1/20.

This is the ONE scalar allowance for the whole word. Write arbitrary
gradients as g=t e+v with e^T v=0, first in one real coordinate and then
coordinatewise in a Euclidean space. Set u=mu^T v and z=d79^T v. Their
mixed column with (x_0,t) is (-u/2,Du/2+z). Exact inversion gives

    (-u/2,Du/2+z) Bcommon^(-1) (-u/2,Du/2+z)^T
       =D u^2/4+u z+[q/(qD-1)]z^2
       =D u^2/4+u z+21z^2/D.                        (17)

For clarity the numerator before dividing by qD-1 is
D(qD-1)u^2/4+(qD-1)uz+qz^2. Thus all contact, common-gradient and
initial costs have been charged exactly once. In particular the last term
is the square of the WHOLE sum d79^T v and includes all cross-cell terms.
It cannot be paid anew from 1/20 at every node.

Equivalently, the entire remaining gradient matrix is

    R79=C-D mu mu^T/4-(mu d79^T+d79 mu^T)/2
                                      -(21/D)d79 d79^T,
    R79 e=0.                                        (18)

Full PSD at q=21/(20D) is equivalent to R79 being PSD on the entire
e-perpendicular space, or equivalently on all gradient coordinates since
R79 e=0. Equations (17)--(18) are a necessary-and-sufficient expenditure
identity, NOT a claim that either law pays it successfully. The direct
negative full-polynomial test below disproves that claim.

## 7. Actual terminal-square expenditure

Neither added support touches n. The final incoming-terminal coefficients
remain zero by the explicit formulas, not by deleting rows from a supplied
certificate. Full terminal flow is therefore sum_j lambda_(n,j)=2 and
C_nn=1. More explicitly the receivers are star and the right-spine bridges

    p_l=2^m-2^l-1, 0<=l<m,
    alpha_star=product_(v=0)^(m-1)theta_v,
    alpha_(p_l)=s_l product_(v=0)^(l-1)theta_v.        (19)

An empty product is1. All other alpha_i are zero. The finite telescoping
identity sum_l s_l product_(v<l)theta_v=1-product_(v<m)theta_v shows these
are probabilities. They are exactly lambda_(n,j)/2 from (4), including
the adjacent final bridge p_0=n-1. No unqueried gradient is discarded.

The exact vector in the terminal square is

    Vterm=alpha_star x_0
         -sum_(t<n)[h_t w_t-(w_t-w_(t-1))]g_t,
    w_(-1)=alpha_star, w_t=alpha_star+sum_(i=0)^t alpha_i.

The complete remainder is

    M(q)=||g_n-Vterm||^2+Rprefix,
    Rprefix=q||x_0||^2-2sum_(j in {star,0,...,n-1})alpha_j
                    (f_j-||g_j||^2/2)-||Vterm||^2
                  +sum_(i!=j in {star,0,...,n-1})lambda_ij Q_ij. (20)

The prefix flow cancels every f_j. Formula (20) follows by expanding the
terminal rows, as in
fact1252b8823bb5a290(c), and retains the actual negative square cost.
Equivalently put vterm=(mu_n/2)x_0-sum_(i<n)C_in g_i; then Vterm=vterm
and Rprefix=q||x_0||^2-sum_(i<n)mu_i<x_0,g_i>
+sum_(i,j<n)C_ij<g_i,g_j>-||vterm||^2.
Since the added rows contain no terminal gradients, they do not alter
C_in or vterm. They add their FULL (14) expressions to Rprefix.

## 8. Original-space block direction and its complete base action

Put

    W79=A_2+d_2, Bfirst=b+1+d_1,
    Edef=(t_2-tstar)A_2^2(lambda+t_2)>0,
    delta3=t_3-tstar>0.

W79 is the physical mass of steps0,...,3 and of steps8,...,11.
The notation is local and does not replace W_n(h). In the ENTIRE original
formal space assign scalar values

    x_0=0,
    g_i=1 for i=0,1,2,3;
    g_i=-1 for i=8,9,10,11;
    g_i=0 for every other i=0,...,n.                 (21)

The two active four-step blocks have identical physical words. Hence the
total displacement is W79-W79=0. All positions from index12 onward are0;
g_7=g_15=g_n=0. These are chosen test values, not equality constraints
on formal coordinates and not a legal primal trajectory.

In the first H3, its left H2 gradients are all1, its right H2 all0,
so both child Psrc terms vanish by (6), and Y=0,Z=1 gives -Edef.
The H3 beginning at8 gives the same -Edef. At their H4 join,
Y=-W79,Z=0. Thus the COMPLETE source polynomial is

    Psrc_4=-(1+k_3)Edef-delta3(lambda+1)W79^2<0.     (22)

For every containing source ancestor all its right gradients, bridge and
terminal are zero, so Y=Z=0 and (6) propagates exactly (22), with left
factor1. At the top objective join the left displacement, x_p, g_p and
T_R are allzero; Rchild on its zero right gradients is zero. Equation (7)
therefore gives for every m>=5, in particular all m>=1536,

    Mbase(q;21)=Psrc_4/D<0 for EVERY real q.           (23)

No positive-epsilon primitive child or quotient feasibility has been used.
The parameter signs came from the exact SAME-m (2).

## 9. Law A: every preceding-leaf symmetric pair vanishes

Every edge in (9) connects an even index to the next odd index. Assignment
(21) is constant on every such pair, including pairs outside its support.
Thus g_(p-1)-g_p=0 and its ENTIRE (14) polynomial is zero. All preceding
gradient costs and cross terms cancel exactly; no diagonal-only inference
is involved. Summing every added pair gives

    M_A(q;21)=Mbase(q;21)=Psrc_4/D<0.                 (24)

This proves a stronger, precise scope: for the fixed inherited base, ANY
nonnegative weights omega_p on this support fail for every q. Allowing
different scale laws, horizon dependence, arbitrary magnitudes or zero
weights cannot change (24). The new support is not a repair of the earlier
H4 defect. This is a formal PSD obstruction, not an interpolable witness.

## 10. Law B: every full-cell fan has nonpositive action

For a cell define its UNWEIGHTED full polynomial

    F_(l,o)=sum_(i in E_(l,o))<g_i-g_p,y_p-y_i>, p=o+2^l-1.

Then M_B-Mbase=sum_(l,o)gamma_(l,o)F_(l,o), with no omitted rows.
We evaluate every nonzero cell on (21), including cells whose checkpoint
is zero but whose source leaves are nonzero.

At l=1 each cell comprises four consecutive gradient indices; (21) is
constant on that cell. Its fan is zero. At l=2 only the cells o=0 and
o=8 can contribute. For o=0, p=3 has gradient1. The left leaves0,2 have
the same gradient and contribute zero. The right leaves4,6 have gradient0,
position -W79 and y=-W79, whereas y_3=-A_2. Each contributes
(-1)(W79-A_2)=-d_2. At o=8 the entire geometry through15 is the negative
of the first cell, apart from a common positional translation. A common
translation cancels in y_p-y_i, and negating both factors preserves their
product. Its two nonzero terms likewise equal -d_2. Therefore

    F_(2,0)=F_(2,8)=-2d_2<0.                        (25)

At l=3 only the first cell is active, with p=7, g_p=0 and y_p=-W79.
Its nonzero source leaves are0,2,8,10. Their original proximal positions
are respectively -1,-Bfirst-1,-W79+1,-W79+Bfirst+1.
The four complete terms, in this order, are

    1-W79, 1-W79+Bfirst, 1, 1+Bfirst.

Their sum is 4-2W79+2Bfirst. Since
W79=2b+2+d_1+d_2 and Bfirst=b+1+d_1, this is

    F_(3,0)=-2(b+d_2-1)<0.                          (26)

The positive terms1 and1+Bfirst have been explicitly paid by the negative
terms; they are not omitted. At EVERY l>=4 only the first cell can have
nonzero endpoint gradients, and its checkpoint has y_p=g_p=0. The same
four source leaves now contribute

    1, 1+Bfirst, 1-W79, 1-W79+Bfirst,

whose sum is again -2(b+d_2-1). Every other source and checkpoint has
zero gradient, so its factor g_i-g_p is zero, even if a position is nonzero.
All cells not listed are either disjoint from the support or were covered
by the constant-cell calculation. Hence the ENTIRE added value is

    M_B(q;21)-Mbase(q;21)
      =-2d_2[gamma_(2,0)+gamma_(2,8)]
       -2(b+d_2-1)sum_(l=3)^(m-1)gamma_(l,0)<0.      (27)

This proves M_B(q;21)<0 at every real q. More generally (27) is nonpositive
for arbitrary nonnegative weights gamma_(l,o), provided each individual
cell uses the SAME weight for all of its even source leaves. Thus every
nonnegative combination of these uniform full-cell fans, and also every
addition of the adjacent pairs from Section9, fails on the fixed base.
This is a consequence of the same two support calculations, not a third
law or extra search. Arbitrary unequal within-cell weights are NOT covered.

## 11. Boundary calibrations and scope audit

For n=0, the empty base uses mu_0=2 and M(q)=||x_0-g_0||^2+(q-1)||x_0||^2.
There are no additions in (9)--(10). For an arbitrary all-zero word, the
separate accepted calibration is lambda_(n,star)=2 with all other rowszero,
giving M(1)=||x_0-g_n||^2 on every original independent vector, including
duplicate-position gradients. It is not obtained by substituting a fictitious
positive hierarchy into the zero word. The general zero-step embedding is
exactly that of fact1252b8823bb5a290; neither new law asserts an arbitrary-word
extension or removes any formal coordinate at a zero step.

For a one-step hierarchy there is no non-leaf checkpoint. Direct expansion
of (3)--(7) gives

    Psrc_1=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2,
    Mbase_1(1/D_1)=||x_0-D_1(g_0+g_1)/2||^2/D_1
                              +(3-2b)||g_0-g_1||^2/4.

This is full PSD because b<sqrt(2)<3/2. It does not prove later PSD.

For the epsilon=0 Silver calibration, retain the same definitions with
epsilon=0, so A_j=lambda^j,d_j=A_j/lambda and t_j=tstar. Both additions
vanish identically; (6) gives Psrc_j=0 at all depths. Write A=S_j and
Bjoin=1+tstar(A+1). Direct expansion of kappa_j in (7) gives

    (3A+2Bjoin)^2 kappa_j
      =2(1-tstar)A^3+(6-8tstar)A^2
        +(1+tstar)(8tstar-2)A+(1+tstar)^2(1-2tstar)>0.

All coefficients are positive since 1/4<tstar<1/2. Starting at the empty
square, the Schur remainder Rchild of a PSD child with positive initial
coefficient is PSD, and (7) proves full Silver PSD inductively. This invokes
child PSD ONLY at epsilon=0, where it has just been established. The original
q=21/(20D) merely adds ||x_0||^2/(20D). It does not change the failures for
positive epsilon. No Silver calibration is imported to a deformed child.

The topology note021361fbf2347def explicitly asked for comparison with earlier
finite-row repairs. The H4 vector (21) is the OLD one in b29aef4e7da1bade;
it is not advertised as a new mode. The earlier retained-left bridge lift
was already negative there. The new conclusion is its exact zero action on
ALL preceding-leaf pairs and its nonpositive action on ALL uniform full-cell
fans. The single-source six-row repair of9864512779c0d768 was a different
signed support and failed on another vector in the old two-dimensional plane.
The optimum-profile transports of aadb47e847b5725f preserved full contact and
failed first/deep-gradient tests. Here contact is genuinely broken and the
global charge (17) replaces their exact-contact calculation; nevertheless
the older H4 direction is already decisive. Neither earlier report is a
correctness premise for the derivation in this draft.

These calculations separate the required layers:

* All final signs and original value flow hold; nonzero contact derivatives,
  the initial/common allowance, and actual terminal-square costs are explicit.
* Full PSD fails by the complete original polynomial (24) or (27), with x_0=0.
* The accepted original benchmark comparison (1) remains valid but cannot be
  combined with either failed candidate to bound W or conclude the target.

The vector (21) is NOT an interpolable primal witness: x_0=0 would be an
actual minimizer, incompatible with g_0=1. Its legitimate purpose is to test
PSD on the original independent vector space. No trajectory quotient was used.

## 12. Remaining interface, retrieval, and handoff

The at-most-two-law allocation is exhausted. An eventual repair of this
inherited base must have strictly positive total action on (21), exceeding
-Psrc_4/D, after all signed changes and costs. Adjacent pairs cannot supply
any; the uniform fans make its value worse. To escape this calculation one
must change a substantive ingredient, such as the base or within-cell source
distribution, or use paid signed finite/optimum reallocation. This is a
necessary directional screen, not a sufficient construction, a claim that
any such repair exists, or authorization for another law.

Skills applied: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving,
construct-counterexamples, identify-key-failures. Formal gate calls remain
forbidden by this TASK, so all new algebra stays in this one UNVERIFIED draft.
The only accepted dependencies are the three explicitly scoped fact IDs.

Retrieval stage: mature_subproblem; active program: paid short-leaf coupling;
missing mechanism: original-space curvature on old zero-displacement modes;
search mode: mutation; intent: construction. One Matlas query returned
2210.05371 (bounded constant GD under a PL hypothesis),1906.04648 (noisy
Armijo/SOS),2006.08426 (constrained Shadow-Walk). Their hypotheses/algorithms
do not furnish the needed certificate. Web fallback located related silver
and constant-schedule sources; no result from a new source is used as a premise.
The exact existing downloads/2309.16530.pdf and its extracted text were used
for Definition2.1, Theorem3.4, Lemmas3.5--3.7 and the proof including AppendixA.2.
The published positive residual is -2Q_ji in our convention; its exact Silver
identities cannot be substituted for the positive-epsilon full-space proof.
Its useful method is joint sign and polynomial auditing, performed here.
Primary background: https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.16530 . No external black box
is a proof step, and no additional literature layer is needed to reject this
specified support once it reduces to the already audited H4 test regime.

Resources: two explicit laws; zero mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic
replays, executed trajectories or recurrences, optimizers/solvers/benchmark DP,
formal calls/fact_submit/new facts, installations, paid consults, or subagents.
Only textual mathematics, read-only retrieval and administrative text/JSON/hash
I/O were used. All previous numerical grants stay CLOSED; no time is reused.
No runtime counter/worker/service, other TASK, truth-store file, or original
goal was edited. No outward publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown,
or automatic renewal is performed.
