# General real three-gradient geometry: a complete scoped obstruction

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report, max4 invocation75, 2026-10-02.
The verifier has not checked any new argument below. No fact is asserted.
One authorized invocation; at most two mechanisms; zero mathematical
programs, numerical evaluations, trajectory executions, formal calls,
paid consults, installations, or subagents. Original target: OPEN.

## 1. Result and precise scope

Fix the SAME enclosing integer m>=1536 and tau=1/4. Put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/(4m),
    c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l), 0<=l<m,
    S_l=sum H_l, X_l=1+S_l, n=2^m-1, h=H_m.

Every child uses this one pair (m,tau). Let j_L,j_O,j_R be the roots
of the left immediate child, the whole word, and the right immediate
child, respectively. In zero-based step indices,

    j_L=2^(m-2)-1, j_O=2^(m-1)-1, j_R=3*2^(m-2)-1.

These are precisely the three largest physical steps. Let Pairs_top
be the three chronological pairs (j_L,j_O), (j_O,j_R), (j_L,j_R).
The first pair is the earlier-tie-break top-two selector of recalled
finding25e480a41fc81415. Selecting any pair in this set is allowed.

Consider EVERY finite-dimensional static globally convex C1 function
with 1-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum. Its actual GD uses
all n entries of h. For a selected pair j<k, require only that its
actual queried gradients have the following three contiguous sectors:

    g_i=p for 0<=i<=j,
    g_i=q for j+1<=i<=k,
    g_i=r for k+1<=i<n.                                  (1.1)

The real vectors p,q,r, the initial point, function values, affine
offsets and the minimizer are otherwise unrestricted. In particular,
there is no radial initial-gradient assumption, no angle/sign/rank
restriction, no collinearity or orthogonality condition, no zero-height
assumption, and no specified formula for the function. Dimension three
is included, as is every larger finite dimension. Degenerate vectors,
ties, and coincident gradient sectors are included.

Two separately declared families are analyzed:

* Family E also requires g_n=r at the actual terminal point.
* Family Q imposes (1.1) only: the unqueried terminal gradient and every
  legal static continuation of the complete prefix are unrestricted.

For either family the normalized payoff about ANY chosen minimizer is

    V=2(f(x_n)-min f)/||x_0-x_star||^2.

Thus the conclusions also hold using the TRUE closest minimizer. They
are not bounds for unrestricted W_n(h).

The joint draft proves:

    sup_(Family E, all three pairs) V = 1/(1+2S_m),       (1.2)

with equality attained by a concrete Huber function, and

    1/(1+2S_m) <= sup_(Family Q, all three pairs) V
               <= 1/(1+2S_m-d0^2), d0=1+a,              (1.3)

where d0 is the ACTUAL final physical entry. In particular, for the
ORIGINAL all-integer-split benchmark B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1,

    B_n sup_(Family E) V < 237/250,
    B_n sup_(Family Q) V < 316/333 < 19/20.               (1.4)

All positive queried heights are paid by original interpolation rows.
These results close arbitrary three-gradient geometry at these THREE
specified checkpoint pairs. They do NOT close other checkpoint pairs,
more than three sectors, gradients varying inside a sector, arbitrary
3D functions, arbitrary schedules, or the original minimax question.
Section10 gives an explicit actual-word pair on which this particular
certificate becomes indefinite, preventing an unjustified all-pair
extension.

Mechanism1 is an arbitrary-real-vector interpolation certificate with
an exact original-space lift and no restriction on queried heights.
Mechanism2 retains the complete prefix and prices the original final
step, uniformly over ALL legal terminal completions. Both derivations
belong to this single joint unverified report.

## 2. Accepted inputs, recall, and normalization

Only these accepted facts are correctness premises:

* 1252b8823bb5a290: normalization, necessity of every ordered smooth
  convex interpolation inequality, explicit conjugate interpolation,
  and complete-prefix maximal extension. Its signed residual is

      Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.

* b98bb807d514e80d: canonical Huber/single-cut channel K_n and the
  original all-integer-split benchmark recursions.
* 5fd396eba75651e7: its precise SAME-m tau1/4 hierarchy and the accepted
  numerical-free statement B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250 for every m>=1536.
  No assertion about general witnesses is imported from its spiral
  family obstructions.

The accepted special3D fact c3bfc54321ddec65 was inspected to identify
its scope: its first bridge excess is A_mc*M with A_mc>=4B_mc+4 and
B_mc>=2, and its second is B_mc*M. Its prescribed r=-q/2 plus a third
coordinate and its prescribed words do not give the present hierarchy
comparison. None of its special-word payoffs is transplanted here.

The following recalled reports are awareness, NOT premises:

* 53d8b004885db92c (C104) has planar p=(1,0), q=(-u,-v), r=(s,ell),
  nonnegative u,s, independent but restricted middle intercept and
  residual signs. Its hierarchy has tau=1, not tau=1/4.
* 3387cf58f27ea36b (C106) permits planar r=(-w,ell) and signed terminal
  affine costs, but retains nonnegative original middle residuals and
  middle cost, and closes the canonical H_c>=0 branch. It too uses the
  stated tau=1 hierarchy. Neither its coordinates nor H_c condition
  cover arbitrary 3D heights and initial geometry.
* 25e480a41fc81415 selects the pair (j_L,j_O), with radial p and
  r=-kappa*q in its paid planar law, then completes that particular
  prefix. Its closure does not quantify over all prefixes in (1.1).
* Our2630258977073049 and eea0512960806ccc use opposite-collinear final
  and middle slopes on their own different physical word domains.

Consequently (1.2)--(1.4), if correct, are a substantive enlargement of
the closed GEOMETRY at the declared selections, not a retuning of a
previous law and not a claimed closure of all checkpoint selections.

For a function with advertised smoothness L, replace it by f/L and
translate a chosen actual minimizer to0. The physical steps become h
with smoothness1, the gradients in this report are those of f/L, and
the payoff is exactly W=2R. The chosen minimizer can be a closest one:
the attained minimum set is closed and convex in finite dimensions.
No assumption that the initial gradient is parallel to x_0 is made.

## 3. Exact finite original-space lift of arbitrary three-sector data

This section applies to any original nonnegative finite word written

    h=(block_0,b,block_1,c_step,block_2),
    P=sum block_0, M=sum block_1, T=sum block_2.

The blocks are their actual complete chronological lists; no entry is
replaced or executed by a different algorithm. Define their actual
starting points from any real x_0,p,q,r by

    Z_p=x_0,
    Z_q=x_0-(P+b)p,
    Z_r=x_0-(P+b)p-(M+c_step)q.                          (3.1)

Choose real starting values F_p,F_q,F_r. The proposed data at a query
with already executed sector mass s are

    (Z_p-s p,p,F_p-s||p||^2), 0<=s<=P,
    (Z_q-s q,q,F_q-s||q||^2), 0<=s<=M,
    (Z_r-s r,r,F_r-s||r||^2), 0<=s<=T.                  (3.2)

Only actual partial sums need to be queried. The final points s=P,M
are the first and second checkpoint queries; s=T is the actual
terminal point for Family E. All three endpoint pairs and the zero
optimum jet (0,0,0) form a set E of at most seven jets. Require ALL
ordered residuals Q_ij<=0 between these endpoints and the optimum.
This is an exact characterization of legal data of this form, not a
PSD-Gram-only relaxation.

Necessity of the displayed values for any real function follows by
using both convexity inequalities between two equal-gradient samples:
their value difference is exactly the inner product with that gradient.
In particular each physical step within a sector pays its own
h_i||g_i||^2 decrease, even when h_i is huge, unordered, or zero.

For sufficiency, a residual between two sector points is affine in
each of the two masses separately (in fact independent of the source
mass after cancellation). Its values throughout their rectangle are
convex combinations of its endpoint values. Residuals within one
sector are zero. Residuals against the optimum are affine in the one
mass. Thus the seven-jet tests imply EVERY ordered inequality for
every original physical query, both checkpoint jumps, the terminal,
and the optimum. Duplicate endpoints and zero steps cause no exception.

Here is a single globally defined interpolating function, using only
the accepted construction1252b8823bb5a290. For every endpoint i put

    y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_{i in E}{<y_i,v>-delta_i},
    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                              (3.3)

The optimum supplies y_star=delta_star=0. The optimum-to-i row gives
delta_i>=0. The cited accepted fact proves that F is finite everywhere,
convex, C1, globally1-smooth, and interpolates these exact jets. Every
interior-sector conjugate anchor is affine in its mass s, so it lies
pointwise below the maximum of its two endpoint anchors. Hence (3.3)
is EXACTLY the completion retaining ALL original anchors, not a
completion of a smaller unchecked dataset. It interpolates all of
(3.2). Running actual GD is now a finite induction using (3.1)--(3.2),
and its terminal is Z_r-T r with gradient r and value F_r-T||r||^2.

The complete minimum value and set of this explicit function are

    min F=0,
    argmin F=conv{y_i : i in E and delta_i=0}.             (3.4)

Indeed H(0)=0, the conjugate objective has a unique maximizer, and
grad F(x)=0 precisely when x is in partial H(0). The latter is the
convex hull of the slopes of the active affine terms, namely (3.4).
This follows directly by separation: a vector outside that hull has
a direction violating the subgradient inequality for the finite
maximum. Thus the minimum is attained, and 0 is an actual minimizer.
The construction's payoff about this chosen minimizer is exactly
2(F_r-T||r||^2)/||x_0||^2 when x_0!=0. We do NOT assert that 0 is
its closest minimizer. The upper bounds below apply to every minimizer
after translation, and therefore also to its actual closest one.

This is a real original-space lift, with x_0 and the gradients supplied
as real vectors; no unspecified Gram realization is needed. One can
project all points onto span{p,q,r} if desired: all residuals and
function values are unchanged. If the original radius had an
orthogonal component, this only decreases that chosen radius. The
upper argument below already holds in any dimension without using
this projection or claiming it preserves the complete minimum set.

## 4. A mass-selected certificate without height restrictions

Write d=b-1 and e=c_step-1. For this section the following algebra
does not require d,e positive. Let z be a target value such that

    z <= F_r-t||r||^2                                   (4.1)

for a specified real charge t. For Family E, z=f(x_n), t=T, and
(4.1) is equality. Section7 supplies a different paid t for Family Q.
Define

    u=P+1, v=M+1, w=t+1/2,
    A=u+d=P+b, B=v+e=M+c_step,
    Lc=A+v=u+v+d, D=u+v+w+d+e.                          (4.2)

All these names are local certificate scalars, not benchmark masses.
At the last p query and first q query, the original ordered row gives

    F_p >= F_q + P||p||^2 + b<p,q> + ||p-q||^2/2.

At the last q query and first r query, it gives

    F_q >= F_r + M||q||^2 + c_step<q,r> + ||q-r||^2/2.

The original rows from each first-sector query to the chosen zero
optimum give

    F_p <= <p,x_0>-||p||^2/2,
    F_q <= <q,x_0-Ap>-||q||^2/2,
    F_r <= <r,x_0-Ap-Bq>-||r||^2/2.

Combining these with (4.1), without assigning or optimizing ANY
queried height, yields all three inequalities

    z <= <p,x_0>-u||p||^2-v||q||^2-w||r||^2
                    -d<p,q>-e<q,r>,
    z <= <q,x_0>-v||q||^2-w||r||^2-A<p,q>-e<q,r>,
    z <= <r,x_0>-w||r||^2-A<p,r>-B<q,r>.                (4.3)

Their validity includes every previously retained affine face or
remote row: those are additional necessary constraints on an actual
function. We are taking nonnegative combinations of necessary rows,
not asserting that these five rows alone imply interpolation.
The exact lift in Section3 uses ALL rows when a function is built.

Assume D>0, 2D-u>0, 2D-Lc>0, and put

    alpha=A/(2D-u), eta=(Lc+e)/(2D-Lc).                  (4.4)

If 0<=alpha<=eta<=1, combine (4.3) with respective weights
alpha, eta-alpha, 1-eta. Define

    gbar=alpha*p+(eta-alpha)*q+(1-eta)*r,
    s=p-q, tvec=q-r,
    J11=alpha*(u-D*alpha),
    J22=eta*(Lc-D*eta),
    J12=alpha*u*(1-eta)/2.                              (4.5)

The exact weighted quadratic identity is

    z <= <gbar,x_0>-D||gbar||^2
          -J11||s||^2-2J12<s,tvec>-J22||tvec||^2.       (4.6)

For completeness, before subtracting D||gbar||^2 the coefficients
of ||p||^2,||q||^2,||r||^2 are alpha*u, eta*v,w, while the
coefficients of <p,q>,<p,r>,<q,r> are respectively

    alpha*d+(eta-alpha)*A, (1-eta)*A,
    eta*e+(1-eta)*B.

At p=q=r this quadratic is D||r||^2. The p row sum is
[(1+alpha)u+d]/2=D*alpha. The sum of its p and q row sums is
[(1+eta)Lc+e]/2=D*eta. Both equalities follow from (4.4).
Substitute p=r+s+tvec, q=r+tvec. All terms involving r cancel
after subtracting D||gbar||^2; the remaining entries are (4.5).
In particular the mixed entry is
alpha*u/2+eta*A/2-D*alpha*eta=alpha*u*(1-eta)/2.
This proves (4.6) in the full original vector space.

If the real symmetric matrix J with entries (4.5) is PSD, completing
the square gives

    z <= ||x_0||^2/(4D),  2z/||x_0||^2 <= 1/(2D).       (4.7)

If J is positive definite and either p!=q or q!=r, the inequality
is strict. Thus this certificate handles nonparallel q,r, any third
coordinate, nonradial initialization and arbitrary positive heights
at once. It is a closed formula selected by the schedule, not an SDP,
a search over an unspecified feasible set, or a numerical Gram test.

## 5. Exact hierarchy masses and physical checkpoint partitions

For the hierarchy of Section1 set

    X=X_(m-2), A_h=a*c^(m-2), zeta=A_h/X,
    Y=X_(m-1)=2X+A_h, d0=1+a.

Then the original total mass satisfies

    S_m+1=2Y+c*A_h=[4+(2+c)zeta]X.                      (5.1)

All three pair partitions are printed here, with their FULL masses.
Columns d,e are checkpoint excesses, not physical steps.

|pair|P+1|M+1|T+1|d|e|
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
|(j_L,j_O)|X|X|Y|A_h|c*A_h|
|(j_O,j_R)|Y|X|X|c*A_h|A_h|
|(j_L,j_R)|X|2X+c*A_h|X|A_h|A_h|

For example, in the third row block_1 is exactly
(H_(m-2),1+c*A_h,H_(m-2)); its mass is 2X+c*A_h-1.
No middle root or last leaf is removed. The other rows consist of
the actual adjacent children from the defining concatenations.

The following rational enclosure is proved symbolically:

    12/5<c<29/12,  2/5<a<zeta<c-2<5/12,
    X>=2^(m-2)>=2048,  1<d0<3/2.                        (5.2)

At l=0 the strict a<zeta need not hold, but here l=m-2>0, so it does.
To check (5.2), sqrt(2)>7/5 and sqrt(2)<99/70, by squaring positive
rationals. Thus c>12/5 and
c<(169/70)*(6145/6144)<29/12; the last comparison reduces to
2028<2*6144. Also beta>=6143/6144>99/100, so
a>(99/100)*(12/29)=297/725>2/5. The identity
lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and beta<1 show a<c-2. The mass recurrence is

    X_(l+1)=2X_l+a*c^l, X_0=1.

Its exact closed form, checked by substitution, is

    X_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l, v_h=a/(c-2) in (0,1).

Therefore a<=a*c^l/X_l<c-2 for l>0, and X_l>=2^l. The stated
bounds on m give X>=2048. Finally a<5/12<1/2 gives d0<3/2.
This is a symbolic identity and inequalities, not an executed recurrence.

For either of the two terminal charges used below, write

    w/X=(T+1)/X-delta,
    D/X=4+(2+c)zeta-delta,
    0<=delta<1/1000.                                   (5.3)

For Family E, delta=1/(2X). For Family Q, Section7 gives
delta=(1+d0^2)/(2X)<2/X<=1/1024<1/1000. Thus both are covered
by exactly the same rational analysis, with the ACTUAL last entry paid.
In particular

    23/4 < D/X < 117/20.                                (5.4)

For the lower bound use 4+(2+12/5)*(2/5)-1/1000>23/4.
For the upper bound use 4+(2+29/12)*(5/12)=841/144<117/20.

## 6. Complete positive-definiteness proof over this whole domain

Homogeneity permits dividing u,v,w,d,e,D and J by X in this section.
All subsequent bounds hold for EVERY c,zeta,delta in (5.2)--(5.3),
so they do not rely on sampling m or individual parameters.

Define L0=2+zeta and E0=c*zeta. There are two useful weight types:

    alpha0=(1+zeta)/(2D-1),
    eta0=(L0+E0)/(3L0+2E0-2delta),
    eta1=[3+(2+c)zeta]/[5+(3+c)zeta-2delta].              (6.1)

Here D denotes D/X ONLY in this normalized section. All denominators
are positive by (5.2)--(5.4). The bounds are

    13/100 < alpha0 < 27/200,
    9/25 < eta0 < 37/100 < 3/8,
    33/50 < eta1 < 67/100.                              (6.2)

For alpha0, use
alpha0>(7/5)/(117/10-1)=14/107>13/100 and
alpha0<(17/12)/(23/2-1)=17/126<27/200.

For eta0>9/25, cross multiplication reduces to
7E0+18delta>2L0. Its left side is at least 7*(24/25), while
2L0<=29/6, proving the claim. For eta0<37/100 it suffices that
26E0+74delta<11L0. After substituting L0 and E0, its only nonconstant
left side is (26c-11)zeta+74delta, which is less than
1555/72+74/1000<22. Hence this upper bound holds throughout the domain.

For eta1>33/50 the required inequality is
(1+17c)zeta+66delta>15. Its left side exceeds
(1+17*(12/5))*(2/5)=418/25>15. For eta1<67/100 it is enough that
(33c-1)zeta+134delta<35. The left side is less than
525/16+134/1000<35. This proves (6.2), with no evaluation of a schedule.

For each pair, the weight choices (alpha,eta) from (4.4) are as follows:

|pair|u|Lc|alpha|eta|
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
|(j_L,j_O)|1|2+zeta|alpha0|eta0|
|(j_O,j_R)|2+zeta|3+(1+c)zeta|eta0|eta1|
|(j_L,j_R)|1|3+(1+c)zeta|alpha0|eta1|

The second-row equality alpha=eta0 follows by expanding 2D-u;
the third-row middle mass was retained in Section5. In all rows
0<alpha<eta<1, so every certificate multiplier in (4.3) is nonnegative.

First pair. Using eta<3/8, (5.4) and (6.2), the entries in (4.5) obey

    J11 >= (13/100)*(1-(117/20)*(27/200)) > 27/1000,
    J22 >= (9/25)*(12/5-(117/20)*(3/8)) > 37/500,
    0<=J12 <= (27/200)*(1-9/25)/2=27/625<11/250.

All factors being bounded below are positive. Thus

    det J > (27/1000)*(37/500)-(11/250)^2
          =31/500000>0.                                 (6.3)

Second pair. Here Lc>=3+(1+12/5)*(2/5)=109/25, and

    J11 >= (9/25)*(12/5-(117/20)*(37/100)) >21/250,
    J22 >= (33/50)*(109/25-(117/20)*(67/100)) >29/100,
    0<=J12 <= (37/100)*(29/12)*(17/50)/2 <153/1000.

It follows that

    det J > (21/250)*(29/100)-(153/1000)^2
          =951/1000000>0.                               (6.4)

Third pair. The first-pair J11 bound and second-pair J22 bound apply.
Moreover J12< (27/200)*(17/50)/2=459/20000<23/1000. Therefore

    det J > (27/1000)*(29/100)-(23/1000)^2
          =7301/1000000>0.                              (6.5)

Positive first diagonal entry and positive determinant prove positive
definiteness of each real2x2 matrix. Restoring X multiplies J by X and
its determinant by X^2. The proof holds for all real gradient vectors
and their actual PSD Gram, without imposing any geometric pattern.

For Family E, t=T and 2D=1+2S_m. Equations(4.6)--(4.7) and (6.3)--(6.5)
give the upper half of (1.2). If either gradient change is nonzero,
this upper bound is strict. Positive queried heights have never been
assumed away or treated as free resource: they canceled only through
the five explicitly used original inequalities.

## 7. All legal prefix completions and the actual last leaf

Now require only the queried gradients (1.1), allowing an arbitrary
terminal gradient and any static function consistent with the complete
prefix. No completion is being proposed as a new lower construction;
we prove an upper bound for EVERY such completion using its actual
last jet. It is unnecessary to select a conjugate active face or to
solve for an unspecified terminal Gram.

The last physical step is d0=1+a. The suffix has at least one query,
and its last query is at Z_r-(T-d0)r with value

    f(x_(n-1))=F_r-(T-d0)||r||^2.

This equality follows from all original equal-gradient samples. The
actual next point is x_n=x_(n-1)-d0*r=Z_r-T*r. Smoothness along precisely
this original step, by integrating the Lipschitz gradient, gives

    f(x_n) <= f(x_(n-1))-d0||r||^2+d0^2||r||^2/2
             =F_r-(T-d0^2/2)||r||^2.                    (7.1)

Consequently (4.1) holds for z=f(x_n), t=T-d0^2/2. This is a charged
inequality, NOT an alteration of the physical tail mass or a reset.
All earlier positions, queried values, and every minimum remain those
of the one actual function being bounded. The proof in Section6
applies with delta=(1+d0^2)/(2X), and now

    2D=1+2S_m-d0^2>0.

It proves the upper bound in (1.3). Because the argument began at ANY
chosen actual minimizer, moving to the actual closest one does not
evade it. Completing an old prefix can change the complete minimum
set, but applying these inequalities to that very completed function
and its new closest minimizer already pays that change. There is no
assumption that a radius from a different function is inherited.

All original remote/interior/optimum rows remain valid for each
function in this quantification. They are not deleted from a primal
optimization to assert existence. The proof uses a necessary subset
to obtain a bound on the larger family, then pays the original last
leaf by (7.1). This also covers arbitrary affine conjugate offsets
and all genuine3D nonparallel terminal choices compatible with the
full prefix.

## 8. Explicit sharp member and exact original-B comparison

Let S=S_m, kappa=1/(1+2S), and define the scalar function

    f_H(x)=x^2/2                         if |x|<=kappa,
           kappa*|x|-kappa^2/2           if |x|>=kappa.

Start at x_0=1. Its unique minimizer is0, its derivative is continuous,
nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and it is one fixed global function.
At actual cumulative mass s<=S,

    x=1-kappa*s >=1-kappa*S>kappa,
    grad f_H(x)=kappa,
    f_H(x)=kappa*(1-kappa*s)-kappa^2/2.

Thus every actual physical entry, including the actual last leaf, is
executed with the same gradient. This is a member of all three pair
families with p=q=r=kappa. Its queried proximal heights are

    delta(s)=f_H(x)-kappa^2/2=kappa-kappa^2*(s+1)>0,

since S>0 and s<=S<2S. Its OWN closest radius is exactly1 and

    2f_H(x_n)=2kappa-(2S+1)kappa^2=1/(1+2S).

This proves equality in (1.2) and the lower half of (1.3). Nonparallel
gradients cannot attain equality in Family E by positive definiteness,
although we do not need to decide their restricted supremum.

The exact original benchmark is retained throughout:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

No dyadic-only recurrence is substituted for these all-integer-split
maxima. Fact5fd396eba75651e7 gives exactly

    B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250,

at our SAME tau=1/4 and m>=1536. That proves the first inequality in
(1.4). Put Z=1+2S_m. Equations(5.2),(5.4), with the Family E charge,
give Z>(23/2)X>=23552. Since d0^2<9/4, we have
d0^2/Z<1/1000. Hence, for Family Q,

    B_n V <= B_n/(Z-d0^2)
           < (237/250)*(1000/999)=316/333<19/20.

This is a finite, uniform, quantitatively separated statement at every
declared m, at the ACTUAL horizon n=2^m-1. It is a witness-family
obstruction. It is neither a certificate for unrestricted W_n(h) nor
a negative resolution of the original minimax problem.

## 9. What the original-space lift does and does not buy

The seven-endpoint lift shows that one can safely reason in finitely
many real vectors and paid scalar heights for this three-sector model.
It retains all intermediate samples and gives a genuine global smooth
convex interpolant, attained minimum, exact terminal and complete
minimum hull. A3x3 PSD gradient Gram alone cannot replace its endpoint
interpolation tests or the x_0 inner products.

The certificate then proves a bound over the WHOLE declared geometry,
not just over one explicit feasible parameter law. This is why no
new numerical or symbolic optimizer is needed. In particular, adding
a third coordinate, freeing the angle of r relative to q, introducing
independent affine offsets, or choosing x_0 nonradially cannot repair
the canonical top-two choice while the actual gradients remain in
the three sectors (1.1). The same applies to the other two pairs
printed in Section5 and to arbitrary completions of their entire
queried prefixes, even after paying their new closest minima.

Nothing here declares all real three-gradient geometry exhausted on
ALL possible checkpoint pairs. It is also not an obstruction to a
projection hull whose actual gradients move along an edge inside one
of the displayed blocks: such a trajectory violates (1.1).

## 10. Concrete failure of the certificate on another actual pair

To test a fragile all-pair extrapolation, choose the whole-word root
as the first checkpoint and the immediately following physical leaf
as the second. These are actual positions j=2^(m-1)-1 and k=j+1.
This pair is outside Pairs_top for m>=3. Write U=X_(m-1) and
Droot=a*c^(m-1). Its full masses are

    u=U, v=1, d=Droot, e=a,
    T=U-2-a, w=U-3/2-a,
    D=2U+Droot-1/2.                                    (10.1)

Here the middle block is empty, and the suffix is the full right child
after its ACTUAL first leaf. Formula(10.1) keeps all of that suffix.
As m tends to infinity in the SAME-enclosing-m tau1/4 family,

    Droot/U -> lambda-2=:t0, U->infinity,
    (u,v,d,e,w,D)/U -> (1,0,t0,0,1,lambda).

This follows from the exact mass formula in Section5: c->lambda,
a->lambda-2, v_h->1 and (2/c)^(m-1)->0, since c>lambda>2.
All denominators in (4.4) have positive limits. The weights tend to

    alpha -> (lambda-1)/(2lambda-1),
    eta -> (lambda-1)/(lambda+1)=lambda-2=t0.

Substitute these limits in (4.5), using lambda^2=2lambda+1.
The normalized limiting matrix entries are

    J11/U -> (lambda-1)*t0/(2lambda-1)^2,
    J22/U -> t0^2,
    J12/U -> t0/(2lambda-1).

Its determinant is exactly

    lim det(J)/U^2 = -t0^3/(2lambda-1)^2 <0.              (10.2)

For example, the last simplification uses
(lambda-1)t0=3-lambda. Thus the matrix is indefinite for all sufficiently
large m in this very physical hierarchy. This is a rigorous failure
of THIS PARTICULAR five-row certificate, not a construction of legal
data violating Huber and not a claim about the optimum at that pair.
Additional remote rows may repair the proof. A claimed all-pair
extension of Section6 without such work would be false.

This test was textual and symbolic; no determinant evaluation or
trajectory replay was executed. It pinpoints the remaining degree
of freedom: a materially different checkpoint selection, potentially
with essential remote-row information, remains open even within the
three-gradient program. More gradient sectors or a nonconstant
gradient inside a block are also outside the present obstruction.

## 11. Retrieval, resources and remaining theorem-level gap

Applied skills: query-memory for prior state and exact scope;
obtain-immediate-conclusions for block-value equalities and mass
normalization; direct-proving for the explicit weighted certificate;
search-math-results for interpolation/conjugacy background; and
construct-counterexamples for the failed all-pair extension in Section10.

Matlas was queried for smooth convex interpolation and constructive
strongly convex conjugate envelopes. Its returned two lemmas did not
supply this block certificate. Web retrieval identified the primary
paper by Adrien B. Taylor, Julien M. Hendrickx and Francois Glineur,
"Smooth Strongly Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance
of First-order Methods", arXiv:1502.05666. The exact own local text
downloads/multistage_envelope_completion_20261002/1502.05666v6.txt was
read for its convex interpolation construction and conjugation
definitions/proof context. This reference is background only; every
interpolation premise used here is already the accepted fact1252.
No new external theorem is imported as a correctness premise.

All new conclusions above are asserted jointly and remain UNVERIFIED.
No intermediate draft was promoted or treated as a fact. The original
all-word/all-horizon comparison against the exact B_n remains a
theorem-level gap. The current result supplies no legal new witness
beating Huber on this hierarchy, no improving full-function upper
schedule, and no answer to the original problem.

The authorized deliverable is the complete exact obstruction (1.2)
and uniform full-prefix bound (1.3) on the precisely declared broader
family, together with the exact escape boundary (10.2). No automatic
renewal, outward publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown or
Goal completion is requested or performed.

# Complete hexagonal envelope and a nonlocal triangle switch budget

JOINT UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, invocation77 after ended baseline76.
Project gd-exact-profile-20260926. Guidance5db5ffb3f0b50444.
One invocation; at most two analytical mechanisms; no formal submissions.
All new mathematical arguments form ONE joint candidate. No new fact_id.
Original unrestricted minimax target OPEN.

## Verdict

Mechanism1 derives the EXACT one-step hexagonal image for every R>=0 and
h>1, including both kinds of short original steps. It gives explicit same-m
whole-H_j bounds and an exact complete-word scalar loss. At R=S_j+1, the
scalar composition is S_j/2+1. Combining this with the sharp separate
Euclidean and terminal-objective conversions gives1/(S_j+1), whose ratio to
1/(1+2S_j) tends to2. It exceeds17/(16(1+2S_j)) at every j>=1.
This is a failure of the scalar relaxation, NOT an actual terminal example
violating the triangle bound. One actual fixed-edge trajectory attains every
one-step scalar image on this path, but it does NOT attain the independently
maximized terminal objective. The lost orientation is identified explicitly.
Unbounded initial radius does not force a fixed face; an actual first-leaf
vertex-to-edge switch at arbitrarily large radius is displayed.

Only after this decisive failure, Mechanism2 tests the explicit remaining-mass
potential U_d(x)=max_(g in D)(2<x,g>-d||g||^2), d>=1. It supplies a storage
containing longitudinal position, mass and transverse energy. A direct
one-step inequality is proved for every h<=3/2, and its precise extension
condition is given. Long bridges cannot be handled one step at a time.
Most concretely, the previous strict vertex-switch counterexample is now
paid over its ENTIRE actual H_3=(H_2,1+beta*c,H_2): for every d>=1 its
end/start U ratio is less than7/64. An explicit signed-charge inequality
uses the real initial reserve and the actual outgoing state. This includes
both complete children, the true bridge, and the last leaf of that block.
It is an all-m calibration family, NOT an all-start/all-depth theorem.

The universal U_d block inequality remains unproved. Neither a finite image
composition nor the signed-charge telescope is presented as a solution.
There is no full triangle bound, full-function W upper bound, or arbitrary-N
simplex claim. No original problem quantifier has been changed.

## 1. Frozen word, function, and original scope

Fix an integer m>=1536 once and for all. Define

  epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
  c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
  H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
  n=2^m-1, h_i=(H_m)_i, S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1)h_i, D_m=1+2S.

The same m and parameters are used in every child. In particular the final
physical step is1+a. No child is treated as a separately parameterized word.

Take the three fixed real vertices

  v_1=(1,0), v_2=(-1/2,sqrt(3)/2), v_3=(-1/2,-sqrt(3)/2),
  D=conv{v_1,v_2,v_3},
  F(x)=max_(g in D)[<x,g>-||g||^2/2], x in R^2,
  T_h(x)=x-h P_D(x), x_(i+1)=T_(h_i)(x_i),
  Psi(x)=2F(x), E_m(x_0)=Psi(x_n)/||x_0||^2,
  V_triangle(m)=sup_(x_0!=0) E_m(x_0).

All inner products and norms are Euclidean. P_D denotes Euclidean projection
onto this one fixed triangle. The original W_n(h) is the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite-dimensional convex
L-smooth functions with a minimizer, under steps h_i/L. Hence W=2R, and
V_triangle is a restricted-family quantity at the specified word. A triangle
upper bound, even if proved, would not be an upper bound on W.

Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 gives the all-split benchmark
ratio B_(2^m-1)/D_m<47/50. Its799/800 consequence requires the UNPROVED full
function-class bound W_(2^m-1)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m). No such implication is
invoked here. Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the ordered interpolation
convention and the need to retain the actual terminal; the legality needed
here is also derived directly in Section2. Previous unverified reports are
awareness only, not premises of this joint candidate.

## 2. Static legality and exact original interpolation rows

The vertices satisfy ||v_i||=1, sum_i v_i=0, <v_i,v_j>=-1/2 for i!=j, and
sum_i v_i v_i^T=(3/2)I. Thus

  D={x:<x,v_i>>=-1/2 for all i},
  ball(0,1/2) subset D subset ball(0,1).

Completing the square in F identifies its unique maximizer as g=P_D(x).
The defining necessary and sufficient projection inequality is

  <x-g,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                         (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along a segment toward
z; sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. Adding (2.1) at two
inputs x,y gives ||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2 <= <P_D(x)-P_D(y),x-y>, and then the
Cauchy--Schwarz inequality proves1-Lipschitzness. The maximizing inequalities
bound F(x+t)-F(x)-<P_D(x),t> between0 and ||t||^2. This proves that F is
differentiable with gradient P_D. F is finite and convex because D is compact
and F is a maximum of affine functions of x.

F(0)=0 and F>=0. For x!=0 choose0<r<min(1,1/(2||x||)); then rx belongs to
D, and F(x)>=(r-r^2/2)||x||^2>0. The complete minimum set is{0}. Consequently
||x_0|| is the actual distance to the closest minimizer.

For every original query and terminal i=0,...,n, put g_i=P_D(x_i),f_i=F(x_i),
and include x_*=g_*=f_*=0. For EVERY ordered pair,

  Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
      =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.                            (2.2)

The inequality is (2.1) at j with z=g_i. Thus incoming/outgoing terminal,
remote, reversed, and optimum rows all hold for these actual samples.
Multiplying F by L and using steps h_i/L preserves the points and W score.

## 3. Ordered gaps, metric, values, and face guards

Define labeled coordinates q_i=(2/3)<x,v_i>. Their sum is0, x=sum_i q_i v_i,
and ||x||^2=(3/2)sum_i q_i^2. At the current point relabel only for notation
so that q_1>=q_2>=q_3. A relabeling means a triangle symmetry, not a change
of the physical function or a replacement of the actual state.

Set

  u=q_1-q_2>=0, v=q_2-q_3>=0,
  (q_1,q_2,q_3)=((2u+v)/3,(v-u)/3,-(u+2v)/3),
  A=(2u+v)/3, B=(u+2v)/3,
  Q(u,v)=||x||^2=u^2+uv+v^2.                            (3.1)

Thus the assigned sorted triple(A,B-A,-B) is recovered, and A/2<=B<=2A.
The two gaps contain all the actual planar state up to a triangle symmetry.

The projection regions in this order are

  C: u+2v<=1;
  V: u>=1;
  E: 0<=u<=1 and u+2v>=1.                              (3.2)

Their boundaries overlap and the formulas agree there. With r_i=(2/3)<g,v_i>
for g=P_D(x), the respective projection coordinates are

  C: r=q;
  V: r=(2/3,-1/3,-1/3);
  E: r=(1/6+u/2,1/6-u/2,-1/3).                         (3.3)

For C, membership in D is exactly q_i>=-1/3. For V, the projection normal
inequalities at v_1 are q_1-q_2>=1 and q_1-q_3>=1; ordering reduces these
to u>=1. For E, the point in (3.3) lies on the edge opposite v_3 precisely
when u<=1. Its residual q-r is a nonnegative multiple of(1,1,-2) precisely
when u+2v>=1; this is the outward edge normal, proving (2.1). These cases
cover every u,v>=0. This derives (3.2)--(3.3) without an external theorem.

The actual objective is

  Psi_C=Q(u,v),
  Psi_V=2u+v-1,
  Psi_E=(3u^2+2u+4v-1)/4.                              (3.4)

For the vertex chart set s=<x,v_1>=u+v/2 and let t be the signed coordinate
perpendicular to v_1. Then t^2=3v^2/4, Q=s^2+t^2, Psi_V=2s-1.
For the edge chart let n_E=-v_3, choose a unit edge tangent t_E, and write
z=<x,n_E>=u/2+v, y=<x,t_E>. Then y^2=3u^2/4, Q=z^2+y^2 and
Psi_E=z-1/4+y^2. In particular both charts retain their full transverse energy.

## 4. Every exact physical update and every reordering

All original steps have h>1. Put k=h-1>0. In the following table an ordering
such as213 gives the identities of the old coordinates in new descending
order. The displayed output is the NEW ordered gap pair(U,V).

CORE, u+2v<=1:

  order321: (U,V)=(k v,k u).                             (4.1)

This follows from q'=(1-h)q=-kq; it includes ties.

VERTEX, u>=1:

  h<=u:       order123, (U,V)=(u-h,v);
  u<=h<=u+v:  order213, (U,V)=(h-u,u+v-h);
  h>=u+v:     order231, (U,V)=(v,h-u-v).                 (4.2)

Indeed the labeled adjacent differences after the actual step are u-h and v.
The sign of u-h and then of u+v-h gives exactly these three orders. The last
two old coordinates remain ordered because their gradient coordinates agree.

EDGE, 0<=u<=1 and u+2v>=1: define w=v-h(1-u)/2. Then

  w>=k u:     order213, (U,V)=(k u,w-k u);
  0<=w<=k u:  order231, (U,V)=(w,k u-w);
  w<=0:       order321, (U,V)=(-w,k u).                 (4.3)

Here the labeled adjacent differences are -k u and w. Thus the old second
coordinate is never below the old first; the signs of w and w-k u give the
complete list. No order-persistence assumption enters (4.1)--(4.3).

For each output use precisely these next-query guards:

  next C iff U+2V<=1;
  next V iff U>=1;
  next E iff U<=1 and U+2V>=1.                          (4.4)

Inside a row of (4.1)--(4.3), every boundary in (4.4) is affine in u,v.
Together with u,v>=0, the current face boundaries u+2v=1,u=1 and order
boundaries h=u,h=u+v,w=0,w=k u, this retains all guards. The A,B update is
A'=(2U+V)/3,B'=(U+2V)/3, so the table is also an explicit map in A,B.

The formulas agree on shared boundaries. At u+2v=1, w=-k v and the edge
row becomes(k v,k u), the core formula. At u=1, w=v and (4.3) becomes the
applicable vertex row. The adjacent rows agree when w=0,w=k u,h=u or h=u+v.
No additional branch is inserted by allowing closed inequalities.

The exact new squared norm is

  core:   k^2 Q(u,v);
  vertex: (u-h)^2+(u-h)v+v^2;
  edge:   k^2 u^2-k u w+w^2.                            (4.5)

These are permutation-invariant norm expressions for the raw differences.
In particular the edge cross term has its displayed MINUS sign.


## 5. Exact hexagonal image at every radius and every original step

Define G(x)=max_i|<x,v_i>| and C_R={x:G(x)<=R}. In the ordered chamber,

  G=max(2u+v,u+2v)/2,
  C_R={u,v>=0:2u+v<=2R, u+2v<=2R}.

Let k=h-1>0 and M_h(R)=max_(x in C_R)G(T_h x). The exact formula is:

If h>=2,

  M_h(R)=kR,                                      0<=R<=1;
  M_h(R)=max{k,R-h/2,(R+h-3/2)/2},                 R>=1.       (5.1)

If 1<h<2,

  M_h(R)=kR,                                      0<=R<=1/2;
  M_h(R)=max{k min(R,1),R-h/2,h(2R-1)/4},          1/2<=R<=3/2;
  M_h(R)=max{k,R-h/2,(R+h-3/2)/2},                 R>=3/2.     (5.2)

Endpoints agree. In particular the actual leaf has h=1+a<3/2, the level1
bridge has h=1+beta<2, and every higher bridge has h>=2. No leaf is replaced
by an artificial h=2 step. These formulas are nondecreasing in R.

Proof. In the core the image is -kx; its maximum G is k min(R,1).
For R<=1/2 the entire hexagon lies in D, so this already proves (5.2)'s
first part. For R>=1/2, in the edge chart the labeled post-step dots are

  s'_1=v/2+(1-3h/4)u-h/4,
  s'_2=v/2+(3h/4-1/2)u-h/4,
  s'_3=-(u+2v)/2+h/2.                              (5.3)

The feasible interval and ceiling for its ordered coordinates are

  0<=u<=U_R:=min(1,(4R-1)/3),
  (1-u)/2<=v<=min(2R-2u,R-u/2).                    (5.4)

The ceiling changes at u=2R/3. The negative parts of s'_1 and s'_2 are
at most k min(R,1), since their values on the lower edge boundary are
-k(1+3u)/4 and k(3u-1)/4 and u<=U_R. Also s'_1<=s'_2. The third coordinate
contributes at most max(k/2,R-h/2); k/2 is covered by the core term.
At the upper ceiling, s'_2 equals

  R/2-h/4+3ku/4,                 u<=2R/3;
  R-h/4+3(h-2)u/4,              u>=2R/3.           (5.5)

For h<2 these slopes have opposite signs. The maximum is at u=2R/3
when R<=3/2 and at u=1 when R>=3/2, giving respectively h(2R-1)/4 and
(R+h-3/2)/2. For h>=2 the maximum is at U_R. For R<=1 it is k(R-1/2),
dominated by kR; for 1<=R<=3/2 it is R+h/2-3/2, dominated by k; for
R>=3/2 it is (R+h-3/2)/2.

There is no vertex region for R<1. For R>=1 its post-step dots are

  (2u+v)/2-h, (v-u+h)/2, -(u+2v)/2+h/2, u>=1.

The first and third absolute values are bounded by max(k,R-h/2).
The only further candidate, the positive second dot, has maximum
R+h/2-3/2 for 1<=R<=3/2 and (R+h-3/2)/2 for R>=3/2. The former is at
most k when h>=2. When h<2 it is at most h(2R-1)/4, because their
difference is (2-h)(3/4-R/2)>=0. Negative second dots are covered by the
first-coordinate positive bound. This proves all claimed upper bounds.

For attainment, the core term is attained by x=min(R,1)v_1. A maximizing
term R-h/2 is nonnegative and is attained at the edge-axis point with
u=0,v=R. In the middle part of (5.2), the edge term is attained at
u=v=2R/3, which satisfies (5.4). In the outer part, the mixed term is
attained at u=1,v=R-1/2. For (5.1), if this outer term is strictly larger
than k then R>h-1/2>=3/2, so this last point is allowed. If it ties k,
the core point suffices. All proposed maximizing cases are thus realized.

## 6. Paid norm and objective conversions

For every actual x,

  G(x)^2<=||x||^2<=4G(x)^2/3.                      (6.1)

For example, in the chamber u>=v, the difference
4G^2/3-Q=(u-v)(u+2v)/3 is nonnegative; in the other half chamber
interchange u,v. The lower bound follows because each v_i is a unit
vector. Both constants are attained: on a vertex axis and at u=v.

The always valid objective estimate is Psi(x)<=||x||^2<=4G(x)^2/3.
For every R>=3/2 the SHARP objective maximum on C_R is

  max_(G(x)<=R)Psi(x)=2R-1.                        (6.2)

Indeed in a vertex chart Psi<=2R-1. In an edge chart Psi=z-1/4+y^2
with z<=R and y^2<=3/4, so Psi<=R+1/2<=2R-1. In the core Psi<=1,
also below 2R-1. The point Rv_1 attains equality. Notice that the
edge-axis point -Rv_1 instead has Psi=R-1/4. This distinction survives
even for arbitrarily large R; a gauge radius is not an objective value.

## 7. Explicit same-m complete-H_j envelope estimates

Write E_j(R) for the chronological composition of the EXACT M_h maps
over H_j, with E_0(R)=R. It is only a scalar enclosing radius. From
monotonicity, every actual run satisfies G(T_(H_j)x)<=E_j(G(x)), where
T_(H_j) denotes the complete actual composition on the one fixed F.
It does not follow that E_j is the true image of the whole block.

Put S_j=sum H_j and eta=a/(c-2). Directly from the literal word,

  1+S_j=(1-eta)2^j+eta c^j,    0<eta<1.            (7.1)

For clarity, A_j=1+S_j obeys A_(j+1)=2A_j+a c^j; substitution proves
(7.1), starting at A_0=1. We use the same enclosing m throughout.
Useful coarse rational bounds are

  2/5<a<5/12, 99/100<beta<1, 12/5<c<5/2,
  12/5<beta*c<5/2, 1-eta<8epsilon.                 (7.2)

Here lambda>241/100 and lambda<29/12 follow by squaring their positive
sqrt(2) comparisons. Because epsilon<=1/6144, c<49/20 and beta>99/100,
so a>99/245>2/5; a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1<5/12. Also beta*c=lambda(1-epsilon^2)
>(241/100)(999/1000)>12/5 and is below lambda<5/2. For the last inequality,
c(c-2)<1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2 and
(1-8epsilon)(1+7epsilon+6epsilon^2)<1-epsilon give eta>1-8epsilon.

The exact ruler prefix products are

  P_ell=product_(r=1)^ell(h_(r-1)-1)
       =beta^ell c^(-sbit(ell)),                   (7.3)

where sbit(ell) is the number of binary ones, and P_0=1. The ruler entry
is beta*c^(nu_2(r)-1); counting each power-of-two divisor of the integers
1,...,ell gives sum nu_2(r)=ell-sbit(ell), proving (7.3).
In particular P_ell<=a<1 for every nonempty prefix and
P_block,j:=product_(h in H_j)(h-1)=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j).

The following are quantitative complete-word statements, not unevaluated
max-plus expressions:

  E_j(R)=P_block,j R,                                  0<=R<=1;
  E_j(R)<=max(R,a c^(j-1)),                       R>=0,j>=1;
  E_j(R)<=max(R-S_j/2,C_hex,j),                       R>=0,j>=1;
  C_hex,j=(3a c^(j-1)-S_(j-1))/2.                    (7.4)

For the first line, formulas (5.1)--(5.2) give M_h(r)=(h-1)r for
0<=r<=1 for EVERY original step. For the two short steps this follows
from k>=a>2/5: on 1/2<=r<=1 both r-h/2 and h(2r-1)/4 are at most kr.
Every original prefix product is at most1, so no intermediate scalar
radius leaves this interval. This proves the whole-block equality.

The other two lines follow from the pointwise bounds

  M_h(R)<=max(R,h-1),
  M_h(R)<=max(3(h-1)/2,R-h/2),                    (7.5)

valid for every h>1 and R>=0. To check the second, the mixed outer branch
is at most3k/2 when R<=2h-3/2 and otherwise at most R-h/2. The short
middle branch is at most the outer mixed branch, since their difference
is k(R-3/2)/2<=0; the core term is at most k. The first bound follows
directly from (5.1)--(5.2): the mixed outer branch is an average of R and
k-1/2, and the short middle branch is at most R. The largest k in H_j
is a c^(j-1), proving the second line of (7.4).

Composing the second max in (7.5) by distributivity gives the upper bound
max{R-S_j/2, max_i[3(h_i-1)/2-(mass strictly after i)/2]}.
An occurrence of a level-l bridge is followed by its complete H_l right
child, so its latter expression is at most (3a c^l-S_l)/2. Equality in
this purely affine-max comparison is possible at the last occurrence of
that level, whose suffix is exactly H_l. There is no reset of parameters.
Define T_l=(3a c^l-S_l)/2. Formula (7.1) gives

  T_l=[1+(3a-eta)c^l-(1-eta)2^l]/2.

These T_l strictly increase. Indeed 3a-eta=a[3-1/(c-2)]>a/2>1/5,
and hence (3a-eta)(c-1)>7/25>1-eta by (7.2). Also c^l>=2^l.
Subtracting adjacent displayed formulas proves T_(l+1)>T_l. The maximum
is consequently T_(j-1)=C_hex,j, proving the final explicit upper bound.
It includes every branch, but C_hex,j grows; it is not a sharp convergence
theorem and is not substituted for the desired bound.

## 8. Exact complete-word failure of the scalar relaxation

For every j>=1 choose the scalar initial radius R_0=S_j+1. At every actual
position, let A_i be the original mass already executed. Then

  R_i=S_j+1-A_i/2,
  M_(h_i)(R_i)=R_i-h_i/2,
  E_j(S_j+1)=S_j/2+1.                             (8.1)

This is a symbolic proof for every j, not an executed recurrence. Here is
why EVERY branch choice is valid. Let Hmax=1+a c^(j-1). From (7.1),

  S_j> [c/(c-2)](Hmax-1)-1 >5Hmax-6>4Hmax-5.

Thus R_i>=S_j/2+1>2Hmax-3/2>=2h_i-3/2. Also R_i>3/2 since S_j>1.
In the outer formulas (5.1)--(5.2), R-h/2 dominates the mixed branch
exactly when R>=2h-3/2. It also dominates k there, since h>1. This proves
the induction in (8.1), including the real last leaf.

Combining the separately sharp conversions (6.1)--(6.2) with this exact
scalar path gives the certificate value

  [2E_j(R_0)-1]/R_0^2=1/(S_j+1).                  (8.2)

Its ratio to the baseline1/(1+2S_j) is
(1+2S_j)/(1+S_j)=2-1/(1+S_j). It exceeds17/16 because S_j>1>1/15.
For j=m it tends to2 as m tends to infinity, since S_m>=2^m-1.
This is a precise, unavoidable loss for the relaxation that keeps only the
scalar radius, uses these exact one-step images, and applies the sharp
separate initial-norm and terminal-objective conversions. Any weaker
one-step bounds or weaker separate conversions cannot repair that loss.
It does NOT exclude a bound retaining an objective or directional state.

The incompatibility is concrete. Start the ACTUAL fixed F at
x_0=-(S_j+1)v_1. Every point is on the same physical edge-normal ray with
z_i=S_j+1-A_i/2>1/2 and y_i=0. Its actual gauge is exactly R_i, so ONE
legal trajectory attains all the scalar image radii in (8.1). But its
actual terminal value is S_j/2+3/4, not S_j+1. Its terminal point is on
an edge axis, whereas the maximizer of (6.2) is on a vertex axis. The
initial Euclidean bound is already exact on this edge ray. No orientation
switch can be inserted at the terminal of this static trajectory.

Alternatively, the actual start +(S_j+1)v_1 stays in the same vertex
through the terminal and finishes at v_1 with Psi=1. Neither of these
legal starts realizes (8.2) as its normalized objective. No full-word
triangle counterexample is claimed.

## 9. Actual fixed-face criteria, and why large radius is insufficient

For a particular vertex, order the other coordinates so its upper gap is
u_0 and the remaining gap is v_0>=0. The condition

  u_0>=S_m+1                                      (9.1)

is necessary and sufficient for that SAME vertex chart to hold at all
queries AND the terminal. Under this condition the gap at any prefix is
u_0-A_i>=1 and the other gap is unchanged. Conversely, if the chart holds
through the terminal, its affine update gives u_n=u_0-S_m>=1.

For a fixed physical edge, write x=z n_E+y t_E. The condition

  z_0>=1/2+S_m/2, |y_0|<=sqrt(3)/2                 (9.2)

is necessary and sufficient for that edge chart to be valid throughout,
including terminal. Necessity uses z_n=z_0-S_m/2>=1/2 and initial edge
membership. Sufficiency follows from z_i=z_0-A_i/2 and
|y_i|=P_i|y_0|<=|y_0| using (7.3). Boundary choices are harmless.

Both fixed-face classes have normalized payoff at most1/D_m. Indeed
D_m P_block,m^2<1 because (7.1) gives D_m<2c^m and P_block,m=beta^n c^(-m).
In a vertex chart the difference between ||x_0||^2 and D_m Psi(x_n)
is (s_0-D_m)^2+t_0^2. In an edge chart it is
(z_0-D_m/2)^2+(1-D_m P_block,m^2)y_0^2. The equalities at x_0=D_m v_1 and
x_0=-(D_m/2)v_1 are legal by (9.1)--(9.2). Thus the classes actually
attain1/D_m. This is not extended to the face-changing domain.

There is no scalar large-radius criterion for fixed faces. Choose
0<delta<a, u_0=1+delta and v_0=T with T arbitrarily large. This is a
strict vertex query. The first actual leaf1+a gives ordered gaps
U=a-delta in (0,1), V=T-a+delta. For T large enough U+2V>1, so the next
query is strictly on an edge. Meanwhile ||x_0||^2=u_0^2+u_0 T+T^2 tends
to infinity. This family rules out assuming that all large starts have
one face. Criteria (9.1)--(9.2), not radius alone, identify the histories
to which the fixed-face sharpening applies.

## 10. Mechanism2: a remaining-mass potential with longitudinal storage

For d>=1 define the explicit function

  U_d(x)=max_(g in D){2<x,g>-d||g||^2}
        =d Psi(x/d)
        =||x||^2/d-dist(x,dD)^2/d.                 (10.1)

Completing the square proves every identity. In particular U_1=Psi and
0<=U_d(x)<=||x||^2/d. Formula (3.4), applied at x/d, evaluates U_d without
an optimization oracle: in the scaled core it is Q/d; in a scaled vertex
chart it is 2s-d; in a scaled edge chart it is z-d/4+y^2/d. The scaled
guards are respectively u+2v<=d, u>=d, and u<=d<=u+2v. Thus both the
longitudinal coordinate and the transverse square remain explicit.

The concrete universal law tested in this mechanism is

  U_d(T_(H_j)x)<=U_(d+2S_j)(x), all x, d>=1, j<=m.  (10.2)

It is a proposed all-block inequality, NOT a proved theorem. At j=m,d=1
it would imply the baseline triangle estimate. It is stronger than that
estimate because of the extra subtracted distance in (10.1), and because
it quantifies over all trailing masses and all child levels. We do not
describe proving (10.2) as a minor remaining technical task.

## 11. Direct short-step payment and an exact long-step obstruction

There is a useful direct theorem for one step on ANY nonempty compact
convex body D. Let d>=1,h>0, g=P_D(x), q=P_D((x-hg)/d). Then

  U_(d+2h)(x)-U_d(x-hg)
     >= B(d,h)||q-g||^2,
  B(d,h)=h[(2-h)(d+2h-1)-h]/(d+2h-1)^2.           (11.1)

Proof. Put theta=h/(d+2h-1) in (0,1] and z=theta*g+(1-theta)*q in D.
Write x=g+w with <w,q-g><=0, the original projection inequality. Evaluate
the maximum U_(d+2h) at z and put e=q-g. Subtracting U_d(x-hg), whose
maximizer is q, gives

  -2theta<w,e> + [d-(d+2h)(1-theta)^2]||e||^2.

The coefficient of <g,e> has canceled because
theta(d+2h-1)=h. The first term is nonnegative, and the square coefficient
equals B(d,h) after expansion. This proves (11.1) directly, including
boundary projections and without any smoothness or external black box.

For 0<h<=3/2 and d>=1 one has B(d,h)>=0: the numerator bracket is at
least (2-h)2h-h=h(3-2h). Therefore every actual short leaf satisfies

  U_d(T_(1+a)x)<=U_(d+2(1+a))(x).                  (11.2)

For 3/2<h<2 the same proof works if
d>=h/(2-h)-2h+1. This condition is stated, not assumed for every actual
level1 step. In particular it must be checked against the real suffix mass.

The proposed ONE-STEP U payment is false at every long bridge h>2, even
on actual reachable inputs. Choose a nonzero x sufficiently near zero
that x and (1-h)x are in the triangle core. Its scaled versions also lie
in the core. Then

  U_d(T_h x)=(h-1)^2||x||^2/d
             >||x||^2/(d+2h)=U_(d+2h)(x).         (11.3)

At any specified original bridge this x is reachable by taking an
appropriately small nonzero initial point along the same axis: the finite
prefix products are all nonzero, so the initial radius can be chosen to
keep the entire prefix in the core and attain the specified tiny bridge
input. No arbitrary cloud of unrelated states is used in this test.

This failure does NOT refute (10.2), which groups both real children with
their bridge. Nor does the short-step result prove that block law by
iteration. The exact interface is the compensation from BOTH complete
child runs, not an unearned monotonicity across the bridge.

## 12. Joint derivation of forecasts and the block-charge interface

For the actual seven projection charts f define orthogonal projectors and
offsets P_f,b_f by

  core: P_f=I,b_f=0;
  edge: P_f=t_E t_E^T,b_f=n_E/2;
  vertex: P_f=0,b_f=v_j.

Their ranges are orthogonal to their offsets. In the actual chart,
P_D(x)=P_f x+b_f and Psi(x)=||P_f x||^2+2<b_f,x>-||b_f||^2.
These are exactly the cell formulas derived in Sections2--4.

At each original position i, including the terminal, set

  s_i=sum_(t=i)^(n-1)h_t,
  d_i=1+2s_i,
  p_i=product_(t=i)^(n-1)(h_t-1),
  L_i^f(x)=p_i^2||P_f x||^2+2<b_f,x>-d_i||b_f||^2. (12.1)

Empty suffixes have s_n=0,p_n=d_n=1. At the actual update, the projected
coordinate is multiplied by -(h_i-1) and the offset coordinate is shifted
by -h_i b_f. Hence

  L_i^f(x_i)=L_(i+1)^f(x_(i+1))                   (12.2)

when f is the actual present chart; no future chart persistence is used.
Choose the smallest face containing the projection at each actual point,
and let sigma_i be its chart. Define

  Delta_i=L_(i+1)^(sigma_(i+1))(x_(i+1))
                    -L_(i+1)^(sigma_i)(x_(i+1)).       (12.3)

For any COMPLETE chronological block of positions b,...,e-1,

  sum_(i=b)^(e-1)Delta_i=L_e^(sigma_e)(x_e)-L_b^(sigma_b)(x_b). (12.4)

For the full word L_n=Psi, so this is the true terminal identity. A chart
change after the last original leaf is included. The explicit correction
is p_(i+1)^2<x',(P_new-P_old)x'>+2<b_new-b_old,x'>
-d_(i+1)(||b_new||^2-||b_old||^2), with x'=x_(i+1).
In particular mass terms are retained in every change of face dimension.

The proposed new storage is the fully explicit, possibly signed quantity

  Z_i(x)=U_(d_i)(x)-L_i^(sigma_i)(x).                 (12.5)

It is NOT a constant attached to a face, nor a transverse square alone.
In the scaled core, for example, it is

  vertex actual: Q/d_i-2s+d_i;
  edge actual:   Q/d_i-z+d_i/4-p_i^2 y^2;
  core actual:   (1/d_i-p_i^2)Q.

Other cases follow from the displayed scaled chart formulas (10.1).
Some Z_i can be negative; no unproved positivity is imposed at every cut.
Identity (12.4) now gives the exact block-payment interface

  sum_block Delta_i
    =Z_b(x_b)-Z_e(x_e)+U_(d_e)(x_e)-U_(d_b)(x_b).  (12.6)

An actual inequality on the LAST difference would be progress. Merely
rewriting it as (10.2) would not be. Section13 proves such an inequality
on a concrete all-m family containing the old strict vertex-switch test.

For context, the exact full initial reserve, also jointly derived, is

  R_initial=||x_0||^2-D_m L_0^(sigma_0)(x_0)
     =||(I-P_sigma0)x_0-D_m b_sigma0||^2
                  +(1-D_m p_0^2)||P_sigma0 x_0||^2.   (12.7)

Orthogonality and completing the square prove the equality; positivity
follows from D_m p_0^2<1 proved in Section9. The full target would require
sum_i Delta_i<=[R_initial+||x_0||^2/16]/D_m. That equivalent global
comparison remains UNPROVED. The new storage does not establish it by
definition.

## 13. A genuine complete-child payment for the strict switch test

Fix the original same-m parameters and abbreviate

  A=1+a, B=1+beta, K=beta*c, t=(K+2)/4,
  S_2=2A+B, S_3=2S_2+1+K.

The actual H_3 is (A,B,A,1+K,A,B,A), with both H_2 children and all
seven physical updates. Take the initial ordered gaps

  u_0=S_2+K/2, v_0=t.                             (13.1)

This is the previous strict vertex-switch input, initialized by its actual
left child rather than inserted at the bridge. All of the first H_2
queries are strictly in vertex1: the upper gap after any prefix is at
least K/2>1. Thus after the left child the gaps are (K/2,t).

The bridge1+K has h-u=1+K/2=2t and u+v<h. Row (4.2) gives ordered
gaps (t,t), strictly in vertex2 since t>1. At this same post-bridge point,
the old and new longitudinal dot products are -3t/2 and 3t/2. Its
vertex-to-vertex forecast correction is therefore6t>0. The old and new
transverse squares are both3t^2/4, as was the transverse square before
the bridge. No local transverse release pays the positive correction.

Now retain the ENTIRE real right H_2. Define

  r=A-t, s=2t-A,
  w=s-B(1-r)/2=[(3-beta)t-2-(1-beta)a]/2.           (13.2)

The successive ordered gaps after its three physical steps are

  after A: (r,s),        an edge;
  after B: (w,beta*r-w), a core point;
  after A: (a(beta*r-w),a*w), the actual block end.  (13.3)

All guards follow from exact rational bounds, with no numerical replay:
Section7 gives 11/10<t<9/8 and 7/5<A<17/12. Therefore

  11/40<r<19/60<1/3,
  s>47/60>0,
  r+2s=3t-A>113/60>1.

Thus the first post-step state is an edge. For w, using 99/100<beta<1
and a<5/12 gives

  47/480<w<209/1600<1/6,
  beta*r>(99/100)(11/40)>1/4.

Consequently 0<w<beta*r and the middle edge update uses its middle
ordering row. Its next core guard is
w+2(beta*r-w)=2beta*r-w<2r<2/3<1. The last step is therefore the core
update stated in (13.3). This proves the whole physical itinerary.

Let Q_0=||x_0||^2 and Q_7=||x_7||^2. The exact metric gives

  Q_7=a^2[(beta*r)^2-beta*r*w+w^2]
      <=a^2(beta*r)^2<1/16,
  Q_0> S_2^2>16,
  S_3<27/2.                                      (13.4)

For the first bound, 0<w<beta*r implies w^2-beta*r*w<0, while a<1/2
and r<1/3. For the second S_2>4; for the last S_2<5 and K<5/2.
Also ||x_0||<=u_0+v_0<59/8<8 and S_3>11. Hence for every d>=1,
x_0/(d+2S_3) lies in the ball of radius1/2, and so is in D. The final
point x_7/d is in that same core because ||x_7||<1/4. Thus BOTH U
values below are exactly their core expressions, not loose estimates:

  U_d(x_7)/U_(d+2S_3)(x_0)
     =(Q_7/Q_0)(d+2S_3)/d
     <(1/256)(1+2S_3)<7/64.                       (13.5)

This is a strict, quantitative, COMPLETE-H_3 block-payment inequality
for every enclosing m>=1536 and EVERY trailing d>=1. Its family is
(13.1) and its triangle rotations, not all initial points.

Specialize to the actual full-word suffix d=d_7=1+2(S_m-S_3), so d_0=D_m.
Apply (12.6) over the first seven original steps. Equation (13.5) gives

  sum_(i=0)^6 Delta_i
     < Z_0(x_0)-Z_7(x_7)-(57/64)Q_0/D_m.          (13.6)

Here Z_0=R_initial/D_m exactly, because the initial scaled point is in
the core. Moreover Z_7>=0 for this actual suffix. To check the latter,
the block product is beta^7 c^(-3), so
p_7=beta^(n-7)c^(3-m) and
d_7 p_7^2<2c^(6-m)<1, using m>=1536. Since the actual outgoing point is
also in the core, Z_7=(1/d_7-p_7^2)Q_7>=0. Therefore (13.6) pays the
old strict switch by a REAL initial reserve and dissipation across BOTH
children. No face constant or fictitious transverse drop is used.

This result is deliberately scoped. Later switches in the remaining
H_m suffix have not been bounded. The example is not assumed to remain
in the core after x_7. Inequality (13.6) is not promoted into a full-word
or all-start estimate. It supplies a nontrivial test passed by the new
storage, whereas the earlier local transverse proposal failed this test.

## 14. The remaining interface and scope of the two attempts

Mechanism1 is decisively closed AS A SCALAR-ONLY CERTIFICATE at the
requested constant: its exact complete-word value and sharp separate
conversions already give (8.2). This failure leaves the actual dynamics
open and identifies precisely the lost terminal face information.

Mechanism2 has two direct advances, jointly derived: the short-step
inequality (11.1) and the complete-H_3 payment (13.6). The attempt to
compose short-step payments across a long bridge fails by (11.3).
The other attempt, using the complete scalar hierarchy estimate to prove
(10.2), cannot work with only the separate norm/objective conversions,
as (8.1)--(8.2) show. Direct counterexample checks on fixed-face histories
and the strict-switch family do not refute the universal block law, but
absence of a counterexample is not a proof.

The actual all-start induction needed is a comparison for U_d, or a
replacement directional storage, across H_j, its real level-j bridge,
and the second H_j, for arbitrary incoming x and arbitrary real trailing
mass d from the SAME enclosing word. The state must carry the actual
child output; independently maximizing child orientations is forbidden.
Equation (10.2) states one concrete proposed law with that scope. We have
neither proved nor refuted it. Even proving it would bound only this
triangle projection family, not the full W supremum in PROBLEM.md.

## 15. Retrieval, author audit, and bounded closeout

Skills used: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples,
search-math-results. The search stage was mature_subproblem; active program

and analogy. Two Matlas queries were issued. Their hits were respectively
1709.04543 Definition2,1810.11640 Definition3,2105.13913 Proposition3.1,
and2111.07720 Lemma5.1,1807.00264 LemmaIV.2,2403.10232 Lemma5. None is
a discrete long-step block inequality for this map. Web retrieval also
did not supply that missing result. None is used as an external premise.

An attempted public PDF reread of the previously identified exact
simplex-projection paper arXiv:1309.1541 failed with
"Fatal error: connection failed: error sending request". No successful
reread is claimed. Its previously known subject is a single simplex
projection, not the all-block law. Every needed projection statement,

round introduces no external-theory branch requiring an unexamined black
box: the obstruction reduces to exact scalar maps, while the storage

The tool failure occurred before the following file-write operation in
that call; the storage text was written successfully in a separate call.

The initial broad recall output was truncated. Relevant prior report

The author audit checks every radius endpoint in (5.1)--(5.2), attainment
