<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-001085 -->
# Independent caps with one common outward curvature kink

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. No verifier call, accepted fact, or minimax resolution.**

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; invocation86 after ended85;
completed ceiling86 / attempted87; one-invocation cap1. Assignment guidance
01add6113f777746; plan d3bb77837e54072e. No renewal is inferred.

This report contains two substantive mechanisms, in the authorized order.
First it derives an all-start bound for the ENTIRE balanced-cap region.
Second it pays one explicitly defined separated-cap original-root transfer
whose complete physical suffix stays in the smaller-cap tail. The latter
is an itinerary result, not a bound for the whole separated-cap family.

All mathematical ingredients are proved together below. The earlier odd
draft 8a5a6aef2db4f411 and its public analytical audit c2c80ed9020960f5
were read for awareness. Neither is used as an accepted premise. The
current TASK forbids fact_submit, so the entire proof remains UNVERIFIED.

## 1. Precise family, same-m word, and candidate conclusions

Fix ONE integer m>=1536. At every child depth retain

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, b=c-2, ell=1+a,
    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    S=S_m, D=1+2S, n=2^m-1.

Here 0<=j<m in the word recursion, and d_m is an auxiliary scale, not
an additional step. Write h_i, 0<=i<n, for the physical chronological
entries of H_m. Every symbol in this report is local; for example the
word H_j, mass A_j, ratio b, and ray functions do not redefine unrelated
symbols in the project glossary.

Fix 0<kappa<=1 and two finite caps B_+>=kappa, B_->=kappa. Put

    v=1-kappa, u_sigma=v+B_sigma, sigma in {+1,-1},
    Bmax=max(B_+,B_-), Bmin=min(B_+,B_-),
    G_sigma(r)=min(B_sigma,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(sigma*r)=sigma*G_sigma(r), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F_sigma(r)=f(sigma*r).

The signs + and - in subscripts mean +1 and -1. The common core has
radius1 and curvature kappa on BOTH sides. Caps are the only independent
ray parameters. This is one fixed function for the entire word.

Let Psi_j(s) be the actual output of the complete H_j starting at s, using
this same f. Set p(x)=|g(x)|, including p(0)=0. For a full nonzero start s,
write R=|s| and x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i). The terminal is x_n=Psi_m(s).
The true original distance to the unique minimizer will be R, on either ray.

**Candidate balanced-cap theorem.** If

    Bmin/Bmax >= b=c-2,

then, for EVERY permitted kappa, pair of caps, and nonzero start s,

    2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <= (1+21/(4m))/D <17/(16D).       (1.1)

No function- or start-dependent eventual threshold appears: m>=1536
suffices. The first mechanism establishes (1.1) in Sections2-12.

**Candidate separated-state theorem.** Without the balanced assumption,
fix one ray with larger cap B and the opposite ray with smaller cap K,
where kappa<=K<B. Set J=m-1, A=A_J, d=d_J, T=S_J=A-1. Consider exactly
the histories for which the entire left H_J and its root source stay
on the B tail, the original root crosses to the K ray, and the entire
right H_J including its terminal stays on the K tail. Such a history
exists precisely for original radii

    v+AB <= R <= (A+d)B-AK-v.                         (1.2)

The interval is nonempty precisely when dB-AK>=2v; this condition
itself implies K/B<b. On this state the exact terminal and payoff are
given in Section13, and uniformly

    2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <204/(343D)<3/(5D).              (1.3)

This is one paid escape into the smaller tail, with no return across
zero in the suffix. It does not assert (1.1) for arbitrary separated
caps or arbitrary itineraries. A concrete nonempty member of this
state with a genuine outward kink is also given in Section13.

Neither theorem is a bound on the full finite-dimensional smooth-convex
supremum W_n(H_m). In particular neither supplies the full-class
hypothesis in accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201. The original minimax
problem in PROBLEM.md remains OPEN, and W=2R retains its original
meaning; the plain R used here is explicitly the original radius.

## 2. Both exact ray energies and legality of the fixed objective

For EACH sigma separately the derivative and twice-energy are

    G_sigma(r)=kappa*r,                     0<=r<=1,
                 r-v,                      1<=r<=u_sigma,
                 B_sigma,                  r>=u_sigma;

    2F_sigma(r)=kappa*r^2,                  0<=r<=1,
                 (r-v)^2+kappa*v,           1<=r<=u_sigma,
                 2B_sigma*r-Ctail_sigma,   r>=u_sigma,
    Ctail_sigma=B_sigma^2+v*(2B_sigma-kappa).         (2.1)

Indeed the negative-ray substitution t=-z in the defining integral
gives f(-r)=integral_0^r G_-(z)dz; hence its cap area must use B_-, not
B_+. Integrating the core and middle gives the first two expressions.
At u_sigma the energy is B_sigma^2+kappa*v; adding
2B_sigma*(r-u_sigma) gives the displayed Ctail_sigma. At1 both
expressions equal kappa. Values and first derivatives agree at both
knots. The middle may collapse when B_sigma=kappa, separately on either
ray. If kappa=1, v=0 and the whole law is an asymmetric Huber law.

Across the entire real line g is continuous, nondecreasing, and has
piecewise slopes kappa,1,0. Partition an arbitrary interval at its
knots and at0; the increment of g is between zero and that interval's
length. Thus g is globally 1-Lipschitz. Its integral is finite,
differentiable, convex, and 1-smooth. Since every G_sigma(r)>0 for r>0,
0 is the unique minimizer, with f(0)=0. No evenness is assumed.

The entire actual transcript is smooth-convex legal. For x<y, put
Delta=g(y)-g(x), so 0<=Delta<=y-x. On [x,y],

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).

Both right sides integrate to Delta^2/2. This proves both ordered
interpolation rows, and therefore for every ordered pair of actual
queries, terminal, or minimizer,

    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.

Repeated positions have identical jets. Their scalar Gram matrices
are positive semidefinite because the associated quadratic forms
are squares of real linear combinations. This is an actual function
construction, not independent prescriptions for two child transcripts.

For each ray, throughout its domain,

    0<=G_sigma(r)<=min(r,B_sigma),
    G_sigma(r)>=kappa*min(r,1).                       (2.2)

If p=G_sigma(r), then G_sigma(t)<=min(t,p) on [0,r]. Integrating gives

    2F_sigma(r)<=2r*p-p^2.                            (2.3)

There is also the strictly scoped conversion

    2F_sigma(r)<=p^2/kappa  when r<=u_sigma.           (2.4)

It is equality in the core; in the middle the difference is
v*(p^2-kappa^2)/kappa>=0. It is NOT asserted farther along a tail.

A strict crossing means consecutive nonzero iterates have opposite
signs. A zero hit stays zero. In a segment with no strict crossing
and no zero hit, all points stay on one ray and radii decrease. If
the segment has step mass M, original radius R, terminal radius r,
and terminal gradient p on that same ray, monotonicity gives

    R>=r+M*p,
    2F_sigma(r)<=2R*p-(1+2M)*p^2<=R^2/(1+2M).        (2.5)

The last inequality is completion of the square. In particular all
full-word noncrossing histories have score<=1/D, independent of cap
balance. Multiplying f by any advertised smoothness L_adv>0 and using
steps h_i/L_adv leaves the actual points and normalized score unchanged.

## 3. Quantitative masses and literal suffixes of the original word

For m>=1536 the following bounds follow directly:

    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>999/1000,
    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,
    2/5<a<1/2, 0<a<b<3/7<beta, ca=beta<1.          (3.1)

For c, use 7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and
(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7. Then
a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5 and a<1/(12/5)<1/2.
The identity lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda give
c*(c-2)>1>beta, proving b>a. The other comparisons follow from
b<3/7 and beta>999/1000.

Let eta=a/b, so 0<eta<1. The word masses obey

    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, A_0=1,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                         (3.2)

Substitution verifies the formula and its initial value. Consequently

    A_j>d_j/b for every j>=0,
    A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    D<2c^m=2d_m/a.                                  (3.3)

For L_j=S_j/d_j and j>=1,

    L_j>7/5.                                         (3.4)

At j=1 this follows from L_1=(1+a)/beta>7/5. The recurrence
L_(j+1)=(2L_j+1+1/d_j)/c and c<17/7 preserve the strict bound,
since (19/5)/(17/7)>7/5.

The useful finite margins are

    S>1000, d_(m-1)>9, d_(m-1)>1/epsilon,
    d_m>2/a, d_m^2>D, D/S<201/100.                  (3.5)

Indeed d_(m-1)>2^m/5. At integer m=8, 2^m>20m, and doubling
preserves this inequality on increasing m by1. Thus
d_(m-1)>4m=1/epsilon; for m>=1536 this also gives the bounds9
and1000, using S>=1+d_(m-1). Since 2/a<5, the bound d_m>2/a
and then d_m^2>D follow from (3.3). Finally D/S=2+1/S.

A physical level-k bridge in a complete H_j has a chronological
prefix containing its full immediate left H_k, hence prefix mass
at least S_k. Its physical suffix is exactly

    H_k, (1+d_(l_1)),H_(l_1), ...,
         (1+d_(l_t)),H_(l_t),
    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                              (3.6)

Ascend its binary word tree: each ancestor reached through its left
child contributes its bridge and complete right child; each reached
through its right child contributes nothing. This proves (3.6),
including empty suffixes and skipped ancestor levels. All later
arguments use this physical suffix, not a selected subsequence.

## 4. Exact SIGNED interval images, including unavailable pieces

Fix h=1+d with d>=0. For a source on ray sigma, express its signed
output relative to that source sign as T_sigma(r)=r-hG_sigma(r).
Then

    T_sigma(r)=(1-h*kappa)*r,           0<=r<=1,
                 h*v-d*r,             1<=r<=u_sigma,
                 r-h*B_sigma,         r>=u_sigma.    (4.1)

Adjacent formulas agree at the knots. Define [z]_+=max(z,0).
For an entire source interval 0<=r<=R the exact greatest positive
same-ray output and opposite-ray output are respectively

    Stay_sigma(R)=max([1-h*kappa]_+*min(R,1),
                      [R-h*B_sigma]_+),
    Cross_sigma(R)=max([h*kappa-1]_+*min(R,1),
                       [d*min(R,u_sigma)-h*v]_+).     (4.2)

To prove these expressions, first maximize each affine piece. On
the core its signed maximum and minimum are its appropriate endpoint
or zero. On a present middle piece, T_sigma is nonincreasing: its
greatest positive value is already the core endpoint at1, and its
most negative value occurs at min(R,u_sigma). On a present tail,
T_sigma is increasing: its positive maximum is the endpoint at R,
and its most negative value is at u_sigma, already represented by
the middle endpoint. The origin always supplies zero.

There are two possible formal pieces that are not present. If R<1,

    dR-hv-(h*kappa-1)R=hv*(R-1)<=0,

so the formal middle crossing term is dominated by the actual core
crossing term. If R<u_sigma, the formal tail stay R-hB_sigma is
at most the actual endpoint R-hG_sigma(R), hence cannot exceed the
true stay maximum. If that formal value is positive, the actual
pre-cap endpoint is positive and already accounted for by the core
endpoint. This proves both maxima without adding unattained branches.

For an input interval [-P,Q], where P,Q>=0, the exact output is
[-Pnext,Qnext] with

    Qnext=max(Stay_+(Q),Cross_-(P)),
    Pnext=max(Stay_-(P),Cross_+(Q)).                   (4.3)

The maxima are attained on the two compact source intervals. The
whole step map is continuous and fixes0, so its image is exactly
that interval, with no holes. Inductively the image of any interval
containing0 under an entire word is an interval containing0. In
general Pnext and Qnext differ: oddness and a symmetric radius
recurrence are not used.

Equations(4.1)-(4.3) cover R=0, R=1, R=u_sigma, B_sigma=kappa,
v=0, d=0, h*kappa=1, and zero outputs because the adjacent affine
formulas and positive parts agree. For d=0 a positive pre-cap
middle stay can equal1-kappa; the actual hierarchy has d_j>0.

For a STRICT crossing from actual radius r on ray sigma, with
p=G_sigma(r) and opposite-ray landing z>0,

    z=(1+d)p-r<=d*p.                                 (4.4)

This uses p<=r and is independent of the destination cap. Its
destination gradient satisfies G_(-sigma)(z)<=z, not necessarily
G_sigma(z); this distinction is retained below.

For a positive same-ray output from a strictly pre-cap source
r<u_sigma, one necessarily has h*kappa<1, the output lies in
the core, and it is at most both

    (1-h*kappa)*r and (1-h*kappa)*min(r,1).            (4.5)

In the core this is (4.1). In the middle write the output as
1-h*kappa-d*(r-1), and use r>=1. A cap-knot source is instead
assigned to the saturated case; its two output formulas agree.

If all steps of H_j have h*kappa<=1, the closed core [-1,1]
is absorbing, regardless of the caps, with full product

    Pcore_j=product_(h in H_j)(1-kappa*h)
       <=exp(-kappa*S_j).                            (4.6)

The factors are nonnegative and 1-z<=exp(-z); no physical step
or terminal-only entry is replaced by an extra factor.

## 5. First exterior crossing: payment from the ORIGINAL radius

Consider the first strict crossing in a complete-word trajectory,
with exterior source r>1 on a definite ray sigma. Put
p=G_sigma(r), let k be its physical level, P its chronological
prefix mass from the original start of that word, and z its
landing radius. Earlier radii decrease on this same source ray,
and their gradients are at least p. Exterior sources have r>=v+p:
equality in the middle, inequality in the tail. Thus

    R>=r+P*p>=v+(1+P)p>=v+A_k*p,
    z<=d_k*p-v.                                      (5.1)

The last inequality is the actual update, not a new starting
condition for a child. It uses no property of the destination cap.
Since A_k>d_k/b and R-v>=A_kp>0,

    z <=(d_k/A_k)*(R-v)-v
       < b*(R-v)-v=:Lland(R).                        (5.2)

In particular when v>0, strict crossing requires p>v/d_k and

    R>v*Qprice, Qprice=(c-1)/(c-2)>10/3.             (5.3)

The inequality Qprice>10/3 follows from b<3/7. In a nonempty
complete word whose steps preserve the core, a first crossing
must have exterior source; any earlier core entry is absorbing.
Such a word has v>0 because all its steps exceed1. These facts
justify (5.3) whenever it is invoked for a core-preserving child.

Whenever the original denominator is needed below, R in (5.1)
is explicitly the start of H_m or its original left prefix,
which has that same start. Applying (5.3) to a right child is
used only to rule out a crossing there, never to reset the
full-word denominator.

## 6. Joint signed gradient grid and crossing capture: balanced caps

In this section assume Bmin>=b*Bmax. Since b>a,

    a*Bmax < Bmin <= B_sigma on either ray.           (6.1)

For EVERY j>=1 through m prove jointly:

    p(Psi_j(s))<=a*c^(-q)*Bmax
      for every integer q>=0 and |s|<=d_j*c^(-q)*Bmax; (6.2)

    any strict crossing within a complete H_j
      implies p(Psi_j(s))<=a*Bmax.                   (6.3)

The function and both caps stay fixed at every q. The grid bounds
the actual terminal gradient on its actual sign; it is not a radial
map for an odd extension and does not replace either cap by w.

First give the derivation of capture at any depth using only grid
claims at proper-child depths. A level-k crossing has landing
radius<=d_k*Bmax by (4.4). Its immediate full right H_k therefore
has terminal gradient<=aBmax if k>=1, by its q=0 grid. If k=0,
the empty child leaves a landing whose destination gradient is
at most its radius<=aBmax. At any subsequent retained ancestor
of level l in (3.6), (6.1) puts the incoming point strictly
before the cap of its actual ray. If that bridge crosses, its
landing is at most d_l*aBmax<=d_lBmax, and the full following
H_l restores the bound by its grid. If it has a positive
same-ray stay, (4.5) puts that stay in the core, with no gradient
increase. It also gives kappa*(1+d_l)<1, so the subsequent
smaller steps of H_l preserve the core and cannot increase
its gradient. Zero is absorbing. Every suffix step is covered
by (3.6); every grid depth is less than the depth of the entire
word whose capture is being established.

For the base j=1, put w=c^(-q)Bmax and R=beta*w=d_1*w. Unlike
the odd proof, the smaller-cap ray MAY already be saturated.
There are three cases at the single step h=ell=1+a.

* A crossing lands at radius<=a*p(s)<=a|s|<=a*beta*w<a*w.
  Its destination gradient is no greater than that radius.
* A positive pre-cap stay has output gradient at most
  kappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+*min(R,1)<=R/(4ell)<a*R<a*w.
  Here 4aell>4*(2/5)*(7/5)>1.
* A positive saturated stay on ray sigma has radius at most

      beta*w-ell*B_sigma <= [beta-ell*b]*w <a*w.       (6.4)

  The first inequality uses B_sigma>=bBmax>=bw. For the strict
  second inequality, ell*b-(beta-a)=b-a>0, since beta=ac.
  The output gradient is at most its radius, including if that
  output itself is still saturated. If the displayed upper
  radius is negative, this stay case is simply impossible.

Zero gives zero. This proves (6.2) at j=1, and the preceding
proper-child suffix argument proves (6.3) there.

Now assume both claims through j>=1. For the parent take any
actual start with radius R<=c*d_j*w, w=c^(-q)Bmax. Let y be
the radius of its actual left-child output on its actual ray
sigma, and z the radius after the parent bridge. If y=0 the
rest is zero. Partition the nonzero bridge as follows.

(a) Strict crossing. If q=0, G_sigma(y)<=Bmax=w. If q>=1, the
left child starts within d_j*c^(-(q-1))*Bmax, so its grid gives
G_sigma(y)<=a*c*w=beta*w<=w. The bridge lands at radius<=d_j*w.
The complete right-child grid gives terminal gradient<=a*w.

(b) Positive saturated same-ray stay, including y=u_sigma.
The left child could not have crossed, because its capture
would imply G_sigma(y)<=aBmax<B_sigma, contradicting saturation.
It could not have hit zero. Thus all its points remain on
the current ray, with radii decreasing to y>=u_sigma; all
its queried gradients are B_sigma. Consequently

    y=R-S_j*B_sigma,
    z=R-(A_j+d_j)*B_sigma
       <=c*d_j*w-(A_j+d_j)*B_sigma <d_j*w.          (6.5)

To verify the last payment, use (3.3) and balance:

    (A_j+d_j)*B_sigma
       >=(A_j+d_j)*b*Bmax
       >(1+b)*d_j*Bmax
       >=(c-1)*d_j*w.

Then the full right H_j uses its q-th grid. A prior crossing
is ruled out before asserting the all-tail prefix; no tail
assumption is inferred from the endpoint alone.

(c) Positive pre-cap same-ray stay, with no crossing in the
left child. By (4.5), kappa*(1+d_j)<1, and the right child
stays in the core. Put t=kappa*d_j and L=L_j=S_j/d_j.
The noncrossing left child gives

    R>=y+S_j*G_sigma(y)>=(1+kappa*S_j)*y,

where G_sigma(y)>=kappa*y is used only at this pre-cap source.
Combining (4.5)-(4.6), R<=c*d_j*w, and 0<t<1 gives

    p_out<=kappa*exp(-Lt)*(1-kappa-t)*R/(1+Lt)
       <=c*t*(1-t)*exp(-Lt)/(1+Lt)*w
       <=c*t*(1-t)/(1+Lt)^2*w
       <=c/[4*(1+L)]*w<a*w.                         (6.6)

The third line uses exp(Lt)>=1+Lt; the fourth follows from

    (1+Lt)^2-4*(1+L)*t*(1-t)=(1-(L+2)t)^2.

Finally c/[4*(1+L)]<85/336<2/5<a by (3.1),(3.4).

(d) Positive pre-cap same-ray stay, with a crossing in the
left child. Again kappa*(1+d_j)<1, so both children preserve
their cores. The first crossing in that original left child
has exterior source and pays R>vQprice by (5.3), using the
actual parent start. Here 0<t<v. The parent bridge output
is at most 1-kappa-t=v-t, and its full right child yields

    p_out<=kappa*(v-t)*exp(-Lt).

Since R<=c*d_j*w and R>vQprice, w>vQprice/(c*d_j), giving

    p_out/w<c*t*(v-t)*exp(-Lt)/(vQprice)
       <=c*v/(4Qprice)<=c/(4Qprice)<a.              (6.7)

We used t*(v-t)<=v^2/4 and
c/(4Qprice)<51/280<2/5<a. All divisions by v are in the
core-preserving case v>0.

A zero bridge is already settled. These are all bridge
outcomes and all first-child crossing possibilities. Thus
(6.2) holds at j+1. The proper-child suffix argument then
gives (6.3) at j+1. This is a noncircular joint induction.

In particular every crossing history ends strictly before
its ACTUAL terminal-side cap knot, since

    p(x_terminal)<=aBmax<Bmin<=B_terminal-side.       (6.8)

The balanced ratio is used in (6.4),(6.5),(6.8), not silently
extended to a cap ratio below b.

## 7. Global gradient control and the asymmetric Huber reference

For 0<R<=d_jBmax choose an integer q>=0 with

    d_j*c^(-(q+1))*Bmax<R<=d_j*c^(-q)*Bmax.

An exact grid boundary is the upper endpoint. For every actual
start |s|<=R, (6.2) gives terminal gradient
<=a*c^(-q)Bmax<beta*R/d_j. For R>d_jBmax, a crossing history
uses (6.3) and aBmax<beta*R/d_j. A noncrossing history on
its own ray, with terminal radius r and gradient p, has

    |s|>=r+S_j*p>=A_j*p,
    p<=R/A_j<b*R/d_j<beta*R/d_j.

A zero hit gives p=0. Thus at all j>=1 and R>0,

    p(Psi_j(s))<=beta*R/d_j for every |s|<=R.        (7.1)

Now define the asymmetric unit-curvature Huber objective J
whose signed derivative on ray sigma is sigma*min(r,B_sigma).
We first assume Bmin>=1 and Bmin/Bmax>=b. This is exactly the
present family with kappa=1, common knot1, v=0, and the same
two caps. The already derived grid and capture apply directly.
On a crossing history its terminal is pre-cap on its actual
side by (6.8), so its twice-energy is p_terminal^2. Equation
(7.1) and d_m^2>D give its score<=beta^2/d_m^2<1/D. Every
noncrossing history has score<=1/D by (2.5). Hence

    2J(Psi_m^J(s))<=s^2/D.                          (7.2)

For arbitrary positive balanced caps, scale BOTH caps by their
common divisor Bmin: write x=Bmin*y and

    J(x)=Bmin^2*J_tilde(x/Bmin),

where J_tilde has caps B_sigma/Bmin>=1. The signed GD recursion
in y uses the identical physical word; the original radius and
energy scale by Bmin and Bmin^2. Thus (7.2) holds for every
positive pair of balanced caps. This scale change is only a
proof of the auxiliary Huber reference, not a replacement of
the original kink function or its core knot.

For this J, the exact signed interval update has ray maxima

    Stay_sigma^J(R)=[R-hB_sigma]_+,
    Cross_sigma^J(R)=d*min(R,B_sigma), h=1+d.         (7.3)

Inside the Huber core the signed output is -dr. On its tail
it is r-hB_sigma. The largest crossing is the core endpoint
at min(R,B_sigma), and the largest positive stay is the tail
endpoint at R, if positive. These give (7.3) even for absent
tails. The pair update is (4.3) with these two functions. Both
functions, hence both pair coordinates, are nondecreasing in
the relevant nonnegative radii.

Suppose now that an actual kink history has EVERY queried
point and its terminal at radius>1. Start the auxiliary J
interval at [-R,R], with R=|s|, and propagate its exact two
signed endpoints through the original physical word. Inductively
the actual signed kink point lies within this Huber interval.
For if its next step crosses from side sigma, (4.4),(2.2) give
landing<=d*min(r,B_sigma), controlled by that side's crossing
term in (7.3). If it stays on its side with positive output,
a pre-cap middle source would land in the core by (4.5),
contrary to the exterior-history assumption. Hence this stay
is saturated and equals r-hB_sigma, controlled by the same-side
stay term. Coordinate monotonicity proves the induction.

Let the actual terminal side be sigma_n. The corresponding
signed endpoint of the final Huber interval has radius at
least |x_n|. It is attained by an ACTUAL Huber trajectory
starting at some s_* in [-R,R], because the complete map is
continuous on that compact interval. Both ray energies are
nondecreasing in radius, and G_sigma<=min(r,B_sigma) gives
F_sigma(r)<=J(sigma*r). Therefore

    2f(x_n)<=2J(Psi_m^J(s_*))<=s_*^2/D<=R^2/D.       (7.4)

The selected endpoint is on the actual terminal side. No
evenness, oddness, single symmetric envelope, or artificial
denominator is used. The comparison imposes no inner-reversal
condition: its premise is the actual history remaining exterior.

## 8. All starts with large curvature mass

Assume kappa*S>=12 and balanced caps. Noncrossing histories
use (2.5). Crossing histories end strictly pre-cap on the
actual terminal side by (6.8). Thus (2.4),(7.1) give

    D*2f(x_n)/R^2<=D*beta^2/(kappa*d_m^2)
       =(D/S)*(beta*S/d_m)^2/(kappa*S)
       <(201/100)*(17/7)^2/12
       =58089/58800<1.                              (8.1)

Here beta*S/d_m<c follows from A_m<c^m=d_m/a.
No limitation on previous sign changes, tail visits, or core
returns is imposed. The terminal-side energy conversion is
legitimate solely because strict pre-cap capture was proved.

## 9. No inner reversal and the residual parameter band

Assume first kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1. All physical steps preserve
the closed core. Noncrossing histories use (2.5). Crossing
histories that never enter that core use (7.4). For a crossing
history with any core entry, the first crossing has exterior
source and pays R>vQprice; the terminal stays in the core on
whichever side it has reached, so 2f(x_n)<=kappa. Hence

    score:=2f(x_n)/R^2<kappa/(v^2*Qprice^2).           (9.1)

This includes a first core entry at the actual terminal.
Put d=d_(m-1) and t_c=c*(c-2). The increasing function
z/(1-z)^2 on (0,1), evaluated at kappa<=1/(1+d), gives
kappa/v^2<=(1+d)/d^2. With D<2c^m and
(c-1)^2=t_c+1, obtain

    D*kappa/(v^2*Qprice^2)
       <[2*t_c^2/(beta*(t_c+1))]*(1+1/d).           (9.2)

For example 2c^m/d=2c/a=2c^2/beta gives this expression
by direct substitution. The identity lambda^2=2lambda+1 yields

    t_c=1+2*(lambda+1)*epsilon+lambda^2*epsilon^2
       <1+8epsilon.

Indeed lambda<5/2 and epsilon<1/1000 bound its coefficient
above1 by 7+(25/4)/1000<8. Since t_c>1 and d>1/epsilon,
the right side of (9.2) is less than

    (1+8epsilon)^2*(1+epsilon)/(1-epsilon).

The exact difference
(1-epsilon)*(1+19epsilon)-(1+8epsilon)^2
=2epsilon-83epsilon^2 is positive. Therefore the last
quantity is less than (1+19epsilon)*(1+epsilon)<=1+21epsilon,
using 19epsilon<=1. This proves (1.1) in this parameter region.
Also 21/(4m)<=21/6144<1/16, proving its strict final envelope.

The remaining parameters satisfy kappa*S<12 and
kappa*(1+d_(m-1))>1. Write d=d_(m-1). From (3.3),

    kappa*S>S/(1+d)>[c*d/b-1]/(1+d)>5.              (9.3)

For the final inequality, c/b>17/3 and d>9 imply
(c/b-5)d>(2/3)*9=6. On 12/5<c<17/7,

    b/c^2<3/40:

the polynomial 3c^2-40c+80 decreases there and is27/49>0
at17/7. Since d_m<b(S+1),

    kappa*(1+d_(m-2))
       <12*[1/S+(3/40)*(1+1/S)]
       =9/10+129/(10S)<1.                           (9.4)

Here S>1000. Set J=m-1. The ORIGINAL root is consequently
the sole physical step that can reverse a core input; both
complete H_J children individually preserve that core. In
this whole residual band,

    5<kappa*S<12,
    v>49/50, D*kappa<603/25<25.                     (9.5)

Indeed kappa<12/S<1/50 and D/S<201/100. The equality
kappa*S=12 belongs to Section8; equality at the largest
core-nonreversal threshold belongs to the first region here.

## 10. Root source in the core, including an exterior root landing

In the residual band put y=|Psi_J(s)|. If y=0 the terminal
is zero. Suppose 0<y<=1. Regardless of its sign the root
gradient has magnitude kappa*y and the root reverses, with
actual opposite-ray landing z=q*y, where

    t=kappa*d_J, q=kappa*(1+d_J)-1=t-v>0,
    L=S_J/d_J>7/5.

The child core nonreversal in (9.4) implies

    v<t<=c*v, q<(3/2)*v, q<(3/5)*t.                (10.1)

For the last two bounds use c<5/2, q<= (c-1)v,
and q/t<=1-1/c<3/5. The root landing may exceed1.

The complete right H_J cannot strictly cross. Such a crossing
would have an exterior first source because this child
preserves the core, and (5.3) would require its OWN starting
radius z>vQprice. But z<=q<3v/2<vQprice. Thus it stays on
the root's destination ray until possibly hitting zero.
It must enter the core by its actual terminal: otherwise
all its queried gradients are at least kappa by (2.2),
giving terminal radius<=z-kappa*S_J<=q-Lt<0, impossible.

For a bound that retains the entire return and suffix, let

    C_h(r)=r-h*kappa*min(r,1)

for each actual right-child step. Since 0<=h*kappa<=1,
C_h is nonnegative and nondecreasing on [0,infinity).
Along the noncrossing actual child, (2.2) gives
r_next<=C_h(r); monotonicity bounds its terminal by the
full chronological composition of these C_h maps from z.

For r>0 define Bfun(r)=log r for r<=1 and r-1 for r>=1.
For alpha in [0,1] and a positive output,

    Bfun(r-alpha*min(r,1))<=Bfun(r)-alpha.           (10.2)

Inside the core this is log(1-alpha)<=-alpha. Outside
with outside output it is equality; an outside-to-core
transition uses log w<=w-1 at w=r-alpha. Zero stays zero.
Since Bfun(z)<Lt (if z>1 use z-1<q<Lt), summing over
EVERY actual child step makes the final comparison radius
less than1 and at most Kfun(z)*exp(-Lt), where

    Kfun(z)=z for 0<=z<=1; exp(z-1) for z>=1.

For 0<=y<=1,

    Kfun(q*y)<=y*Kfun(q).                            (10.3)

If q<=1 this is equality. If q>1 and qy<=1, it uses
q<=exp(q-1). If qy>=1, differentiate exp(qy-1)/y:
its derivative has the sign of qy-1, so its maximum on
[1/q,1] is exp(q-1). These cases prove (10.3). Hence

    |x_n|<=y*Z, Z=Kfun(q)*exp(-Lt).                 (10.4)

Uniformly Z<1/4. If q<=1, Lt>7q/3 from (10.1), so
Z<q*exp(-7q/3)<=3/(7e)<1/4, with e=exp(1)>2.
The maximum follows by differentiating w*exp(-7w/3).
If q>1, Z<exp(-1-4q/3)<exp(-7/3)<1/4 because
exp(7/3)>e^2>4.

Pay the ORIGINAL R separately. If the original left child
did not cross, all its radii decreased on its original ray
to y, whose gradient is kappa*y. Thus
R>=(1+kappa*S_J)*y=(1+Lt)*y. By (9.5),
Lt>(7/5)*(49/50)>1, so y/R<1/2. If the left child did
cross, its first source is exterior and (5.3) pays the
original R>vQprice>49/15>3; as y<=1, now y/R<1/3.
The terminal is in the common core, so (9.5),(10.4) give

    D*2f(x_n)/R^2<25/64<1/2  in the noncrossing-left case,
    D*2f(x_n)/R^2<25/144<1/2 in the crossing-left case. (10.5)

Both actual signs, both destination caps, an exterior root
landing, and entry into the core only at the actual terminal
are included. The argument uses no even objective values.

## 11. Original-radius landing price in the residual band

For the first strict crossing of the FULL trajectory with
exterior source, (5.2) is

    z<Lland(R)=b*(R-v)-v.

Define

    Rcrit=v+(1+v)/b, Lland(Rcrit)=1.                 (11.1)

Any landing z>=1 forces R>Rcrit, including the tie z=1,
because (5.2) is strict. In the residual band b<3/7 and
v>49/50 imply

    Rcrit>v+(7/3)*(1+v)
       =(10/3)v+7/3>28/5,
    D*kappa/Rcrit^2<(603/25)/(28/5)^2=603/784<1.     (11.2)

If R<=Rcrit and every step AFTER this first crossing
preserves the core, its landing is below1 and all later
radii are at most z. Also

    Lland(R)/R=b-(c-1)*v/R

increases with R>0 and equals1/Rcrit at Rcrit. Hence

    |x_n|/R<=1/Rcrit.                               (11.3)

If R>Rcrit and a later core entry is absorbing, then
|x_n|<=1 also gives |x_n|/R<1/Rcrit. No assertion that
the original root preserves a core input is made here;
its location relative to each entry is checked next.

## 12. Exterior root source: exhaustive balanced-region closure

Remain in the residual band, and suppose y=|Psi_J(s)|>1.
The original left child never entered the closed core,
because it preserves that core and ends outside. Thus
every point through the root source is exterior. Zero
hits are settled; histories with no strict crossing use
(2.5). Consider the remaining histories.

Case A: the original left child had a crossing. Its first
crossing is the full trajectory's first; both source and
landing are exterior because a core landing would stay
core through the child endpoint. Thus (11.1) pays
R>Rcrit. If the root output or any later point enters the
core, every remaining step is in the right H_J and
preserves it. The actual terminal energy is at most
kappa, and (11.2) gives score<603/(784D). If no such
entry occurs, the entire original history, all queries
and the terminal, is exterior; apply the signed asymmetric
Huber comparison (7.4). Repeated exterior crossings on
either side are included.

Case B: the original left child had no crossing. The first
crossing is at the root or inside the right child. Its
source is exterior: at the root this is y>1; in the right
child an earlier core entry would have been absorbing
and would forbid the crossing. Every step after this first
crossing belongs to the core-preserving right H_J.

If R<=Rcrit, (11.3) gives a core terminal with
|x_n|/R<=1/Rcrit, so score<603/(784D). If R>Rcrit
and a point from the first crossing onward enters the
core, it stays there and gives the same bound. If R>Rcrit
and there is no such entry, the full history is exterior:
the original left child was exterior, and any earlier
right-child core entry would have forbidden the first
crossing. Apply (7.4).

These are all possibilities when y>1. A saturated same-side
root step entering the core without an earlier crossing
cannot have a later crossing, so belongs to (2.5). A
same-side entry at the last physical step uses the
crossing-and-core alternative if there was an earlier
crossing, and otherwise (2.5); no fictitious post-entry
contraction is applied.

Together Sections8-9 exhaust parameter choices outside
the residual band. Within it Sections10 and12 exhaust
the actual left-child endpoint. This proves (1.1) as a
JOINT UNVERIFIED candidate for the entire balanced region.

All boundaries are included: a radius1 is in the closed
core; each ray's own cap knot is in its saturated branch
with matching formulas; either middle may collapse;
zero is absorbing; kappa=1 is covered without dividing
by v; an exact grid boundary is an upper cell endpoint;
the balanced ratio may equal b; R=Rcrit has a strictly
subunit first landing. Strict b>a ensures even the
balanced-boundary terminal is strictly pre-cap.

## 13. Second mechanism: one paid separated-cap tail escape

Only after the balanced mechanism closes do we drop its
ratio assumption for this ONE explicitly specified state.
Let the initial ray have cap B, the opposite ray cap K,
with kappa<=K<B; write sigma for that initial sign. Keep
the same fixed law, same m, and original complete word

    H_m=(H_J,1+d,H_J), J=m-1,
    A=A_J, T=S_J=A-1, d=d_J, D=4A-1+2d.

Specify the actual itinerary: every point of the original
left child, including its output at the root source,
lies on the sigma tail; the root crosses to -sigma;
every point of the full right child, including the true
terminal, stays on that smaller K tail. This is a
large-to-small escape with its last (and only) sign
transfer at the original root. Neither child's start
is independently selected.

The state is characterized exactly by (1.2). To see it,
if the original R>=v+AB, the left child has constant
gradient magnitude B on every query and output, since
its decreasing radii terminate at

    r_root=R-TB>=v+B.

This also proves the converse within the stated all-tail
left-child itinerary. The root's signed output is

    x_after_root=-sigma*z,
    z=(1+d)B-r_root=(A+d)B-R.                       (13.1)

The complete right child has constant gradient magnitude
K and radius decreasing from z to

    r_n=z-TK=(A+d)B-R-TK.                           (13.2)

All its queries and terminal stay on the K tail exactly
when r_n>=v+K, that is R<=(A+d)B-AK-v. Under this
condition z>0 automatically, so the root crossing is
strict. The endpoint inequalities imply both children
stay in their claimed regions at every physical prefix,
because those prefixes have nonnegative increasing
step masses bounded by the respective complete masses.
This proves necessity and sufficiency, not merely a
formally concatenated endpoint calculation.

The admissible original-radius interval is nonempty
if and only if

    dB-AK>=2v.                                      (13.3)

It forces K/B<=d/A<b by (3.3). Thus every instance in
this state is in the separated region; the upper-bound
argument below never borrows balanced capture.

The terminal is actually on the smaller tail, so keep
that ray's TRUE intercept from (2.1):

    2f(x_n)=2K*((A+d)B-R-TK)-Ctail_K,
    Ctail_K=K^2+v*(2K-kappa).                       (13.4)

For fixed permitted kappa,B,K and a nonempty interval
(1.2), the normalized score in (13.4) strictly decreases
as R increases. Indeed let N(R) denote its numerator;
N(R)=2f(x_n)>0 because r_n>=v+K>0. Since N'(R)=-2K,

    (N(R)/R^2)'=(-2KR-2N(R))/R^3<0.

The exact optimum within this state is therefore attained
at the legal original start R_0=v+AB, where the root
source equals the larger cap knot v+B. There

    z_0=dB-v,
    r_n=dB-v-TK,
    max score =
      [2dBK-(2A-1)K^2-4Kv+kappa*v]/(v+AB)^2.      (13.5)

All tail area terms are retained before comparison.
Since K>=kappa and v>=0, -4Kv+kappa*v<=0, while
(v+AB)^2>=A^2B^2. The numerator in (13.5) is positive,
so these changes give a valid upper bound. Put t=K/B.
Then completing the square yields

    max score <=[2d*t-(2A-1)t^2]/A^2
       <=d^2/[A^2*(2A-1)].                         (13.6)

For a completely uniform same-m constant, use A>2
(as J>=1 and A_1=2+a>2) and q=d/A<b<3/7. Thus

    D*d^2/[A^2*(2A-1)]
       =q^2*(4-1/A+2q)/(2-1/A)
       <(9/49)*(4+6/7)/(3/2)
       =204/343<3/5.                              (13.7)

Every inequality has an absolute margin independent of
the function, initial radius, and m>=1536. Equations
(13.4)-(13.7) prove (1.3) for exactly this state, with
the original denominator and full physical suffix.

For a concrete nonempty fixed function with a genuine
outward kink take

    kappa=1/2, v=1/2, B_+=8, B_-=1,
    s=R_0=8A+1/2>0.

Its ratio is1/8<b. From A<c^J and d=a*c^J,
d/A>a>2/5, hence 8d-A>(11/5)A>1=2v. Thus (13.3)
holds strictly for every same m>=1536, without any
evaluated recurrence. The actual left child stays on
the positive tail and ends at17/2. The original root
lands at -(8d-1/2). The full right child stays on the
negative tail and terminates at

    x_n=-(8d-A+1/2).

This radius exceeds its cap knot3/2. The negative tail
intercept is Ctail_-=7/4, so the exact score is

    [16d-2A-3/4]/(8A+1/2)^2 <3/(5D).               (13.8)

Thus a smaller-side terminal really can remain saturated;
we have not replaced its objective by p_terminal^2/kappa.
The original all-tail approach and root transfer pay
its energy despite that saturation.

This same example pinpoints a forbidden extension of the
balanced proof. Its terminal gradient equals1<=aBmax=8a,
yet the terminal is beyond the smaller cap knot. Moreover
2f(x_n)=16d-2A-3/4>(22/5)A-3/4>2, whereas
p_terminal^2/kappa=2. Thus even the weaker observation
p_terminal<=aBmax after a crossing does not permit the
pre-cap energy conversion without a destination-cap
comparison. This is an exact legal original full-word
failure of that implication, not a counterexample to
the normalized objective bound (1.1).

The larger theorem for ALL separated-cap histories
remains OPEN. A prior crossing, a root source not on
the larger tail, departure from the smaller tail, or
a later return across zero is outside the explicit
state (1.2). No third mechanism is opened to treat them.

## 14. Status, provenance, and bounded handoff

The first mechanism gives a complete self-contained
candidate for every balanced pair of caps, every allowed
common curvature, every actual start, and every same
m>=1536. Its new sign-sensitive pieces are the exact
two-endpoint map, the saturated depth-one base, the

The second mechanism gives one entire-original-word

Its coefficient is below3/5; it does not close all

# Two correlated prefix gradients: exact price and a larger threshold exclusion

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Max4 round42,
the second and final round of the allocation after completed40, with
completed ceiling42 and attempted ceiling43. No formal gate is allocated.
This document is neither a fact nor a solution of the original Goal.

## 1. Result, quantifiers, and limits

Let h=(u,w,z,v) have nonnegative entries and sum at least168/25. The
new regional assertion is that the original full primitive certificate
does not exist whenever

    u>=7/4,       4/3<=w<=3/2,       0<=v<=sqrt(2).       (1)

There is no additional upper or lower restriction on z. If the old first
and third cuts hold, a single static convex1-smooth function, in dimension
at most3, follows all four actual steps and has primitive energy

                         V_h < -5/16.                    (2)

The function may depend on h. No common-function assertion is made.
All twenty finite interpolation rows and all optimum rows are retained.
No ordering, reversal, support, rank, or lossless ansatz reduction is used.

The new geometric mechanism consists of two DISTINCT initial gradients
with the same projection onto a terminal supporting face. Their fresh
components have a freely chosen covariance. Both early projection normals
are retained. This changes the initial state of the round41 core-return
construction; in particular x_0=g_0 is not imposed. The middle query here
has zero normal. This is an explicit alternative to the initially
considered nonzero-middle-normal route, not a claim to have solved that
more general route.

Sections3--5 solve the complete covariance minimization within this
specified family, including both signs of the earliest normal and both
signs of the prefix coefficient. Sections6--9 prove (1)--(2) on the
entire stated region, not only a boundary word.

Appendix A reproduces the COMPLETE previous small-second-step proof
4e70302143741847 as joint proof text. Consequently this joint draft
excludes the union of (1) with

    h>=0, sum h>=168/25, w<=4/3,                         (3)

including every first/third/last step allowed in (3). The new part has
w>4/3 and therefore lies outside that previously excluded slab. No
independence from EVERY older necessary predicate is claimed: no complete
old-conjunction survivor in (1) has been supplied.

Accepted inputs are only04219554e41d9750, Section10, and1252b8823bb5a290,
with their exact primitive identity and interpolation orientation. Every
other ingredient used here is derived in this joint document, including
Appendix A. Previous unverified drafts are not correctness predecessors.
The defective scalar display in43a616da7da451c7 is not used or repaired;
its historical object and hash are preserved. No unchecked global
Bernstein table or global scalar reduction is imported.

This is a scope-complete larger regional exclusion, not a full-cone
decision. No threshold seed, complete expanded-conjunction survivor,
or original all-word exact-profile result is obtained. The precise
remaining bridge is stated in Section10.

## 2. Original full certificate, primitive energy, and cut witnesses

Put s=u+w+z+v and C=1+s. Original finite indices are0,1,2,3,4. Let
Lambda_ij>=0 for every i!=j, Lambda_ii=0, e_all=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, and

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e_all)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2u,-2w,-2z,-2v,2s)^T,
    Dprim=diag(u(2-u),w(2-w),z(2-z),v(2-v),-s(s+2)),
    M=Dprim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.

The requested array must obey L_flow^T e_all=omega and M PSD on ALL of
R^5. In particular formal gradients are not identified at a zero step.
For actual smoothness-one GD jets define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f_4)
        +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_4||^2.

Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives the full identity

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_4)>.

Actual convex1-smooth jets have Q_ij<=0 by1252b8823bb5a290. Thus any
negative actual V_h contradicts every original array.

All constructed values in this proof are saturated values

    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

Direct substitution and telescoping give

    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>,
    V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_4+s g_4||^2.                    (4)

Indeed sum_i[2h_i<x_i,g_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2]
=||x_0||^2-||x_4||^2. The remaining terms are
-2s<x_4,g_4>-s^2||g_4||^2.

For any index j put Pj=sum_(i<j)h_i, Rj=sum_(i>j)h_i. Every original
certificate necessarily satisfies the four scalar cuts

    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+Pj)(1+Rj).                       (5)

Here is a complete witness proof. If h_j>1, let A=1+Pj, B=1+Rj,
r=(h_j-1)/B, and K=[-r,1]. The function
F_K(x)=max_(g in K)(xg-g^2/2), started at A, has gradient1 at every
prefix query through j, the selected step goes from1 to-B r, and
every subsequent gradient is-r. The final point and gradient both
equal-r, including any zero steps. These are projections onto K,
so all rows hold;0 lies in K and the minimum value is0. Formula(4)
gives V_h=A^2-C^2 r^2, proving(5). For h_j<=1 the cut is automatic.

## 3. Entire phase of the new correlated-prefix family

In this section assume only

    1<u<2, w>1, z>1, v>=0,
    a=u-1, b=w-1, t=(z-1)/(1+v), b*t<1.             (6)

Set

    k=u+w, N=k+1, A=k-1=a+w,
    S=(1+t)(1-bt)/b>0,
    L=1-A*t,
    H0=(1+t)*max(L,0),
    Dp=a*u-w*(1-a).

Let e be a unit vector. Let F,G be arbitrary vectors orthogonal to e,
with ||F||^2=S. Write H=<F,G>, R0=||G||^2. Define the actual jets

    g_0=-t e+G,       g_1=-t e+F,       g_2=e,
    g_3=g_4=-t e,

    x_2=e, x_1=e+w*g_1, x_0=e+w*g_1+u*g_0,
    x_3=-(z-1)e=-(1+v)t e, x_4=-t e.              (7)

All four GD equalities in the ORIGINAL order hold. In particular the
last equality remains valid at v=0. Assign the saturated values of(4).
The complete target normals y_j=x_j-g_j are

    y_0=L e+wF+aG,
    y_1=(1-bt)e+bF,
    y_2=0, y_3=-vt e, y_4=0.                     (8)

The COMPLETE finite-row and Euclidean-realizability conditions on H,R0
are precisely

    H<=S,
    a R0 >= wS+(a-w)H,
    a R0+wH >= H0,
    R0 >= H^2/S.                                 (9)

Here is every finite row, grouped by target. Into target0 they are

    Q_10=wS+(a-w)H-aR0,
    Q_20=(1+t)L-wH-aR0,
    Q_30=Q_40=-wH-aR0.

Into target1 they are

    Q_01=b(H-S), Q_21=(1+t)(1-bt)-bS=0,
    Q_31=Q_41=-bS.

All four off-diagonal rows into target2 and all four into target4 vanish.
Into target3, sources0,1,4 give zero and source2 gives-vt(1+t)<=0.
There are exactly twenty rows in this list. The first three conditions
in(9) are therefore necessary and sufficient for all finite rows. The
last one is the exact two-vector Gram condition. Conversely any H,R0
satisfying it is attained in at most two fresh dimensions, by

    F=sqrt(S)e_2,
    G=(H/sqrt(S))e_2+sqrt(R0-H^2/S)e_3,

with e,e_2,e_3 orthonormal. Degenerate equality endpoints are included.

Let K=conv{g_0,g_1,e,-t e}. It contains0. If(9) holds, every normal
in(8) supports K at the indicated gradient. Hence the SINGLE static
function

    F_h(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}             (10)

has exactly all the jets in(7). Completing the square identifies its
unique maximizing gradient with the Euclidean projection P_K(x).
Adding two projection inequalities proves
<x-x',P_K(x)-P_K(x')>>=||P_K(x)-P_K(x')||^2.
Consequently P_K is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing inequalities also give
0<=F_h(x')-F_h(x)-<P_K(x),x'-x>
 <=<P_K(x')-P_K(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.
This proves differentiability with gradient P_K; convexity and finiteness
follow from the compact maximum formula. Since0 is in K, minimum0 is
attained at0. Every Q_(i,star)=0; all Q_(star,i)<=0 because the supporting
inequalities include0. No optimum row is omitted.

## 4. Exact covariance minimization, with every phase retained

For this declared family the energy is

    V_h=(1-kt)^2+w^2 S+u^2 R0+2uwH-C^2 t^2.       (11)

Normalize theta=H/S, r=R0/S, alpha=w/a. The domain(9) becomes

    theta<=1,
    r>=theta^2,
    r>=alpha+(1-alpha)theta,
    r>=H0/(aS)-alpha*theta.                        (12)

One always has0<=H0<=A S. To verify the upper bound: if L<=0 it is
immediate; if L>=0, direct cancellation gives

    A S-H0=u(1+t)/b>0.                            (13)

Thus

    theta_*=(H0/S-w)/a lies in[-alpha,1].           (14)

For theta<=-alpha the bound alpha+(1-alpha)theta is no greater
than theta^2. Both remaining possible costs strictly decrease as theta
increases there: the quadratic cost has derivative
2u^2 theta+2uw<0, and the other affine cost has derivative
uw(a-1)/a<0. Hence no minimizer lies below-alpha.

For-alpha<=theta<=1,

    alpha+(1-alpha)theta-theta^2=(1-theta)(theta+alpha)>=0.

Only the two affine bounds remain. Their intersection is theta_*.
Below theta_* the cost slope is uw(a-1)/a<0. Above it the slope is
u Dp/a. This proves the complete minimizer description:

* If Dp>0, choose theta=theta_* and
  r=alpha+(1-alpha)theta_*.
* If Dp<=0, choose theta=1,r=1. At Dp=0 other minimizers may exist;
  this choice still attains the minimum.

These choices and(12) prove attainment. The exact minimum initial norm
squared is

    J_min=(1-kt)^2+k^2 S
            -(u/a^2)*max(Dp,0)*(A S-H0).           (15)

The minimization is over this ENTIRE covariance family and its fixed
saturated values. It is not asserted to optimize arbitrary gradient
geometries or arbitrary smooth functions.

For Dp>0 an equivalent simpler expression is

    J_min=(1-kt)^2+(w^2/a^2)S+(u Dp/a^2)H0.        (16)

Indeed substitution of theta_* in the cost leaves S coefficient
w^2+u^2 w/a-u Dp*w/a^2=w^2/a^2. Formula(16) contains BOTH signs of L.
In particular no unsupported extrapolation of a normal sign occurs.

The selected Dp>0 geometry has Q_10=Q_21=Q_43=0, and every row into
targets2 and4 is zero. Hence every alternative assignment of finite
values for this same geometry differs from the displayed values by a
common constant. If delta_i is the value perturbation, the zero rows
into2,4 give delta_2=delta_4 and delta_i<=delta_2 for every i. The row
Q_21 gives delta_2<=delta_1, Q_10 gives delta_1<=delta_0, and Q_43
gives delta_4<=delta_3. All five deltas are therefore equal. Thus its
energy cannot be reduced by another legal assignment of finite values.
This rigidity claim concerns the SELECTED geometry, not all of(12).

## 5. Explicit two-phase energy of the selected construction

Continue with Dp>0. Put

    J0=N+1+1/a+w/(a^2 b),
    Kp=N^2-J0,
    G_A=N^2-Kp(1+t)-C^2t^2,
    G_B=(1-kt)^2+(w^2/a^2)S-C^2t^2,
    beta=u Dp/a^2>0.

Then(16) gives the exact energy

    V_h=max(G_A,G_B),
    G_A=G_B+beta(1+t)L.                            (17)

The active expression is G_A when L>=0 and G_B when L<=0. To check
the formula for J0, substitute H0=(1+t)(1-A t) into(16). The t^2
coefficient cancels by

    a^2 k^2-w^2=u Dp A.

Its constant term is

    1+w^2/(a^2b)+u Dp/a^2
      =N+1+1/a+w/(a^2b)=J0.

The resulting affine expression in t is(1+t)J0-tN^2, as also follows
by evaluating at t=-1. This last algebraic evaluation identifies a
polynomial; it does not claim that t=-1 is a legal trajectory.

## 6. Reduction of the entire new region to one rectangle

Assume(1) and C>=C0:=193/25. If the first or third cut(5) fails, its
explicit witness already excludes the full array. Suppose these two
cuts hold. The first cut
gives u<=2C/(C+1)<2. Since w<=3/2 and v<=sqrt(2)<3/2,

    z>C0-1-2-3/2-3/2=43/25>1.

The third cut gives0<t<=N/C<1. Therefore(6) holds, because b<=1/2.
Also throughout the closed bounding rectangle

    7/4<=u<=2, 4/3<=w<=3/2,                      (18)

one has Dp>=15/16>0: Dp increases with u and is nonincreasing in w, and
at u=7/4,w=3/2 its value is15/16. Thus the selected construction in
Sections3--5 applies to every possible old-cut survivor in(1).

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2), with12/5<lambda<17/7. The actual t is
(C-N)/(1+v)-1. Since N<=9/2, C>=C0, and1+v<=lambda,

    t >= (C0-9/2)/lambda-1
       >277/850>3/10.                            (19)

Both expressions in(17) strictly decrease in t on the whole bounding
range C>=C0, (18), t>=3/10. For G_A, observe

    (Kp)_u=2N-1+1/a^2+2w/(a^3b)>0,
    (Kp)_w=2N-1+1/(a^2b^2)>0,
    Kp>=Kp(7/4,4/3)=151/48>0.

Thus (G_A)_t=-Kp-2C^2t<0. Write

    G_B=1+w^2/(a^2b)+B t-E t^2,
    B=-2k+(w^2/a^2)(1/b-1),
    E=C^2-k^2+w^2/a^2.

Here k>3,w/a<=2,1/b-1<=2, so B<2. Also C0^2>59 and k<=7/2,
so E>59-49/4>46. Hence (G_B)_t<2-92*(3/10)<0.
With t held fixed, both expressions have C derivative-2Ct^2<0.

Increase1+v to lambda and decrease C to C0 in these algebraic
expressions, holding u,w fixed. Both changes decrease t, which stays
above3/10. They can only increase max(G_A,G_B). No claim that the
virtual comparison words satisfy all older predicates is needed.
The signs and denominators just used hold on the entire paths.
It remains to bound the expressions on(18) with

    C=C0, t=t0(N):=(C0-N)/lambda-1.                (20)

The closed virtual endpoint u=2 is permissible in the displayed
algebraic functions and their continuous extensions; the actual
certificate survivors already have u<2.

## 7. Separate convexity bounds the first algebraic phase by four corners

In this section G_A always denotes its displayed algebraic expression,
even where L<0; the actual second-phase correction is handled next.
Put Hc=C0-lambda. From(20),

    lambda G_A=(C0-N)J0-(Hc-N)N^2
                         -(C0^2/lambda)(Hc-N)^2.  (21)

For fixed a, N=a+b+3 and
J0=N+d0+e0/b, d0=1+1/a+1/a^2, e0=1/a^2.
Expanding(21) and differentiating twice with respect to w gives

    lambda (G_A)_(ww)
      =6N-2(Hc+1+C0^2/lambda)
                  +2(C0-a-3)/(a^2 b^3).          (22)

For fixed w define

    U=C0-w-2-w/b,
    V=(w/b)(C0-w-2).

The nonpolynomial part of (C0-N)J0 is U/a+V/a^2, up to a constant.
Consequently

    lambda (G_A)_(uu)
      =6N-2(Hc+1+C0^2/lambda)+2U/a^3+6V/a^4.     (23)

Here are explicit signs on the ENTIRE rectangle. We have N>4,
Hc+1<158/25, C0^2/lambda<25, a<=1, b<=1/2, and C0-a-3>=93/25.
Therefore the right side of(22) is greater than

    24-1566/25+1488/25=522/25>0.

Also U>=C0-3/2-2-4=11/50>0, and
V>=(3)(C0-3/2-2)=633/50. The right side of(23) is greater than

    24-1566/25+1899/25>0.

Thus G_A is convex separately in u and w. Applying the interval
convexity bound in u and then in w bounds it above by its largest
value at one of the four corners. This is not a numerical scan.

At (u,w)=(7/4,4/3), N=49/12,Kp=151/48, and t0>49/100 using
lambda<17/7. Hence, with C0^2>59,

    G_A<17-3*(149/100)-59*(49/100)^2
        =-16359/10000<-7/10.

At (7/4,3/2), N=17/4,Kp=295/48, and t0>21/50. Thus

    G_A<181/10-6*(71/50)-59*(21/50)^2
        =-2069/2500<-7/10.

At (2,4/3), N=13/3,Kp=76/9, and t0>39/100. Thus

    G_A<19-8*(139/100)-59*(39/100)^2
        =-10939/10000<-7/10.

For the last corner use the sharper lambda<169/70, which follows
from sqrt(2)<99/70 since9801>9800. At (2,3/2), N=9/2,Kp=43/4,
and t0>282/845. Therefore

    G_A<81/4-(43/4)*(1127/845)
                -(37249/625)*(282/845)^2
        =-323676976/446265625<-7/10.               (24)

For the last comparison,
10*323676976=3236769760>3123859375=7*446265625.
These exact rational inequalities prove G_A<-7/10 throughout(18).

## 8. The entire second phase costs less than31/80

By(17), the actual energy on(20) is

    max(G_A,G_B)=G_A+beta(1+t0)*(-L)_+.           (25)

The coefficient satisfies

    0<beta=u^2/a+w*(1-1/a^2)
       <=u^2/a=a+2+1/a<=49/12<25/6.

For N in[49/12,9/2], the function (N-2)t0(N) is decreasing, because
its derivative is (Hc+2-2N)/lambda<0. Its maximum is therefore at
N=49/12. The bound lambda>41/17 follows from sqrt(2)>24/17,
since578>576. It gives

    t0(49/12)<6247/12300<11/20,
    (25/12)*t0(49/12)-1<343/5904<3/50.

Consequently, throughout the rectangle, including every L<0 point,

    beta(1+t0)(-L)_+
      <(25/6)*(31/20)*(3/50)=31/80.               (26)

Combining(24)--(26) and the reductions in Section6 proves

    V_h<-7/10+31/80=-5/16.

This proves(1)--(2). In particular the earliest normal was never
assumed nonnegative. The comparison paid for its entire negative phase.

The direct proof strategy therefore reaches an actual new complete

# Nested terminal triangles exclude the entire small-second-step region

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Max4 round40, the last
of the two analytical rounds under master_guidance78e668c9782fc528.

## 1. Statement, scope, and accepted inputs

The regional assertion proved in this draft is:

For every four-step word h=(u,w,z,v) with nonnegative entries,

    u+w+z+v >= 168/25,       w <= 4/3,                 (1)

there is no original primitive certificate with all twenty nonnegative
finite multipliers, exact flow, and a matrix positive semidefinite on
the full five-gradient space. The first, third, and last steps have no
additional prescribed intervals. No ordering, reversal, or coordinate
permutation is used.

If the four old cut conditions and the old terminal-triangle condition
hold, the proof supplies one explicit static function depending on h,
of dimension at most three, whose primitive energy is

    V_h < -961/4375.                                  (2)

This removes the previous restriction 6w+7v<=18 from the round39 draft.
It covers its actual complement within w<=4/3, and does not merely fix
the first step or perturb the former physical box.

Only accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, and accepted1252b8823bb5a290
are inputs. The first supplies the exact original-array identity; the
second supplies the interpolation orientation. All functions, every
physical update, all directed rows, and all parameter comparisons
needed below are proved jointly here. Neither unverified8d4369993526363e
nor any earlier unverified triangle assertion is a correctness premise.

Audit8d78e0975df8400a and guidance1501c682ec2aff49 have been read. The
original scalar draft43a616da7da451c7 remains defective as printed; its
original file and hash are preserved. No scalar first-increment bound,
global domain reduction, Bernstein table, broad row-redundancy claim,
or whole-box assertion from that package is used here. This document
does not purport to repair or accept that separate scalar package.

## 2. The full original certificate and a realization device

Put s=u+w+z+v and C=1+s. Original finite indices are0,1,2,3,4.
Let Lambda_ij>=0 for all twenty distinct finite pairs and Lambda_ii=0.
Let e_all=(1,1,1,1,1)^T and define

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e_all)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2u,-2w,-2z,-2v,2s)^T,
    Dprim=diag(u(2-u),w(2-w),z(2-z),v(2-v),-s(s+2)),
    M=Dprim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.

The proposed certificate requires L_flow^T e_all=omega and M PSD on
all of R^5. Original gradients at zero steps are not identified or
deleted. For actual positions X_i, gradients G_i, and values F_i set

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<G_i,X_j-X_i>+||G_i-G_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(F_i-F_4)
         +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||G_i||^2-sC||G_4||^2.

The accepted exact identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(G_0,...,G_4)>.(3)

The inner product of matrices is trace of the transpose times the
second matrix. Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0.
Thus a negative actual V_h contradicts the entire original certificate.

All witnesses here use saturated values

    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                       (4)

Their residuals and primitive energy simplify exactly to

    Q_ij=<G_i-G_j,X_j-G_j>,
    V_h=||X_0||^2-||X_4+sG_4||^2.                  (5)

For the second identity, 2sum h_i<X_i,G_i>-sum h_i^2||G_i||^2
telescopes to ||X_0||^2-||X_4||^2 under the original updates; the
remaining terms are -2s<X_4,G_4>-s^2||G_4||^2.

Here is the static realization used below, including the optimum.
Suppose K is the convex hull of the displayed gradients, contains0,
and every source vertex satisfies <G-G_j,X_j-G_j><=0. Define

    F_K(X)=max_(G in K)(<X,G>-||G||^2/2).             (6)

Completing the square identifies the unique maximizer as the Euclidean
projection P_K(X). The normal inequalities imply P_K(X_j)=G_j, so
the function has precisely(4) at each original query. Two projection
inequalities, added, give

    <X-X',P_K(X)-P_K(X')> >= ||P_K(X)-P_K(X')||^2.

Hence P_K is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing inequalities give

    0<=F_K(X')-F_K(X)-<P_K(X),X'-X>
       <=<P_K(X')-P_K(X),X'-X><=||X'-X||^2.

This proves differentiability and gradient P_K. The function is convex
as a maximum of affine functions, and finite because K is compact.
Since0 is in K, F_K>=0=F_K(0). In particular it has a true minimizer.
No uniqueness or normalized objective payoff is asserted. For(4), all
Q_(i,star) vanish; Q_(star,i)=-<G_i,X_i-G_i> will also be checked.

## 3. The older cuts and the terminal triangle, freshly derived

For j in{0,1,2,3}, let Pj=sum_(i<j)h_i and Rj=sum_(i>j)h_i.
Every full certificate obeys

    C(h_j-1)_+ <= (1+Pj)(1+Rj).                     (7)

When h_j>1, put A=1+Pj,B=1+Rj,r=(h_j-1)/B. The function(6) with
K=[-r,1], started at X_0=A, has gradient1 throughout the prefix up
to j. The selected update moves X_j=1 to -Br. The remaining gradients
are all -r, and X_4=G_4=-r. Every original zero step is included.
Equation(5) gives V_h=A^2-C^2r^2, proving(7). When h_j<=1 the cut
is automatic.

Write

    m=1+w, N=u+m, D=1+v, c=z-1, d=v-1.

Then C=N+D+c. In our threshold region, the endpoint cuts give

    u,v <= 2C/(C+1) < 2.                            (8)

If an endpoint is at most1 its bound is automatic; otherwise(8) is
the rearranged corresponding cut. Consequently

    z>C0-19/3=104/75>1,   C0:=193/25,               (9)

using w<=4/3. Thus c>0 is derived, not imposed as a reduction.

When v>1, the terminal adjacent pair also requires cd<1. If cd>=1,
use the one-dimensional function with derivative min(x,1), namely
F(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1 and F(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1. Its initial point N
gives the exact queries

    X=(N,m,1,-c,cd),       G=(1,1,1,-c,1).

All values are(4), the function has minimum0, and(5) gives

    V_h=N^2-(N+zv)^2<0.                             (10)

Now assume v>1 and cd<1. Define

    E=Dd=v^2-1,
    Ktri=D+c(1-E)=c^2 d+(c+D)(1-cd)>0,
    Rtri=c^2 d/Ktri,   t=c/D,
    e=(1,0), r=(Rtri,sqrt(Rtri(1-Rtri))),
    p=-(c e+r)/v,   n=-c e-p,
    Btri=(cd-Rtri)/v.                               (11)

Here0<Rtri<1 and Btri>0: Ktri-c=D(1-cd)>0 implies cd>Rtri.
Direct inner products give

    <e,r>=||r||^2=Rtri,  <e,p>=-(c+Rtri)/v<0,
    <e,n>=-Btri,   <p,n>=<r,n>=t Btri.               (12)

For the last identity, expand p and n and use Ktri Rtri=c^2 d;
equivalently <p,n>=Rtri(1-cd)/v and
Rtri(1-cd)/(cd-Rtri)=c/D=t.

Take the original base trajectory

    Xbase=(N e,m e,e,-c e,r),
    Gbase=(e,e,e,p,r).                               (13)

All physical updates hold because -c e-vp=r. Its nonzero target
normals are (N-1)e at0, w e at1, and n at3. At0 and1 the e-component
of each other vertex is at most1. At3, the comparisons from e and r
are respectively -(1+t)Btri and0 by(12). The other target normals are
zero. This checks all ordered finite rows. Also
c e+v p+r=0, with positive coefficients, so0 belongs to conv{e,p,r}.
The optimum products are N-1,w,0,t Btri,0, all nonnegative.
Thus(6) is one legal static function realizing(13), and(5) yields

    V_h=N^2-C^2 Rtri.

The older terminal-triangle necessity, on its proved domain, is

    cd<1,       C^2 Rtri<=N^2       when v>1.         (14)

Nothing from an unexamined global domain reduction was used.

## 4. The enlarged nested family and every original row

This section is valid for all u>1,w>=0,z>1,v>1 with cd<1, without
assuming a threshold. Put

    a=u-1, q=a/m>0, t=c/D>0,

and use all triangle quantities from(11). Given rho>=0, form vectors
in the direct sum of two Euclidean planes:

    X_0=(N e,sqrt(rho)e),       G_0=(e,sqrt(rho)e),
    X_1=(m e,-sqrt(rho)a e),    G_1=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),
    X_2=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),      G_2=(e,-sqrt(rho)q e),
    X_3=(-c e,sqrt(rho)cq e),   G_3=(p,-sqrt(rho)q p),
    X_4=(r,-sqrt(rho)q r),      G_4=(r,-sqrt(rho)q r). (15)

All vectors lie in the sum of the two-dimensional subspace
{(y,-sqrt(rho)q y): y in R^2} and the line generated by(e,sqrt(rho)e).
Thus the function and trajectory can be placed in dimension at most3.
The use of direct-sum coordinates is just explicit notation, not a
restriction on the full original certificate.

The first auxiliary update is1-u=-a, the second is-a+wq=-q because
a=mq, the third is-q+zq=cq, and the fourth iscq e+vq p=-q r.
The base updates were checked in(13). Every step retains its original
length. Assign the values(4).

The complete target normals are

    Y_0=((N-1)e,0), Y_1=(w e,-sqrt(rho)wq e),
    Y_2=0, Y_3=(n,-sqrt(rho)q n), Y_4=0.

Here is the exhaustive finite-row list, grouped by target:

* Target0: sources1,2 give0; source3 gives
  (N-1)(<e,p>-1)<0; source4 gives(N-1)(Rtri-1)<0.
* Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q)<=0; source2 gives0;
  sources3,4 respectively give
  w*(1+rho*q^2)(<e,p>-1)<=0 and
  w*(1+rho*q^2)(Rtri-1)<=0.
* Targets2 and4: every source gives0.
* Target3: sources1,2 give-(1+rho*q^2)(1+t)Btri<0;
  source4 gives0; source0 gives precisely

      Q_03=Btri[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-qt)].                (16)

Thus all twenty original finite rows hold if and only if

    0<=rho<=(1+t)/(q(1-qt))       when qt<1,
    arbitrary rho>=0             when qt>=1.         (17)

This includes qt=1 and rho=0. Necessity is the single row(16), since
Btri>0. No other pair is deleted. The optimum products <G_i,Y_i> are

    N-1, w(1+rho*q^2), 0, t Btri(1+rho*q^2), 0.

They are nonnegative; hence every optimum row holds too. Moreover
cG_1+vG_3+G_4=0, so the static function(6) for

    K_nested=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3,G_4}                  (18)

has minimum0 and realizes every sample. It is one fixed function for
this word and this amplitude, not a different function at each query.
It is not asserted to be a common function independent of the word.

Its exact energy for every allowed amplitude is

    V_h(rho)=N^2-C^2 Rtri+rho*(1-C^2 q^2 Rtri).     (19)

This is the complete amplitude test for this explicit family on the
stated domain. At qt<1 it suffices and is necessary to test both
endpoints of(17), since(19) is affine. At qt>=1 it is equivalent to
N^2-C^2 Rtri>=0 and1-C^2 q^2 Rtri>=0. These equivalences concern
this family alone, not all legal functions or all certificates.

For qt<1 choose the upper endpoint in(17), and define

    k=(qN^2-1/q)/(1+q)
      =u*(N-m/(u-1))=u^2+m*u-m-m/(u-1).

Expanding(19) after multiplication by1-qt gives

    V_h=(1+q)/(1-qt) * Gtri,
    Gtri=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2 Rtri.                      (20)

Indeed the coefficient of C^2 Rtri becomes-(1+q), and the remaining
numerator is (1+q)N^2-(1+t)(qN^2-1/q).

## 5. The planar member needed below v=sqrt(2)

For u>1,w>=0,z>1,v>=0, put q=(u-1)/m,t=c/D and use scalar base data

    Xbase=(N,m,1,-c,-t), Gbase=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    Xaux=(1,-a,-q,cq,tq), Gaux=(1,-q,-q,tq,tq),
    X_i=(Xbase_i,sqrt(rho)Xaux_i),
    G_i=(Gbase_i,sqrt(rho)Gaux_i).

The four updates are N-u=m,m-w=1,1-z=-c,-c+vt=-t, and
1-u=-a,-a+wq=-q,-q+zq=cq,cq-vtq=tq. Use(4).
The base normals are(N-1,w,0,-vt,0), and the auxiliary normals are
(0,-wq,0,vtq,0). The complete finite rows are:

* Target0: sources1,2 give0 and sources3,4 give-(1+t)(N-1).
* Target1: source0 gives-rho*w*q*(1+q); source2 gives0;
  sources3,4 give-w*(1+t)(1+rho*q^2).
* Targets2,4: all sources give0.
* Target3: sources1,2 give-vt*(1+t)(1+rho*q^2); source4 gives0;
  source0 gives vt[-(1+t)+rho*q*(1-tq)].

All optimum products are N-1,w(1+rho*q^2),0,
vt^2(1+rho*q^2),0. Also G_4=-tG_1, so0 is in the hull of
G_0,G_1,G_4. The function(6) realizes these data.

The full allowed amplitude is(17) when v>0 and tq<1, and arbitrary
rho>=0 when v=0 or tq>=1. In particular, whenever0<tq<1 the capped
rho=(1+t)/(q(1-tq)) is legal even at v=0. At that amplitude(5) gives

    V_h=(1+q)/(1-tq) * Gcut,
    Gcut=N^2-k*(1+t)-C^2 t^2.                       (21)

This is rederived here; no fixed trajectory is extrapolated across
an unchecked phase.

## 6. Two elementary tail formulas

Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), so lambda(lambda-2)=1, and let L=C-N denote
the sum c+D in this section only. At fixed D and c=L-D, write

    R(L,D)=c^2(D-2)/[D+c(1-D(D-2))].                (22)

For D>=lambda and cd<1 its denominator is positive. With
E=D(D-2)>=1 and chi=1-(E-1)t in(0,1], equation(22) reads

    R=t^2 E/chi,
    R_c=E*t*(1+chi)/(D*chi^2) >= 2t/D >0.           (23)

For the inequality, (1+chi)/chi^2>=2 follows from
1+chi-2chi^2=(1-chi)(1+2chi)>=0. In the planar case Rcut=t^2,
the corresponding derivative is exactly2t/D.

For any fixed0<rbar<1, the inequality R(L,D)>rbar is equivalent,
on a positive denominator, to

    F_rbar(D):=(D-2)(L-D)(L-(1-rbar)D)-rbar*L >0.   (24)

If L>=18/5 and lambda<=D<=14/5, its second derivative satisfies

    F_rbar''=6(1-rbar)D-2L(2-rbar)-4(1-rbar)
        <=[64(1-rbar)-36(2-rbar)]/5
         =-(8+28rbar)/5<0.                         (25)

Therefore positive endpoint values imply positivity throughout the
closed interval, by concavity. This avoids assuming R is monotone
in D; it need not be. At D=lambda, its exact value is

    R(L,lambda)=(L-lambda)^2/lambda^2.               (26)

## 7. Every possible old survivor in(1) enters the proved phase

Assume all cuts(7) and the terminal condition(14) hold. First v<9/5.
Indeed if v>=9/5, the last cut implies C>=v/(2-v)>=9. By(8),
N<13/3 and D<3, so c=C-N-D>5/3 and d>=4/5. Thus cd>4/3,
contradicting(14). This used no capacity upper bound from elsewhere.

Next N>4, so u=N-m>5/3. If v<=sqrt(2), the third cut gives

    N>=C(C-D)/(C+D).

For C>=C0,D<=lambda<17/7, this is greater than
C0(C0-17/7)/(C0+17/7)=89359/22200>4. The monotonicities follow from
partial_C[C(C-D)/(C+D)]=1-2D^2/(C+D)^2>0 for C>D, and
partial_D=-2C^2/(C+D)^2<0.

For sqrt(2)<=v<9/5, suppose instead N<=4. Then L=C-N>=Lstar:=93/25.
Use(22) at the virtual Lstar. Its denominator stays positive because

    (Lstar-D)(D-2) <= (Lstar-2)^2/4
                  =1849/2500<1.

The monotonicity R_c>0 in(23), with the actual cd<1, gives
R(L,D)>=R(Lstar,D). Take rstar=16/C0^2, which lies in(0,1/3).
At D=lambda, using lambda<17/7,

    C0*(Lstar/lambda-1)
       >C0*(Lstar/(17/7)-1)=43618/10625>4.

Thus R(Lstar,lambda)>rstar. At D=14/5, direct substitution gives

    R(Lstar,14/5)=2116/5185>1/3>rstar.

Equations(24),(25) with L=Lstar show R(Lstar,D)>rstar for the whole
interval. Consequently C^2 R(L,D)>16>=N^2, contradicting(14).
This proves N>4 also on the higher-terminal branch.

We have therefore derived, for every old survivor in(1),

    5/3<u<2, 1<=m<=7/3, c>0,
    q=(u-1)/m in(0,1), 0<t<=N/C<1.                (27)

The last inequality is the third cut. Thus qt<1. All zero and small
step cases were handled before choosing a family; none was discarded.
For v<=sqrt(2) use the planar function in Section5. For v>=sqrt(2)
use the nested triangle in Section4, whose cd<1 was proved above.

## 8. Reduce the whole region to fixed first step and prefix mass

On the bounding rectangle5/3<u<2,1<=m<=7/3, the coefficient k obeys

    k_m=u-1-1/(u-1)<0,
    k_u=2u+m+m/(u-1)^2>0,
    k>=k(5/3,7/3)=5/6>0.                           (28)

Both energies have the form

    G=N^2-k*(C-N)/D-C^2 R,

where R=t^2 in the planar case and R is(22) in the triangle case.
Where c>0 and the triangle denominator, if present, is positive,

    G_C=-k/D-2C R-C^2 R_c<0,
    G_m=2N-k_m*(1+t)+k/D+C^2 R_c>0.                (29)

For the planar case additionally

    G_D=(k+2C^2 t)*(C-N)/D^2>0.                    (30)

In the planar branch increase D to lambda using(30). In either branch
increase m to7/3 and decrease C to

    Cmin(u)=max(C0,u/(2-u)),

which is permitted by the original first cut. These comparisons only
increase G. They need not preserve every other old predicate, since
they are comparisons of the explicit expressions, not new candidate
certificates. Their necessary sign conditions do hold: c stays positive,
since C0-2-7/3-14/5=44/75>0; k stays positive by(28). In the triangle
case m increasing and C decreasing only decrease c and hence preserve
cd<1 and positivity of the denominator.

We are left with m=7/3 and lambda<=D<=14/5; the planar case has just
D=lambda. For each such D the maximum over u is at

    U0=2C0/(C0+1)=193/109.                          (31)

To prove this, first take5/3<u<=U0. Here Cmin=C0, N>4, U0<9/5, and

    k_u<18/5+7/3+21/4=671/60<45/4,
    7/25<t<3/5,
    C0^2>59.

For the lower t bound, c>59/75 gives t>59/210>7/25. For the upper
bound use N>4 and lambda>12/5, giving t<(93/25)/(12/5)-1=11/20.
By(23) or its planar equality,

    G_u=2N-k_u*(1+t)+k/D+C0^2 R_c
        >8-18+59/5=9/5>0.                          (32)

Next take U0<=u<2 with Cmin=C_u=u/(2-u). Since U0>7/4,
C_u'=2/(2-u)^2>32. Let L0=C0-U0-7/3. It exceeds18/5. Along this
branch c=C_u-u-7/3-D increases with u. Therefore

    t>2/7, R_c>=2t/D>10/49, C_u^2 R_c>10.

The total derivative is

    2N-k_u*(1+t)-k*(C_u'-1)/D
        -2C_u C_u'R-C_u^2 R_c*(C_u'-1)
      <26/3-310<0.                                 (33)

This proves(31). All comparison paths in u toward U0 decrease c:
below U0, increase u at fixed C0; above U0, decrease u with C=C_u.
Thus a triangle denominator that was positive remains positive. No
unproved phase or physical reversal is used in the reduction.

## 9. Uniform terminal comparison at the remaining corner

At u=U0,m=7/3,C=C0 the exact constants are

    N0=1342/327<411/100,
    L0=C0-N0=29561/8175,
    723/200<L0<11/3, in particular L0>18/5,
    k0=815039/427716>19/10.                          (34)

For example N0-m/(U0-1)=1342/327-109/36=4223/3924,
which gives the displayed k0 after multiplication by193/109.

We claim, uniformly for lambda<=D<=14/5, that

    C0^2 R(L0,D) > (383/100)^2.                     (35)

Its denominator is positive throughout, since
(L0-D)(D-2)<(11/3-2)^2/4=25/36<1. Put
rbar=(383/772)^2; then0<rbar<1/4 and C0=772/100.
At D=lambda, use sqrt(2)<99/70, hence lambda<169/70, and(34):

    C0*(L0/lambda-1)
      >(193/25)*[(723/200)/(169/70)-1]
       =324433/84500 > 383/100.

At D=14/5, c=L0-D>4/5 and

    Ktri=14/5-(31/25)c<226/125,
    c^2 d>64/125,
    R(L0,14/5)>32/113>1/4>rbar.

Thus(24),(25) prove(35) over the entire interval. The planar energy
at D=lambda has exactly the same R by(26). Combining(29)--(35)
now gives for either branch

    G < (411/100)^2
         -(19/10)*(18/5)/(14/5)
         -(383/100)^2
      = 2779/1250-171/70
      = -961/4375.                                  (36)

Every quantity bounded below in the subtracted products is positive.
The multiplier in(20) or(21) is(1+q)/(1-qt)>1 at the original word.
Hence its actual static witness satisfies(2).

## 10. Conclusion, exact comparison scope, and remaining task

If any old cut fails, Section3 gives an actual scalar V_h<0. If the
terminal condition fails at v>1, its scalar or planar function in
Section3 gives V_h<0. Otherwise Sections7--9 apply the explicitly
legal nested family and give the strict uniform margin(2). In every
case(3) excludes all original twenty-sign, exact-flow, full-R5 arrays.
This proves the regional assertion(1) as a joint unverified draft.

The explicit old inventory N_pub, including every N1--N8 predicate on
its conditional domain, is retained. This exclusion needs only its
four cuts and terminal adjacent triangle; no omitted old predicate is
asserted redundant. S_scalar remains the entire specified proportional-
gradient polynomial geometry with all legal values, not all nonlinear
one-dimensional trajectories. No smaller list is substituted for it.

More precisely, the conjunction of the full old inventory, S_scalar,
and the full planar-plus-terminal-triangle nested families just derived
is empty in(1). This is a correctly scoped enlargement of the nested
family. It is not a claim that the previously published planar test
alone already excludes every new point here. No independent-test
separation from the complete previous conjunction is asserted: that
would require an actual survivor of that previous conjunction. Nor is
one inferred from a virtual parameter used in the comparisons.

This complete regional exclusion is the output. The only surviving
threshold possibility left by it has second physical step w>4/3, still
subject to every old predicate and original-array requirement. There
is no justification for swapping w and z or reversing the word.

No exact h,Lambda,M seed, global four-step impossibility, negative
objective error, or original asymptotic profile theorem has been
established. The sufficient lift04219554e41d9750 remains conditional.
The original Goal remains OPEN. Formal verification is not authorized
in this allocation; all new assertions here remain UNVERIFIED.

Resource use: zero numerical evaluations, solver calls, machine-algebra
